# The system and auth APIs

Reference for `/api/system/v1` and `/api/auth/v1`, served by `occulited` behind lighttpd. Not
normative together with its fixtures (the metadata API is, see [meta-api.md](meta-api.md)); this is what
exists, kept in step with the code. JSON in and out, errors as `{"error", "message"}`. Behind lighttpd every `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`
needs a `Content-Length` (send `{}` when there is nothing to say) — `mod_proxy` answers `411` otherwise. Every
route needs a credential (session or API token, see meta-api.md → Conventions) unless marked
*open*, and the scope its row names (the next section). All system
commands are argument slices, never shell strings; with `--root` pointing anywhere but `/` they
are logged instead of executed.

**Machine-readable** (openccu-lite task 298): [openapi.json](openapi.json) is the OpenAPI 3.1 document of every route
here, in meta-api.md and lite-rpc's — summary, scope, parameters, request and answer schemas — generated from the route
table and the handlers' source, checked by CI and attached to each release. The running system serves it as
**`GET /api/openapi.json`** (**`system:read`**; `info.version` is the running occulited's). This prose stays the
reference for what a route does. The event streams (the SSE routes and lite-rpc's WebSocket) and their messages are in
[asyncapi.json](asyncapi.json) (AsyncAPI 3.0), lite-rpc's methods and the scope each needs in
[lite-rpc-methods.json](lite-rpc-methods.json); all three are generated together (`OCCULITED_UPDATE_DOCS=1 go test
./internal/httpapi -run TestAPIDocuments`).

## Scopes

Every route needs a credential unless marked *(open)*, and **every route names the scope it needs**
in bold after its name — one scope per route, `GET /led/state` the one exception with two (either
reaches it). The middleware looks the route up in occulited's table (`internal/httpapi/scopes.go`;
a test walks it) and answers `403 {"error": "forbidden", "scope": "<name>", "message": …}` when
the session or token lacks it — the `scope` field names what is missing, so a program can tell its
operator which scope its token needs. A route without a scope in the table is reached by nobody.
The names are frozen with the API at 1.0.

| Scope | Covers |
| --- | --- |
| `meta:read` | the metadata API's reads: the snapshot, objects, enums, the export, the event stream. **The local token's only scope.** |
| `meta:write` | the metadata mutations and the imports; includes `meta:read` |
| `system:read` | every system read that is not the journal: status, health, radio, services, timers, network, firewall, remote access, time, storage, warnings, the installed addons, the catalogue, the navigation, the certificate and HTTPS settings (no secrets), the firmware information, the LED configuration |
| `logs:read` | the journal: `/log`, its stream and download, the boots, the boot timeline, the kernel log, the run logs — split from `system:read` because the journal is sensitive |
| `system:write` | the system configuration: network, firewall, remote access (classic RPC), time, SSH, certificate, HTTPS and HSTS, timers, starting and stopping services, log levels, the journal settings, the LED configuration, the warning silences, the device firmware, the disk cleanup; includes `system:read` and `led` |
| `addons:write` | addon management: install, update, uninstall, the policies, the catalogue refresh, addon start and stop, the per-addon legacy session switch, the ownership repair; includes `system:read` |
| `power` | reboot, halt, the system update, the restore, the radio module's firmware (the list included) |
| `backup` | creating and downloading backups (the jobs later) |
| `led` | the status LED's state, an override of the token's own, locate — the role `led` |
| `radio:keys` | the HmIP device keys: the list, adding and removing a key, applying them, and the key sheet — the one answer with every key in clear. No other scope includes it (`system:write` does not); Full access does. The token page says it *reads every HmIP device key in clear*. |
| `auth:admin` | accounts, tokens, every session, the security and login settings (the identity provider, the password-login switch, the global legacy session switch); includes `self` |
| `rpc:read`, `rpc:operate`, `rpc:configure`, `rpc:admin` | lite-rpc (`/api/rpc/v1`, below): every route needs `rpc:read`, and each call the tier its method needs; each tier includes the ones before it. The table per method: [lite-rpc-methods.json](lite-rpc-methods.json). |
| `addon:<id>` | **an addon's pages through the system** (openccu-lite task 307, GitHub issue #3): lighttpd's session gate in front of `/addons/<id>/` - and of the segment the addon's drop-in proxies, `/addons/red/` for `redmatic` - accepts a token with this scope as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, on every request; nothing else, and no API route needs it. One scope per installed addon (`GET /tokens` lists them as `addons`); `422 invalid-scope` for an addon that is not installed. Only Full access includes it: `addons:write` manages addons, it opens no page. An addon's own token never gets one. See *The gate and API tokens* below. |
| `*` | **Full access**: every scope that exists and every one added later, the `rpc:*` tiers included (a Full-access token has `rpc:admin`, and opens every addon's pages at the gate). Offered when a token is created, with the hint that explicit scopes are safer for a program. |
| `self` | the caller's own account and session: `POST /logout`, `POST /password` (one's own), `GET`/`DELETE /sessions`, `DELETE /sessions/{sid}` (one's own), `/me/preferences`, `/me/webauthn` (one's own security keys, task 262), `POST /legacy-sid`, `POST /ticket`. Every account session has it; a token only through `auth:admin`. It cannot be given to a token. |

**Accounts stand on the ladder**: `level` is one of `read`, `operate`, `configure`,
`administer`, and a session's scopes follow it — read: `logs:read`, `meta:read`, `system:read`, `rpc:read`;
operate: the same with `rpc:operate` (may switch and set values); configure: `meta:write` (names, rooms,
functions, favorites) and `rpc:configure`; administer: Full access — plus `self` for every account. `role`
is **derived** (`administer` → `admin`, else `user`) and answered for one release, so older clients keep
working; `users.json` carries `level` (an older file's `admin`/`user` load as administer/operate and are
rewritten at the next save, the role beside the level). The last administer account cannot be moved down
or deleted. **Tokens carry scopes**
(`users.json` stores `scopes` instead of `role`; an older file loads with `admin` → `*`, `user` →
the three reads, `led` → `led`, and is rewritten at the next save) and may carry an expiry and
allowed address ranges (checked against the address lighttpd forwards, so a program on the
system itself is never held to them). `GET /api/auth/v1/state` answers `scopes` for every caller and
`role`, `level` and `account_id` for an account. **The local token** `<state>/local-token` holds `meta:read` alone. **An
addon's own token** comes from its manifest's `runtime.api_scopes` (occulited's
`docs/manifest-format.md`): minted at every start, after an install and after a policy change,
written to `/run/occulite/addon-tokens/<id>.api` (`0600`, the addon's user) beside its control
token, in memory only, gone with the addon; never `*`, `auth:admin`, `power`, `backup` or `radio:keys` (a
declaration naming one is logged and the scope left out). The Addons page shows each addon's
scopes. **The install token** (openccu-lite B-274) is `/run/occulite/install-token` (`0600`, root), minted at
every start of occulited on the systemd product, in memory only: the credential of `POST /addons/install/local` and of
nothing else. **The console's token** (occulited task 22) is `/run/occulite/console-token` (`0600`, root), minted at every
start of occulited on the systemd product, in memory only, the session `token:console`: `system:read`, `power` and
`backup`, accepted from the loopback alone (behind lighttpd a request carries the browser's address) - the credential
of `occulited update`, which calls the routes below as the Updates page does (occulited's `docs/config.md`).

## The journal: who changed what

**Every change of the system names its caller** in the journal at the default level (task 269, after the login lines
of `POST /login` below): the middleware puts the session's `user` and the address lighttpd forwards (`remote`) into
the request, and a handler's Info line of a change carries both — `trust: certificate added … user=admin
remote=192.0.2.10`. A change whose handler writes no such line (the line that says what changed comes from deeper
down, or there is none) gets one from the middleware after the answer: `api: change` (Info) with `method`, `route`
(the route's pattern, e.g. `/api/system/v1/services/{id}/{action}`), `status`, `user` and `remote`. **A refused
attempt is Info too**: a change answered `403` — by the handler, or by the scope check before it runs — is
`api: change refused` with the same fields (`user` is `token:<name>` for an API token). Other refusals (`400`,
`404`, `409`, `413`, … : nothing changed) are Debug; a failed change (`5xx`) stays Info. A change is every `POST`, `PUT`,
`PATCH` and `DELETE` of a scope that changes the system (`system:write`, `addons:write`, `auth:admin`, `backup`,
`power`, `radio:keys`, `self`); **not** in Info are the RPC proxy (`/api/rpc/v1/json/…` and `/xmlrpc/…`: `setValue`
and every other interface call, at the rate of the frontends and automations), the metadata (`meta:write`), the
status LED's live state (`led`: overrides and locate), the `POST`s of `system:read` (previews and searches), the LED
page's preview, the browser's boot timings, the own UI preferences and the session hand-overs (`POST /ticket`,
`POST /legacy-sid`).

## `/api/auth/v1`

**Session ids** are 26 characters of base32 (`A–Z`, `2–7`, 130 bits, `crypto/rand.Text`); the
API takes one as either session cookie, as `Authorization: Bearer <id>` or as `?sid=<id>`, with or without the CCU's `@`
wrapping.

**Two cookies per scheme** (openccu-lite task 259, D-78): a login sets the **session cookie** `occulite_session` (over
HTTPS `__Secure-occulite_session`, Secure) with **`Path=/api`** - it reaches the API and nothing else - and the **gate
cookie** `occulite_gate` (`__Secure-occulite_gate`) with the same id and **`Path=/addons/`**, which opens the addon
pages at lighttpd's gate and at occulited's own guard of the addon CGIs and is **no credential on the API** (401). So
no request to an addon's page, CGI or proxy carries the API's cookie; what an addon's server side learns of the
session is the gate's `X-Occulite-Session` header, as before. All cookies are `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, 30 days. A
browser from before the change holds the session's name at `Path=/`; `GET /state`, which the shell calls on every
load, deletes that cookie and sets the two new ones, as a login does.

**The gate and API tokens** (openccu-lite task 307, GitHub issue #3): the gate in front of `/addons/` also accepts
an **API token as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`** - on every request, never from a URL or a cookie, and never
exchanged for a session - when the token holds the **scope `addon:<id>`** of the addon behind the path (its id, or
the segment its lighttpd drop-in proxies), or Full access. A Bearer with a token's shape is final: no cookie is
looked at after it. **401** for a token the system does not know or an expired one; **403** `forbidden` (and a
journal line naming the token and the addon) for a token without the addon's scope - its other scopes count for
nothing at the gate - or one sent from outside its `ips`. occulited's own guard of the addon CGIs and static files
takes the same token for the same path (and, as before, any token with `system:read`). **The identity headers** a
request the gate accepted carries to the addon: `X-Occulite-Session` holds the credential the gate accepted - the
session id or alias as before, **the token itself** for a Bearer, so an addon's check against `GET /api/auth/v1/state`
keeps working (it answers `authenticated: true, user: "token:<name>", scopes` and no `role`, `level` or `sid`);
**`X-Occulite-Auth`** says what it was, `session` or `token`; **`X-Occulite-Token`** names the token (a Bearer
only). A client-sent copy of any of the three is removed first, in any spelling (`X_Occulite_Auth`), everywhere
lighttpd runs. The gate reads the tokens from a mirror beside the sessions' - `/run/occulite/gate-tokens/<sha256 of
the secret>`, written by the daemon on every token and addon change, holding the token's name, the segments it
opens, its expiry and its ranges, never a secret. `last_used` counts the API and the CGIs, not a request lighttpd
proxies straight to an addon's own server.

**Passkeys** (openccu-lite task 262, ASVS V6.5; occulited task 14): an account may register WebAuthn credentials on
its own account (System → Account, *Passkeys*; the routes `/me/webauthn` below). **A passkey is a login of its own,
never a second factor**: it signs in without name and password, and the password login is not touched by an
account's keys — `POST /login` opens the session with the password alone, as it does for an account without keys
(until task 14 an account with a key got `{second_factor: "webauthn", …}` there and finished with
`POST /login/webauthn`; both are gone). The passkey sign-in: `POST /login/passkey` → `{login, options}` (a
discoverable credential, user verification `required`), `POST /login/passkey/finish {login, response}` (the
credential as `PublicKeyCredential.toJSON()`) → the session, method `passkey`, for the account the credential's user
handle names. **A registration makes a passkey or nothing:** resident key and user verification are both
`required`; a credential the browser reports as not discoverable (`credProps.rk: false`) is refused with
`422 webauthn-not-passkey`, one without the user-verified flag with `422 webauthn-refused`; a browser that does not
report `credProps` made the resident credential it was asked for. **Keys registered before task 14** stay in
`users.json` as they are: one made as a discoverable credential with user verification is a passkey; one that is not
(a U2F-style key, a key made without a PIN) is listed with `passkey: false` — *cannot be used to sign in* on the
Account page, which offers to remove it and add a passkey — and signs nobody in. A key whose registration did not
report its residency becomes a passkey at its first discoverable sign-in. A ceremony's state lives 2 minutes, bound
to the client's address, and is spent by its second half whatever the outcome; a refused passkey counts towards the
lockout like a wrong password (`401 invalid-credentials`, `429 locked-out`). **The relying party is the system's
full name** (`<host>.<domain>`): a key is bound to the host name the page was opened on, so a ceremony on an IP
address or a bare host name answers `409 webauthn-needs-name`, and a registration on another name than the system's
full name (where the system knows it) `409 webauthn-wrong-name` — both carry the name to use in `name`. A rename of
host or domain orphans the keys (the Network page warns). The browser needs a secure context and, in Chrome, a
certificate it trusts. Adding and removing one's own key needs the **confirmed ticket** of task 154 for the path
`/api/auth/v1/me/webauthn` (`X-Occulite-Confirm`); an administrator removes another account's keys with
`auth:admin`, which ends that account's sessions like a password reset. **The console** is the way back in, as root:
`occulited admin list` and `occulited admin reset-auth <user>` (the account's passkeys removed, a one-time password
set with `must_change_password`, its sessions ended; logged, and the Status page's notice `console-reset`;
[config.md](config.md) → *The console's account commands*), and `occulited webauthn list <user>`,
`occulited webauthn remove <user> <id>|--all` for single keys. An account has at most 10 keys. `users.json` holds
per key the library's record (id, public key, sign count, flags, AAGUID, transports, `extensions.rk`), a name,
created and last used; a sign count that goes backwards refuses the login and is logged (`the key's signature
counter went backwards`). Passkey sign-in follows the password-login switch (`auth.oidc.password_login`): off, no
local sign-in of any kind. OIDC logins, API tokens and pairing tickets are not touched.

**The header credential** (task 259): a **state-changing** call (anything but GET, HEAD, OPTIONS) whose only credential
is a cookie must carry **`X-Occulite-Request`** with any value, else **`403 request-header`**. A form post or a link
from another site cannot set a custom header, which closes the top-level POST that `SameSite=Lax` lets through; the
shell sends it on every call. A call with an `Authorization` header (a Bearer session, a token), a token as the
basic-auth password or `?sid=` needs none, nor does a read, nor an open route (`/login`, `/setup`, `/ticket/redeem`),
nor a program on a system with authentication off, which sends no cookie. `curl` with the cookie: add
`-H 'X-Occulite-Request: 1'`, or send the id as `Authorization: Bearer`. **The legacy alias** — the CCU's ten-character `?sid=@xxxxxxxxxx@`, which the addon CGIs parse — is a
second credential a session gets on request (`POST /legacy-sid`), and **the API never accepts it**: not as a cookie,
not as Bearer, not as `?sid=`, from nowhere. Only lighttpd's gate and the tclrega shim take it, for `/addons/` alone,
and occulited's own guard of the addon CGIs by the gate's rule (from `?sid=`, never from a cookie). The shell puts
the alias — never the session — into the URLs of the addons the system marks `legacy_session` (`GET /nav`,
`GET /addons`). Sessions and aliases live in the session store by hash only; a session store from before
(version 1) is dropped at the first start, so everyone logs in once. **One-time tickets** (`POST /ticket`)
stand in for the session on a download URL and hand a session over to a host without the cookie.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /state` *(open)* | `{setup_required, authenticated, user?, role?, level?, account_id?, must_change_password?, sid?, method?, auth_off?, public?}` — `public: true` is the Control app's public mode: the caller has no session and is the public principal, `user` the account, `level` read or operate, no `sid`. `sid` is the caller's own session id — the shell's Bearer on a host without the cookie, never part of a URL; absent for tokens, as is `method` — how the session was opened, `password` or `oidc` (the Users page's Authentication section lets the password-login switch be turned off from an `oidc` session only). `must_change_password` is a password's flag: `false` for a session the provider opened. A session cookie that names no live session is deleted here and on a login over HTTPS — both names over HTTPS, the plain one over HTTP — so a Secure `occulite_session` from a login before the fix stops blocking HTTP logins once the browser visits the HTTPS address. A session the cookie carried gets its two cookies set again here (task 259; and the pre-259 cookie at `Path=/` deleted); a Bearer session gets no cookie. |
| `POST /legacy-sid` **`self`** | Any session (a user's self-service route): `{legacy_sid}` — the caller's session's `?sid=@..@` alias, made on the first ask and answered again afterwards. `GET /state` carries it as `legacy_sid` once this process knows one; a restart forgets the alias itself, and the next ask replaces it (the old one keeps working at the gate until then). The alias ends with its session — logout, expiry, a password change. `409 no-session` for a token. The shell asks only when it opens an addon marked `legacy_session`. |
| `POST /ticket` **`self`** | Any session: `{path}` → `{ticket, expires_in}` — a one-time credential for one `GET` of that API path, redeemed as `?ticket=<ticket>` on exactly that path (spent by the redemption, on any path; 60 s; memory only, a restart voids it). The Backup and Log downloads fetch one on the click, so no download URL carries the session. `path: "session"` makes a session hand-over ticket for `POST /ticket/redeem`, which lives **90 s** — as long as the network change's confirm window, since it is fetched right before the change; `expires_in` says which. `422` for a path that is not an API path (or `"session"`), `409 no-session` for a token. |
| `POST /ticket` with `confirm` **`self`** | `{path, confirm: true, password}` → `{ticket, expires_in}` — a **confirmed ticket** for a route that asks again even in a valid session (the key sheet, `GET /api/system/v1/radio/hmip/device-keys/export`, sent back in `X-Occulite-Confirm`). Single use, 60 s, bound to this session and path; never a download or hand-over ticket. It needs the account's password: a wrong one is `401 invalid-credentials` and counts like a failed login (the lockout). An account without a password, or any account while password login is off, gets `409 confirm-oidc` with the `url` to confirm at the provider; with neither, `409 no-confirmation`. With the login off (auth mode `off`) it is issued without a password. |
| `GET /confirm?path=&return=` **`self`** | How this session confirms: `{method: "password" \| "oidc" \| "none" \| ""}`, with `url` for `oidc` (`""`: no password and no provider). A token is not asked (`409`). |
| `GET /oidc/confirm?path=&return=` **`self`** | Sends the browser to the provider with `prompt=login` and `max_age=0`. The callback issues the confirmed ticket only for the session that set out, when the provider signed in **the same account** and the ID token's `auth_time` is after the round trip's start (30 s of clock skew allowed), and returns to `return` with `#confirm=<ticket>` — the fragment, which no server and no log sees — or `#confirm-error=<why>`. It opens no session. |
| `POST /ticket/redeem` *(open)* | `{ticket}` (one made with `path: "session"`) → `{sid, user, role, must_change_password}` + the two cookies of the request's scheme (see above): **a session of its own** for the ticket's account, on the same login method — the store holds no id it could hand over. The network change's confirm link carries such a ticket to the new address (`/network?confirm=…&ticket=…`); the shell exchanges it before its first request (`main.ts`) and drops it from the URL. `401 invalid-ticket` when unknown, spent or run out. |
| `POST /setup` *(open, first boot only)* | `{username, password}` creates the first administrator and logs in. `409` once a user exists. |
| `POST /login` *(open)* | `{username, password}` → `{sid, user, role, level, account_id, must_change_password}` + the two cookies of the request's scheme (see above), whatever passkeys the account has (no second factor, occulited task 14): `occulite_session` at `/api` and `occulite_gate` at `/addons/` over HTTP, `__Secure-occulite_session` and `__Secure-occulite_gate` (Secure) over HTTPS. Neither scheme's touch the other's, so a login over one scheme never locks the other out; both names are accepted on either scheme, and of several credentials the first live one counts. Lockout after repeated failures per user and per remote (`429`). An account without a password fails like a wrong password (`401`). **`403 password_login_disabled`** for every account while the switch `auth.oidc.password_login` is off ([config.md](config.md) → *External login*); the switch is read from the file again whenever it changed, so the console's `occulited auth password-login on` works without a restart. `POST /setup` is never affected. **The journal** (B-231), at the default level: `auth: login` (Info: `user`, `role`, `method` `password`/`oidc`/`ticket`, `remote`) for every session opened; `auth: login refused` (Warn: the name as given — non-printables replaced, at most 64 characters —, `method`, also `token` for a refused API token, `remote`, `reason` such as `unknown user`, `wrong password`, `no password`, `password login off`) and `auth: login locked after repeated failures` (Warn, once when a lockout starts: `remote`, `user` when the name is locked, `until`). Never a password or a token. Refusals while a lockout holds are Debug, and beyond 30 Warn lines of refused logins a minute the rest of that minute is Debug too (announced once). |
| `POST /logout` **`self`** | Ends every session the request carries (both session cookies, bearer, `?sid=`) and sends deletions for all four cookies - the session ones at `/api` and at `/` (their place before task 259), the gate ones at `/addons/`. Over HTTP a browser ignores the deletion of the Secure ones, so a session from an HTTPS login in the same browser stays. Carries the header credential when it rides on the cookie (the shell does). |
| `POST /password` **`self`** | `{current, password}` for oneself; `{user, password}` with `auth:admin` resets another user (and sets `must_change_password`). `409 no-password` for one's own change on an account that has no password (an administrator's reset gives it one); `403 password_login_disabled` while the switch is off. |
| `GET /sessions[?all=true]` **`self`** | Own sessions, or all with `all=true` (`auth:admin`): `{sessions: [{id, user, role, created, last_seen, remote?, agent?, method}], current}`. **`id` is the session's handle, not its id**: the first six hex digits of the SHA-256 of the id and `……`, e.g. `3f9a0c……`; `current` is the caller's own handle (empty for a token). occulited stores sessions by hash only, so no route can hand out an id, and sessions survive a restart of occulited and a reboot ([security.md](https://github.com/hobbyquaker/openccu-lite/blob/main/docs/security.md), [config.md](config.md) → *The sessions*). `method` is `password` or `oidc` (`anonymous` for auth-off's session). `DELETE /sessions` signs out everywhere else; `DELETE /sessions/{handle}` ends the session with exactly that handle (`……` optional) — one of the caller's own, any for an administrator, never the caller's current one — and answers `{ended}`; a prefix, an id or anything that is not a handle ends nothing. |
| `GET /webauthn` *(open)* | `{passkeys, registered, name}` — whether any account has a passkey (the login page's button), whether any has a key at all (the Network page's warning before a rename), and the system's full name keys are made on (`""` when not known). |
| `POST /login/passkey` *(open)*, `POST /login/passkey/finish` *(open)* | The passkey sign-in: `{}` → `{login, options}` (a discoverable credential, user verification required; `409 webauthn-needs-name` on an address or a bare host name, `429` for a locked address, `403 password_login_disabled` while the switch is off); then `{login, response}` → the login's answer + cookies, method `passkey`. A key that did not verify the user is refused (`401`). `401 invalid-credentials` for a refused key or a spent/run-out `login` (counts towards the lockout of the address), `429 locked-out`. |
| `GET /me/webauthn` **`self`** | The caller's keys: `{keys: [{id, name, created, last_used?, passkey, transports?}], name}` — `id` base64url, `passkey` whether it signs in (`false`: a second-factor key from before task 14 that cannot), `name` the system's full name. A token has none (`409 no-session`). |
| `POST /me/webauthn/begin` **`self`**, `POST /me/webauthn/finish` **`self`** | Adding a key to the caller's account, with `X-Occulite-Confirm` (a confirmed ticket for `/api/auth/v1/me/webauthn`; `403 confirm-required` without). `begin {name}` → `{registration, options}` (the creation options: the account's keys excluded, resident key and user verification **required**, `credProps` asked for; `409 webauthn-needs-name`/`webauthn-wrong-name`, `409 conflict` at the tenth key, `422` for a name that is empty or over 64 characters); `finish {registration, response}` → `201 {key}` (`409 conflict` for a key registered already, `422 webauthn-refused` when the attestation does not verify or the user was not verified, `422 webauthn-not-passkey` for a credential that is not discoverable, `409 pending-unknown` for a spent or run-out `registration`). Logged: `auth: passkey added`. |
| `DELETE /me/webauthn/{id}` **`self`** | Removes the caller's own key, with `X-Occulite-Confirm` → `{ok, keys}`; `404` for an unknown id. The caller's sessions stay. |
| `GET /users/{name}/webauthn`, `DELETE /users/{name}/webauthn/{id}` **`auth:admin`** | Another account's keys, and their removal by an administrator — the recovery when a key is lost — which **ends that account's sessions** like a password reset (the caller's own stays when it is the account). `404 unknown-user`/`not-found`. `GET /users` carries `webauthn_keys` per account, `webauthn_unusable` (how many of them cannot sign in) where there are such, and `console_reset` (when `occulited admin reset-auth` last reset the account). |
| `GET/POST /users`, `PATCH/DELETE /users/{name}` **`auth:admin`** | `POST {username, password, level}` (`role` is the older alias: `admin` = administer, `user` = operate; the default without either is operate); `PATCH {level}` (or `{role}`); the list carries `level` and the derived `role`. every account has a stable `id` (8 hex characters, given once, kept across reads and writes; an older file gets ids when it is read) — the key of what hangs off an account, its favorites node. The last administrator cannot be demoted or deleted. The list never carries an account's preferences. The list is `{users: [{name, role, created, must_change_password?, password_set, last_provider_login?}]}` — `password_set` whether the account has a password, `last_provider_login` when the identity provider last signed it in (RFC 3339). While a provider is configured `POST` takes `password` as optional (absent or empty: an account without a password, which signs in through the provider only); without a provider an empty password is `422 invalid-body`. The `external` field of the first shape is gone. |
| `GET /me/preferences`, `PUT /me/preferences` **`self`** | Any role, the caller's own account only: the shell's per-account preferences, kept with the account in `users.json` so a phone and a desktop show the same tab bar. `{addons: [{id, pinned?, fullscreen?}]}` — the addons with a web frontend of their own in the user's order, `pinned: true` for the ones that have a tab in the tab bar, `fullscreen: true` for the ones whose frontend the shell shows as the whole window (occulited task 24: acted on only for an addon whose manifest declares `ui.fullscreen`, `fullscreen` on `GET /addons`; off until the user ticks it on the Settings page); beside it the App's choices `start_page`, `app_fullscreen` and `app_hidden` (below, under the `favorite` enum). The shell reconciles the list against the installed addons: an entry whose addon is gone, switched off or without a frontend is skipped, an addon the list does not name joins the end unpinned — so a stale entry is harmless and the system never edits the list itself. `PUT` replaces the whole set and answers it as stored (an empty list clears the field); a plain user may write their own — it is self-service like `/password`, not an administrator's mutation. Errors: `422 invalid-body` for an id not matching `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,63}`, an id named twice, more than 100 entries or an unknown field; `409 no-account` for a session that has no account — the anonymous administrator of auth mode off and an API token — where `GET` answers the empty set `{addons: []}` and the shell keeps the choice in the browser instead. |
| `GET/POST /tokens`, `DELETE /tokens/{name}` **`auth:admin`** | `GET` → `{tokens: [{name, scopes, prefix, created, last_used?, expires?, ips?}], scopes, addons: [{id, name, scope}]}` — `scopes` the list a token can be given, in the order the page shows them (`*` is offered beside them); `addons` the installed addons' ingress scopes (`addon:<id>`, openccu-lite task 307) with the addon's name, sorted by id. `POST {name, scopes: [...], expires?: <RFC 3339>, ips?: ["192.168.1.0/24", "10.0.0.5"]}` → `201 {name, scopes, token, expires?, ips?}` — the secret exactly once; `role` (`admin` → `*`, `user` → the read scopes, `led` → `led`) is the older body's alias, taken when `scopes` is absent; `422 invalid-scope` for an unknown name, `self`, an empty list, or an `addon:<id>` whose addon is not installed (the message names it), `422 invalid` for an expiry in the past or a range that is none. `ips` is checked against the address the request came from: the connection's, or on a connection from the loopback (lighttpd) the **last** element of `X-Forwarded-For`, the one lighttpd appends — never a client-sent one (B-230); the lockout by address and the journal's `remote` use the same address. The list never carries hashes. On the console: `occulited token <name> --scope meta:read [--scope …] [--role admin\|user\|led] [--expires 2027-01-01\|30d] [--ip RANGE]…` prints a new secret (headless provisioning). It refuses while no user exists (until the setup every credential is refused) and writes `users.json` with the owner of the state directory. The running daemon notices a changed `users.json` (inode, size, mtime) before it checks a credential, and before each of its own saves reads and merges it record by record — its own additions, changes and deletions stand, the rest comes from disk — so a console token works at once and survives the daemon's saves. A `users.json` the daemon cannot read is never overwritten: the save fails (500 on setup or token creation). **The scope `led`** (the role) is for Home Assistant, Node-RED and other programs that drive the status LED (`POST {name, scopes: ["led"]}`, `occulited token <name> --scope led`): it reaches `GET /api/system/v1/led/state`, `POST`/`DELETE /led/override`, `DELETE /led/override/{id}` and `POST`/`DELETE /led/locate`, and nothing else — every other route, the open ones aside, answers `403`, the addon pages behind occulited's CGI route included (those take an account session, or a token with `system:read`). An account cannot have the role `led` (`422` on `POST /users` and `PATCH /users/{name}`). |
| `PATCH /tokens/{name}` **`auth:admin`**, `POST /tokens/self/rotate` **`self`** | `PATCH {label?, scopes?}`: a paired program's `client.label` (the token keeps its `name`), and the scopes **narrowed only** — a scope the token does not hold is `422 invalid` (*widening is a new token or a new pairing*); 404 `not_found`. `POST /tokens/self/rotate`, called **with the token itself**: a new secret `{token, name, previous_valid_s: 60}`, the old one valid for another minute; a session or an ephemeral token is `403`/`404`. `GET /tokens` lists a paired token with **`client: {app, app_version?, instance?, label, paired_at, paired_by, address, fingerprint?, access, addons?, last_address?}`** (`addons` the addon ids the pairing asked for, task 307; `last_address` follows the address its requests come from). |
| `POST /pairing/request` *(open, local networks only)*, `GET`/`DELETE /pairing/request/{id}` *(open; `Authorization: Pairing <poll>`)* | **Client pairing — the program's side.** `POST {app, app_version?, instance?, name?, access?: {devices?, names?, system?}, addons?: ["<id>", …], purpose?: {<area>: text}, commit}` → **`202 {id, poll, nonce, expires_in: 300, interval: 2, fingerprint}`**. `access` is one level per area: **devices** `read` (`rpc:read`) · `operate` (+`rpc:operate`) · `configure` (+`rpc:configure`) · `administer` (+`rpc:admin`: may pair and delete devices); **names** (names and rooms) `read` (`meta:read`) · `configure` (+`meta:write`); **system** `read` (`system:read`, `logs:read`) · `configure` (+`system:write`); an area left out is none, `422 invalid` for another area or level — pairing never grants `*`, `auth:admin`, `radio:keys`, `power` or `backup`. **`addons`** (openccu-lite task 307) names installed addons whose pages the program wants to reach through the system: the scope `addon:<id>` each (see *The gate and API tokens*), at most 8, `422 invalid` naming an addon that is not installed; an ask may consist of `addons` alone, but must ask for an area or an addon. `purpose` is the program's own words per area (200 characters). `commit` is the hex **SHA-256 of the program's `client_nonce`** (16 or more random bytes). `poll` is the request's secret, `nonce` the system's half of the code (hex), `fingerprint` the certificate the code is bound to (hex SHA-256 of the DER of the certificate lighttpd served on this HTTPS connection; empty over plain HTTP). **The code** both sides show: the first four bytes of **SHA-256(nonce ‖ client_nonce ‖ fingerprint)** (raw bytes) as a big-endian integer, modulo 1 000 000, six digits with leading zeros; the program computes it over the fingerprint of the certificate *it* saw. **The poll** `GET /pairing/request/{id}?client_nonce=<hex>` (the first poll reveals it; a nonce that is not the commitment's is `422`; the request appears on the Status page only after the reveal) `&wait=<s>` (long-poll, at most 30) → `{state: pending\|approved\|rejected\|expired}`; **`approved` carries `{token, name, scopes, access, addons?}` once** — the request is gone after that answer (a second poll is 404). Polling faster than `interval` without `wait` is **`429 slow_down`** with `Retry-After`. `DELETE` withdraws the request (204). **Refusals:** `403 pairing-off` (the switch), `403 not-local` (outside the firewall's *local networks* or the loopback), `429 limit` (one pending per address and program, five in all, ten an hour per address, and a ten-minute mute of the address and program after a rejection or a wrong code), `403 forbidden` for another poll secret. |
| `GET /pairing`, `GET /pairing/stream`, `POST /pairing/{id}/approve`, `POST /pairing/{id}/reject`, `PUT /pairing/settings` **`auth:admin`** | **The administrator's side** (the Status page's card). `GET` → `{enabled, requests: [{id, app, app_version?, instance?, name, address, access, purpose?, addons?: [{id, name}], scopes, code, fingerprint?, created, expires, look_alike?}]}` — never the poll secret; `access` always an object, `{}` for a request of `addons` alone (openccu-lite B-296: it was `null`, and the card was not drawn), as in the approved poll answer and the token's `client` record; `addons` the addons asked for with their names (the card names them, as the only grant when no area is asked); `fingerprint` colon-separated for the card's certificate line; `look_alike` when another pending request comes from the same address or under the same program and instance (*compare the code carefully*). `stream` is the same view as Server-Sent Events (`event: pairing`) at once and on every change, `: ping` every 30 s (the Status page takes it from the topic `pairing` of `GET /api/system/v1/stream` since occulited B-53). `approve {code}` mints an ordinary API token — the name a slug of program and instance (`hm2mqtt-js-nas`, `-2` on a collision), the scopes of the levels asked for, **all or nothing**, the `client` record — and wakes the program's poll: `{ok, token: <name>}`; a code that is not the request's rejects it (**`409 wrong-code`**, and mutes); `409 not-ready` before the program revealed its half; 404 for one that is gone. `reject` mutes the address and program for ten minutes. **`PUT /pairing/settings {enabled}`** is the switch (occulited.json `auth.pairing`, on unless `false`), in force at once; the API tokens panel shows it as *Allow programs to ask for access*. Journal (area `auth`): the request, the approval with who and the scopes, a rejection, a wrong code, an expiry. |
| `GET /config`, `PUT /config` **`auth:admin`** | Admin. **`session_idle`, `session_max`** (task 262): the sessions' idle timeout and absolute lifetime in force, Go durations (`"30m0s"`, `"12h0m0s"`); `PUT` takes them as durations (`"45m"`, `"24h"`; an empty string restores the default, an absent field keeps what is stored) within the bounds idle 5 min–30 d, lifetime 1 h–90 d, idle ≤ lifetime (`422 invalid` otherwise), written to `occulited.json` (`auth.session_idle`, `auth.session_max`) and **in force at once**: running sessions end by the new limits at their next request (the lifetime is measured from the login), and the cookies' `Max-Age` follows the lifetime. The defaults are ASVS Level 2's: 30 minutes idle, 12 hours. `{mode: local\|oidc\|off, modes, name, issuer, client_id, client_secret_set, username_claim, scopes, password_login, running, restart_required}` — the `auth` block of `occulited.json` ([config.md](config.md)); `groups_claim` and `admin_groups` are gone (the role is the account's), and a `PUT` that still sends them is `422 invalid-body`. `PUT` takes the **writable fields only** — `{mode, name, issuer, client_id, client_secret, username_claim, scopes, password_login}`, `client_secret` write-only (empty keeps the stored one); GET's read-only `modes`, `client_secret_set`, `running`, `restart_required` or any other field are `422 invalid-body` with a message that names the field and the writable ones (the Users page sent GET's answer back) — validates (`oidc` needs an issuer URL and a client id), writes the file and answers with `restart_required: true` when the running daemon is on another mode — a mode change takes effect at the next start of occulited, which the Users page (System → Users, section Authentication) offers. **`password_login`** takes effect at once and is written `true` for any mode but `oidc`; absent in the body it stays as stored; a `PUT` that turns it off is `403 provider_session_required` unless the caller's session came through the provider (`GET /state` → `method: oidc`) — a save that leaves it off is not a switch-off. In mode `off` `GET /state` answers an anonymous administrator (`auth_off: true`) and sets the session cookie of the request's scheme, so lighttpd's gate and the `?sid=@..@` convention keep working. 501 when the daemon runs without a configuration file. |
| `GET /oidc/trust`, `POST /oidc/trust`, `DELETE /oidc/trust/{id}`, `POST /oidc/test`, `POST /oidc/peer-chain` **`auth:admin`** | The certificates trusted for the identity provider: a private CA, a chain or the provider's own certificate pinned, **for the OIDC client only** — its TLS trusts the system's pool plus these, nothing else does. Stored in the state directory's `trust-anchors.json` (`{anchors: [{id, purposes, pem, added, added_by}]}`, 0600), one store of extra anchors with a **purpose** tag (`oidc` here) that the trust store page takes over without a migration. `GET` answers `{anchors: [{id, purposes, subject, issuer, not_before, not_after, fingerprint, spki, ca, self_signed, names, expired, expires_soon, added, added_by}]}` (`id` = the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256; `fingerprint` the whole, colon-separated; `spki` the SHA-256 of the public key, base64 — what a pin matches, task 232; `expires_soon` within 30 days). `POST {pem}` trusts every certificate of a pasted or uploaded PEM (a certificate already stored gains the purpose); `{pem, fingerprint}` — the page's confirmation of a certificate from `peer-chain` — must be exactly one certificate with that fingerprint (`422 fingerprint_mismatch`). Refused with 422: a private key anywhere in the text (`private_key`), no certificate (`no_certificate`), one that does not parse (`bad_certificate`), expired (`expired`) or not yet valid (`not_yet_valid`); the answer is `{anchors, added}`. `DELETE` takes the purpose away (the anchor goes when it has none left; 404 `not_found`). Every change is applied to the running provider client at once, and read at start. `POST /oidc/test {issuer?}` (the form's issuer, else the stored one) reads the discovery document with that trust and the OIDC pins (task 232): `{ok, error, tls, issuer, authorization_endpoint, token_endpoint, userinfo_endpoint, issuer_mismatch, verified_by: {…the anchor's fields, trusted_here}, leaf?: {…the presented certificate's fields}, pinned?: {…the pin that matched}, pin_only?}` — `verified_by` is the verified chain's root, `trusted_here` whether it is one of these; `leaf` is what the server presented (the page's *Pin the current certificate*), `pinned` the pin a chain certificate's key matched, `pin_only` that the pin alone vouched (no `verified_by` then). A test records no failure for the Status page; the login does. `POST /oidc/peer-chain {issuer?}` reads the chain the issuer's server presents **without verifying it** — `{chain: [{…fields, pem, trusted}], verified, error}` — for the administrator to compare fingerprints before trusting one; nothing is trusted from it by itself; 502 `unreachable`. |
| `GET /oidc` *(open)* | `{enabled, name?, password_login}` — whether an external login is configured, and whether the password form is on (`true` without a provider, where the switch means nothing) ([config.md](config.md)). |
| `GET /oidc/start` *(open)* | Redirects the browser to the provider (authorization code + PKCE; state kept 10 min). |
| `GET /oidc/callback` *(open)* | The provider returns here. The value of `username_claim` is matched to the account of exactly that name — any account, with or without a password, with the role it has here — and on success the session cookie of the request's scheme is set, `last_provider_login` written and the browser lands on `/` (or on the `?return=` path) — the cookie is the session there. **No account of that name**: nothing is created, the journal gets `auth: login refused` with the name, the provider's `sub` (`subject`), `method=oidc` and the remote address, and the browser lands on `/login?error=no-account&user=<name>` — the login page turns that into its own sentence. Any other failure lands on `/login?error=<message>`. |

### Favorites

The metadata store's enum **`favorite`** (Favoriten / Favorites) is kept in step with the accounts by occulited: one
node per account, its id the account's stable `id`, its name the account's name (renamed with it). **An account that
is deleted takes its node along**: the node goes and its members lose the membership, in one
metadata change (`node.deleted`), and no later sync creates it again; the objects themselves and the other nodes
stay. occulited notices the delete while it runs — `DELETE /users/{name}` or another writer of `users.json`; an
account removed while it was stopped, or a `users.json` without any account, removes nothing. Membership is the object's `enums` (`favorite/<account id>`) as
everywhere; **the order within a page** is the object's `meta.occulite.order["favorite/<account id>"]`, a sparse rank
(1000, 2000, …; a dragged card takes the midpoint), absent = after the ranked ones, by name. A node whose id is no
account's is a *waiting* one (the CCU import's, named after the CCU user): when an account of that name is created,
its members and ranks move to the account's node and the waiting node goes. The App's per-account choices are the
preferences `start_page` (`app` or `status`), `app_fullscreen` (the App without the shell's top bar) and `app_hidden`
(Control's tab taken out of the top bar; the page and its APIs stay, reachable by their address, and a `start_page` of
`app` opens Status while it is set — a preference, not a permission) on `GET/PUT /api/auth/v1/me/preferences` — a PUT
replaces the whole object, so a writer sends `addons` and all three along. An addon's frontend shown as the whole window
the same way is `fullscreen: true` on its `addons` entry (occulited task 24): the shell acts on it only for an addon whose
manifest declares `ui.fullscreen` (`fullscreen` on `GET /addons`), the addon's promise of its own way back to `/`.

## `/api/system/v1`

### Status, radio, services

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /health` *(open)* | `{ok, version, commit, release, base, uptime_s, meta: {revision, recovered}}` for a monitoring probe. `version` is occulited's version — the commit it was built from — and `commit` the bare hash (empty when the binary carries none; the forms are under *occulited's version* below), `release` the image's release identity (openccu-lite's own version, `1.0.0-dev.30`; the base version where the image has none), `base` the OpenCCU base version from `/VERSION` (`3.89.11.20260919`) — the shell's top bar shows them before login. |
| `GET /sbom` *(open)* | The image's SBOM, `/usr/share/openccu-lite/sbom.cdx.json.gz` (CycloneDX 1.6, written by the fork's post-build), served as it lies on disk: `Content-Type: application/vnd.cyclonedx+json`, `Content-Encoding: gzip` for a client that accepts gzip, unpacked for one that does not (`Vary: Accept-Encoding`); `ETag` from the file's hash with `If-None-Match` → 304, `Cache-Control: no-cache`; `?download=1` adds `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="openccu-lite.cdx.json"`. 404 `not-found` on an image without it. No session: the Licences page opens before the login. |
| `GET /licenseinfo` *(open)* | OpenCCU-Base's `/www/rega/licenseinfo.htm` (eQ-3's `createLicenseHtml.py`), which lighttpd would otherwise hand to the web UI. `Content-Type: text/html` without a charset (the file names `iso-8859-1` in its meta tag), `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, conditional requests through `http.ServeContent`. 404 `not-found` without the file. No session. |
| `GET /status` **`system:read`** | Hostname, `/VERSION`, `occulited_version` and `occulited_commit` (occulited's own version — the commit it was built from — and the bare hash; the forms are under *occulited's version* below; each left out when the binary carries none — the Status page shows the version, shortened, between openccu-lite's and OpenCCU's version), uptime, load, memory, disks, time, `timezone` (the zone name from `/etc/config/timezone`, `Europe/Berlin`; the POSIX string from `/etc/config/TZ` only when there is no name) with `tz` (the content of `/etc/config/TZ` when it differs), `hm_mode`, `meta_recovered`, and `unclean_shutdown: {at}` when `/var/status/uncleanShutdown` is there — the marker the watchdog unit leaves when the last stop was not clean. On OpenCCU monit read it and raised a ReGa alarm variable; here the Status page is its consumer. `container: "lxc"` when this system is one (absent on a machine or a VM) — from `/run/systemd/container`, PID 1's `container=` variable, or `PLATFORM=lxc`/`oci` in `/VERSION`, in that order. **`clock: {state, synchronised}`** is the boot's clock gate: `state` is what `occu-clock-valid.service` wrote to `/run/occulite/clock-state` — `rtc` (the RTC set a plausible time), `ntp` (chrony synchronised) or `timeout` (neither in time, so the radio stack started with the clock the system had); absent without the file. After a `timeout`, `synchronised` is chrony's word — `chronyc -n tracking` says `Leap status : Normal`, run as occulited's own user (a monitoring command, no helper), asked at most every 30 s and not again in this run once it said Normal; for `rtc` and `ntp` it is `true`. The Status page shows a quiet notice while `state` is `timeout` and `synchronised` is false. |
| `GET /warnings` **`system:read`** | Any session: the Status page's warnings, evaluated in occulited; the page renders this list and keeps the sentences in its i18n, keyed by `id` and `variant`, filled from `params`. `{warnings: [{id, variant, severity: "error"\|"warning", params?, href?, silenced?: {id, variant, by, at, until}}], periods?: [1, 7, 90]}` — errors before warnings, then in the order of the table under *Status page warnings* below. `silenced` is the caller's own silence and, like `periods`, only given to a session with `system:write` (the level `administer`): any other session gets every warning and never a silence. Every read evaluates all sources again. 501 `unsupported` without the tracker. |
| `POST /warnings/silence` **`system:write`** | **Administrator**: `{id, variant, days}` silences one active warning for the caller, `days` 1, 7 or 90; silencing the same warning again replaces the silence. 200 with the list as `GET /warnings` answers it plus `silence: {id, variant, by, at, until}`; 400 `invalid-period`, 404 `not-active` (no active warning with that id and variant), 403 `forbidden`, 500 `save-failed` when the file could not be written (the silence is then not in force). |
| `DELETE /warnings/silence/{id}/{variant}` **`system:write`** | **Administrator**: lifts the caller's own silence of that warning. The variant is the rest of the path, URL-escaped (a backup target's variant is a path). 200 with the list; 404 `not-silenced`. |
| `GET /radio` **`system:read`** | The inventory from `/var/hm_mode` and `InterfacesList.xml`: mode, host, modules (protocol, device, serial, SGTIN, firmware, address), LEDs, interfaces (name, URL, info), `lan_gateways` (the `[Interface N]` sections of `rfd.conf` other than the built-in module, without the encryption key), `security_key_set` / `security_key_known` (`crypttool -v -t 0`). | Each interface carries `subscribers`: the XML-RPC callbacks registered with its process (`/var/*.handlers`), `{id, url, local, duplicate?}`, unique by id and url (rfd lists a callback twice when a client registers again; exact duplicates are dropped); `local` is a callback on this system — the loopback names, and since 28.6 any of the system's own interface addresses; the host is taken from a callback without a scheme too (`127.0.0.1:2126`, `[::1]:2001`: a registration the daemon itself cannot call, but one from this system, occulited B-44). **`duplicate: true`** marks an id that is registered with another callback too: a client's old registration beside its new one. Which of the two is stale is not knowable from the file, so both carry it; it is a hint for the page, nothing acts on it. **`own: true`** marks the RPC process's own registration, `occulited_<interface>` at `http://127.0.0.1:8184/cb/<interface>`: the page labels it *this system (occulited)* and offers no removal (it registers itself again anyway). **Both files are read at every request**: occulited starts right after the network, before the radio detection writes `/var/hm_mode` (5 s on the Charly, 22 s on the Pi 4 with its HmIP-RFUSB) and before `occu-init-hs485d` rewrites `InterfacesList.xml`, so nothing reads either once and keeps it — this route, `GET /status`'s `hm_mode`, the radio sampler and every interface call read them when asked. The Interfaces page reads the inventory again while it shows no module or the radio stack is starting.
| `GET /radio/health` **`system:read`** | Live from `listBidcosInterfaces` on every interface process, sampled once a minute: `{polled, interfaces: [{interface, address, type, connected, default, firmware, duty_cycle, carrier_sense?, carrier_sense_source?, radio?, radio_name?, module?, adapter?, path?}], answering, errors, history: {"<interface>/<address>": [{t, dc, up}]}, rates: {"<interface>": [{t, in, up}]}, subscribers: {"<interface>": {total, internal, external, clients: [{id, url, internal, own?, stream?}]}}, busy, busy_interface}`. **`answering`** lists the interface processes that answered `listBidcosInterfaces` with a fault — hs485d (BidCos-Wired) and user interfaces such as CUxD do not implement it: they are up and have no radio list, so they are neither in `interfaces` nor in `errors` (a process that does not answer at all is in `errors`). **`history`** is a ring of **500 samples per interface** (about 8 h at a sample a minute, plus the module's own reports), oldest first, kept in occulited's database file unless its storage is `ram` (`GET /datastore`) — a restart of occulited starts with the kept history; `busy` = any duty cycle ≥ 80 %. **`carrier_sense`** is `CARRIER_SENSE_LEVEL` rounded to a whole percent, present whenever a value is known (0 included). **`carrier_sense_source`** says where it came from: `interface` when the `listBidcosInterfaces` entry carries it; `device` when it is read from channel 0 of the radio module's own device — neither the HmIP-RFUSB nor the RPI-RF-MOD puts it into the list (hmipserver 2026-09, measured on the Pi 4 and the Charly). For an HmIP entry without a level of its own the sample asks the same interface process `getParamsetDescription("<device>:0", "VALUES")` once per device — `<device>` is the entry's `ADDRESS` without the SGTIN prefix `3014F711A0`, the `listDevices` device of `SUBTYPE CCU` — and, when that names `CARRIER_SENSE_LEVEL`, `getValue("<device>:0", "CARRIER_SENSE_LEVEL")` at every sample. `carrier_sense_source: "device"` without `carrier_sense` means the device has the datapoint and the module has not reported it yet: hmipserver answers fault -5 until then, a few minutes after the radio stack starts. A device without the datapoint, or one hmipserver does not know (fault -2, a LAN gateway), is not asked again for 15 minutes; an interface that does not answer is asked again at the next sample. `DUTY_CYCLE_LEVEL` from the same channel is not used: both modules report `DUTY_CYCLE` in the list, and it feeds `busy` and the firmware gate. **`units`** (systemd only, absent on busybox) are the radio stack's units from one `systemctl show` for all of them: `[{unit, interfaces, active_state, sub_state, starting?, queued?, starting_s?}]` in boot order — `occu-init-rf-hardware` (the detection that writes `/var/hm_mode`), `multimacd`, `rfd`, `hmipserver`, `hs485d`, `hmlangw`; a unit the system does not have (`LoadState=not-found`) is left out. `interfaces` are the `InterfacesList.xml` names the unit's process serves, read at the request: by name (`BidCos-RF` → rfd, `BidCos-Wired` → hs485d, `HmIP-RF` and `VirtualDevices` → hmipserver), else by the URL's port (2000/2001/2010/9292, the internal 32000/32001/32010/39292, the TLS faces 42000/42001/42010/49292). `starting` is true for a unit that is `activating` (not one waiting in `auto-restart`) or `inactive` with a start job that waits for the units it is ordered after (`queued: true`). `starting_s` is how long it has been starting, in whole seconds, from `InactiveExitTimestampMonotonic` against `/proc/uptime` (the wall clock of a system without an RTC is wrong at boot); absent for a queued unit and at 0. The reading is cached for 3 s, the ages moved on. **A stale error is dropped:** an interface in `errors` whose unit became active after `polled` failed because it was starting — it is left out of the answer, and the sampler polls again in the background (at most every 15 s), so the pages do not say *not answering* for up to a minute after hmipserver is ready. The Status and Interfaces pages show an interface of a starting unit as *starting* with its seconds, never down. **`feed`** is occulited's view of the RPC process: `{connected, since?, boot_id?, last_error?, received, interfaces: [{name, state, registered, last_activity, events, telegrams, last_error?}]}` — `connected` says whether `occulited-rpc.service` is there; per interface `state` is `up` (registered, the daemon answers), `down` (it does not) or `silent` (nothing arrived for 5 minutes and a ping is out), `last_activity` the last moment the daemon called the listener for that interface (an event, a PONG for anyone, a `listDevices` at its start — the Interfaces page's *last event … ago*), `events` the events since the process started, `telegrams` the calls among them that carried a device's events (occulited task 13: a `system.multicall` is one however many events it holds, a single `event` one, a ping's `PONG` none). **`module`, `adapter`, `path`** (occulited task 13, from the radio plan of this boot, absent for an entry the plan does not know): the radio behind an entry as the Status page's interface card names it — `module` the module or gateway (`RPI-RF-MOD`, `HmIP-RFUSB`, `HM-MOD-RPI-PCB`, `HM-CFG-USB-2`, `HM-LGW`, `HM-CFG-LAN`), `adapter` the HB-RF board it sits on (`HB-RF-USB-2`, `HB-RF-ETH`, from the raw-uart `device_type`), `path` how the process reaches it: `multimacd` (through the multiplexer: rfd's local module always, hmipserver on `/dev/mmd_hmip`), `direct` (the process opens the UART itself), `usb` (a USB stick without an HB-RF board: the HmIP-RFUSB opened directly, the HM-CFG-USB-2), `lan` (rfd's LAN gateways, by the entry's `type` `HMLGW2` or `Lan Interface`). **`rates`** (occulited task 13): per interface process occulited's subscriber is registered at, a ring of 500 samples like `history`, one per poll, kept in the same database file (series `rate:<interface>` of the health bucket) — `in` the events **per minute** since the previous poll (occulited task 17), to a thousandth: the `telegrams`, so a `system.multicall` with a device's events is one event however many values it carries. The Status page calls them *Events/min* in both languages. There is no outgoing rate (task 13 had one, `out`, of the writes forwarded through lite-rpc; task 17 removed it: it saw only what passed through occulited). Rows kept by task 13's builds (per second, with `out`) are read as `in` × 60. `up: false` (`in` 0) when the subscriber was not registered at the poll: the line breaks there. The first poll after a start, and one after the subscriber took an interface up afresh (its counts start again), only mark the counts. **`subscribers`** (occulited task 13): per interface known to the answer (`interfaces`, `answering`, `errors`, `feed`) that has a handlers file (as `GET /radio`'s `subscribers`; CUxD and custom entries have none and are absent) — `total` the registered callbacks, each id and URL once, `internal` those whose callback is on this system, exactly `GET /radio`'s `local` (the loopback names `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost` - occulited, the addons, hmipserver's own `*_java` - and any of the system's own interface addresses, an addon that announced its LAN address), `external` every other address, and `clients` the entries with `internal` and `own` (occulited's registration). **lite-rpc's open event streams count too** (occulited B-45): every stream of `GET /api/rpc/v1/events` (SSE) or `/events/ws` (WebSocket) whose `interface` filter takes the interface — every interface without one, whatever else it filters — is one subscriber of its own, after the callbacks in `clients`: `id` the token's name (an addon's `addon:<id>`) or the account's, `url` empty, `stream: {id, kind, transport, remote?}` (`kind` `token`, `session` or `public`, `transport` `sse` or `websocket`, `remote` the client's address), `internal` by the same rule applied to `remote` (the loopback or one of the system's own addresses). occulited's own registration in the handlers file (`own`) stays one subscriber — the system's own, which feeds the state store, the history and the service messages — and is not the streams' sum, so nothing is counted twice; the Status page lists the streams under it as *via occulited*. **`carrier_sense_source: "event"`** and a sample with the event's own time: while the RPC process reports the module's `CARRIER_SENSE_LEVEL`/`DUTY_CYCLE_LEVEL` (every 5 minutes, and on a change), the poll takes the last reported level and makes no `getValue`; a level older than 10 minutes falls back to the poll as before. |
| `GET /service-messages` **`system:read`** | Read-only: the service messages the system itself collects — `{count, messages: [{interface, address, channel, key, value, since, seen, type?, name?, enums?}], swept, errors, feed?}`, the oldest first. A message is a maintenance datapoint that is active on a device's channel 0: `true`, a number other than 0, a string other than empty. **Which datapoints count is the CCU's own rule:** the ones whose paramset description carries the service flag (`FLAGS & 8`), read once per device type — `UNREACH`, `STICKY_UNREACH`, `LOWBAT`/`LOW_BAT`, `CONFIG_PENDING`, `UPDATE_PENDING`, `SABOTAGE`, `FAULT_REPORTING`, the `ERROR_*` codes on the types that have them; before the first sweep of a type the well-known names decide. **Two sources:** a sweep of every device's channel 0 (`listDevices`, `getParamsetDescription(<device>:0, VALUES)` per type, `getParamset(<device>:0, VALUES)` per device) at start, every 15 minutes, and whenever the RPC process reconnects or reports an interface up or restarted; and the RPC process's events in between. **Sticky messages** (the description's `FLAGS & 16`: `STICKY_UNREACH`, `STICKY_SABOTAGE`; before the first sweep of a type, a name that begins with `STICKY_`) are the ones no event clears — the interface process resets one when it is acknowledged with `setValue` and sends nothing — so while one is active its device's channel 0 is read again every 60 s, 5 s after an event cleared another message of that device (`UNREACH` going `false`), and 1 s after a `setValue` or `putParamset` on that channel passed through `/api/rpc/v1`; such a read reconciles the device's whole channel 0 by the sweep's rules (answered from the process's cache: no radio traffic). Every read of a channel 0, the 15-minute sweep's too, is handed to the state store, which takes what differs from its entries (`GET /api/rpc/v1/state`) and reports it on the stream as a `state` message. **The transitions** (the rules): inactive → active creates the entry with `since` now; active → inactive removes it; the same active value again changes nothing; an active value that changes to another (a fault code) is a new message with a new `since`. **`seen`** is `event` when the change was watched happening and `start` when a sweep found the message already active — the real start is earlier, and the page says *at least since*. a message found active at the start takes as its `since` the state store's `lc` of that datapoint when the store holds the same value (kept in occulited's database file across a restart), so a fault that stood for weeks does not read as *since the last restart*. An interface that does not answer the sweep keeps its entries (a daemon that is down is not a device that recovered) and is named in `errors`. `name` and `enums` come from the metadata store's device object (`<interface>.<address>`) where there is one. `feed` is `GET /radio/health`'s. 501 without a store (development). |
| `GET /service-messages/stream` **`system:read`** | The same view as Server-Sent Events: one `event: messages` with the whole view at once and again whenever the active set changes, `: ping` every 30 s. The Status page and the Control app take the same view from the topic `service-messages` of `GET /stream` (occulited B-53; this route stays for programs) and also ask `GET /service-messages` every 30 s, so a lost stream costs half a minute at most. |
| `GET /radio/hmip/access-points` **`system:read`** | The HmIP access points paired to HmIP-RF (types `HmIP-HAP*` and `*DRAP`). `{interface: "HmIP-RF", available, error?, access_points: [...], firewall}`. `available` false when HmIP-RF is not in `InterfacesList.xml`; `error` when it does not answer `listDevices`. Each access point: `address, type, firmware, available_firmware?, firmware_update_state?` (device description), `reachable` (`UNREACH` of `:0` negated; `null` when not known yet), `ip_address?` (`:0`'s `IP_ADDRESS`), `duty_cycle?` (percent: `:0`'s `DUTY_CYCLE_LEVEL`, else the `DUTY_CYCLE` of its own `listBidcosInterfaces` entry; absent for a wired DRAP), `duty_cycle_limit?`, `carrier_sense?`, `config_pending?`, `update_pending?`, `connected?` (its `listBidcosInterfaces` entry), `error?` (`:0` did not answer), `sgtin?` (from hmipserver's `<SGTIN>.dev` file names in `/etc/config/crRFD/data`; the company prefix is not always eQ-3's), `name?` (the metadata store's `HmIP-RF.<address>`), `latest?`/`update_available?` (the device firmware check). `firewall` (`null` without the rule file): `ports: [{port, proto, target, rule?, source?, owner?}]` for tcp 9293, tcp 9294, udp 43438 - the first enabled IPv4 rule for the port (rules narrowed by a source port or a destination passed over), or the policy (`rule` absent) - and `hint`: `keep` (paired, all ACCEPT), `blocked` (paired, one not ACCEPT), `unused` (none paired, all ACCEPT), `reopen` (none paired, one not ACCEPT), `unknown` (HmIP-RF did not answer) - the cases. |
| `GET /radio/lan-devices`, `POST /radio/lan-devices/search` **`system:read`** | EQ-3's LAN devices as NetFinder finds them. **Only the POST sends anything** (an outgoing connection only when the user presses the button) - the GET answers the last search's result, however old, and never scans (`?refresh=1` is gone); before the first search it is `{devices: []}` without `scanned`. The search: one Identify (`I`, udp 43439, version 2, type `eQ3-*`, serial `*`) to 255.255.255.255, 224.0.0.1 and each interface's directed broadcast from an ephemeral client socket (no listener), answers collected for 2.5 s (versions 2 and 4 read; this system's own answer left out); then `n` and `c` unicast to each device that is not a CCU. A network write's device found again replaces its entry in the kept result. `{scanned, devices: [...], error?}`; each device: `type, serial, version, protocol_version, ip` (the answer's source), `services?` (`[{protocol, port}]` of the Identify answer), `runtime?` (`n`: `{ip, gateway, netmask, dns1, dns2}`), `config?` (`c`: the same plus `dhcp, auto_ip, crypt, name_max, name`), `kind` (`gateway`, `access-point`, `ccu`, `other`), `writable` (gateways and access points), `password?` (`configured`: the key is the gateway's in `rfd.conf`/`hs485d.conf`; `sticker`: asked), `configured?` (`rf`/`wired`), `name?` (the configured gateway's), `paired?` (an access point paired to HmIP-RF, by SGTIN), `same_subnet` (its running address is in one of this system's IPv4 networks), `link?` (another system's web UI). Keys never leave the system. |
| `POST /radio/lan-devices/{serial}/network` **`system:write`** | Sets the network of a LAN gateway or an HmIP access point: `{dhcp, ip, netmask, gateway, dns1, dns2, password?, other_subnet?}`. The device is looked up afresh by its serial (broadcast). `403 not-writable` for a CCU or another type; `404` when nothing answers; `422 invalid` (IPv4, a contiguous mask /8–/30, not the network or broadcast address, the gateway in the new subnet); `409 other-subnet` (the address is in none of this system's networks and `other_subnet` is not set); `409 address-in-use` (this system's own address, another found device's, or a completed neighbour entry after a probe). `C` is sent with the device's own Auto IP flag, encryption switch and DNS name kept; a device that answers code 3 gets it wrapped (`*`, AES-128-CBC with the device's IV, key = MD5 of the password: the configured gateway key, else `password`, never stored); `422 wrong-password`, `502 no-answer`/`refused`. A configured gateway's new static address is written into `rfd.conf`/`hs485d.conf` and `rfd`/`hs485d` restarts (`gateway_file`, `restarted` or `restart_error`). Then the device is looked up every 5 s for up to 45 s: `{written, restarts, device?, refind_error?}`. Every write is logged (user, device, old and new values, no password). |
| `GET /radio/lan-gateways` **`system:read`** | `{rf: [...], wired: [...], types: {rf: ["Lan Interface","HMLGW2"], wired: ["HMWLGW"]}, pending_key_changes: [serials]}` — the `[Interface N]` sections of `rfd.conf` (without the built-in module) and `hs485d.conf`: `index, type, name, serial, address, has_key`. Keys never leave the system. `GET /radio` carries the same as `lan_gateways`/`wired_gateways`. |
| `PUT /radio/lan-gateways` **`system:write`** | `{class: "rf"|"wired", gateways: [{type, name, serial, key, ip}], restart}` replaces the whole list of that class, numbered afresh, exactly as the WebUI's `BidCoS_RF.setConfiguration`/`BidCoS_Wired.setConfiguration` wrote it (header kept, then one section per gateway). An empty `key` keeps the key the file has for that serial. `restart: true` restarts `rfd`/`hs485d` through the service manager (`restarted`, or `restart_error`); otherwise the daemon reads the file at its next start. A `wired` write keeps hs485d's run marker and the `BidCos-Wired` entry of `/etc/config/InterfacesList.xml` with the file: in with the first gateway, out with the last, the other entries untouched. 422 with the reason on a bad type, serial, key, name or address. |
| `POST /radio/lan-gateways/key` **`system:write`** | `{class, serial, ip, key, current_key}` queues an access-key change for a gateway: `/etc/config/<serial>.keychange`, applied by the firmware's `S59SetLGWKey` (`eq3configcmd setlgwkey`) at the next boot, as `changeLanGatewayKey.tcl` did. |
| `GET /radio/connections` **`system:read`** | Phase 3: the connection of each interface process. `{available, choices: {hmip, bidcos}, options: {hmip: [Option], bidcos: [Option]}, plan?: ConnPlan, mode?, modules: [ConnModule], header_silent?, hb_rf_eth?, hmip_move_back?, hmip_counter_gap?, running: Change\|null, last: Change\|null}`. `available` is false without the boot's `/run/occulite/radio/plan.json` and `modules.json` (an image whose radio stack is not occulited's). **`hmip_move_back`** (openccu-lite B-285) is there while a module-move snapshot is kept and no change runs: `{previous (the module's SGTIN), at, choices (from before the move), devices (device files kept), counter_gap?}`. **`counter_gap`** (openccu-lite B-302) is there when the previous module's HmIP security counter is behind what the module in use sent: `{from, to, highest, back, behind, until}` - `highest` is the module in use's counter as hmipserver last logged it (task 299's state file; its computed counter when none was logged), `back` the previous module's counter at the way back (its logged counter or its computed one from the snapshot's access point file, whichever is higher), `until` when its computed counter reaches `highest` (the counter catches up at the first start of HmIP-RF after that). Devices that heard the module in use drop the previous module's commands until then; the page says the wait before the user confirms. Nothing is written to the identity files for it. **`hmip_counter_gap`** is the same object with `at` after a way back that opened a gap, while HmIP-RF runs on `to` and no start has logged a counter at or above `highest` since (kept in `<state-dir>/hmip-local-key/counter-gap.json`; a closed gap is forgotten), with `hostname` beside it (the system's name, typed as the confirmation of the way back). **`choices`** are read from the daemons' own files: `hmip` is `occulite.hmip.adapter=<serial\|SGTIN>` in `/etc/config/crRFD/hmip_user.conf`, `bidcos` the first-line comment `# occulite.bidcos.module=<serial>\|none` in `/etc/config/rfd.conf`, `""` is auto (upstream's pick). An `Option` is a detected module the process can use, `{id (the serial), hardware, node?, device_type?, sgtin?, version?}`: HmIP takes modules with a real HmIP address (the TK stick included), BidCos-RF modules with a BidCos address except the TK, and the HM-CFG-USB-2. `ConnPlan` is `{multimacd, rfd, hmipserver: {run, node?, reason}, hmrf?, hmip? (Role), rfd_local, rfd_usb_adapter, rfd_lan_gateway, hmip_advanced, missing_hmip?, missing_bidcos?, hmip_pin?, interfaces: [names], notes: [why]}`. **`hmip_pin`** (openccu-lite task 318, D-120): with the HmIP choice on *Automatic*, the module HmIP-RF is kept on - the one it last ran on, on this board (`/usr/local/var/lib/occulite/hmip-module.json`, written by the radio run; a record from another board, e.g. in a restored backup, does not count, and a system without one keeps upstream's pick). When that module is missing, `missing_hmip` names it too and hmipserver runs its VirtualDevices half alone (warning `hmip-module-missing`); a choice of another module is then a move from it (`hmip_move.from`), confirmed, with the module-move snapshot where its identity files are on the system. A `Change` is `{choices, previous, started, finished?, ok, error?, lines: ["hh:mm:ss text"]}`. **`modules`** (openccu-lite B-272) is every module of the detection, `{serial, hardware, node?, device_type?, sgtin?, version?, probe, detail?, roles: ["BidCos-RF"\|"HmIP-RF"]}` - the Interfaces page's module cards, a module in no role (one on an HB-RF-ETH added while both processes are pinned to a stick) included; the GPIO header's UART (or the `ttyAMA0` fallback) whose probe found nothing (`timeout`, `none`) is no module and is left out (openccu-lite B-300: the Pi images create the node whether or not a module is fitted; the detection in `modules.json` and the journal keep it), a header that answered wrongly (`error`) stays. **`header_silent`** (B-300) is there only when such a silent header meets a choice whose module no answering module carries: `{node, probe, detail?, missing: [identity]}` - the page's hint in place of a card. **`hb_rf_eth`** is there when a board is configured under LAN devices: `{address, connected, detected, serial?}` - `connected` is the kernel module's state, `detected` whether a module behind the board is in the detection (the radio hotplug attaches and probes a board some ten seconds after its address is set; until then the page says the board is on its way and polls). |
| `POST /radio/connections/preview` **`system:read`** | `{hmip, bidcos}` (`"auto"` or `""`, `"none"` for BidCos-RF only, or a module identity) → `{choices, plan: ConnPlan, changed, restarts: [units], bidcos_lost, devices: [{address, type}], devices_error?, hmip_move?}`. The plan is made from the boot's detection and today's files with the choices substituted; nothing changes. **`hmip_move`** (openccu-lite B-285) is there when HmIP-RF would leave one module for another: `{from, to (SGTINs), local_key, snapshot, to_version?, refused?}` - `to_version` the target module's application firmware as the detection read it; **`refused`** `{module, version, minimum}` (openccu-lite B-289) when that firmware is below 2.8.0 and local key mode is off: such a module cannot take the HmIP network's key, hmipserver would move the access point onto it without the key and the module could not send, and the key server refuses every exchange away from it - the page says so with the firmware update as the way, and `PUT` refuses the change (with local key mode on the move is offline and allowed; an unknown version is not refused). With local key mode off the move is an adapter exchange through eQ-3's key server, which may refuse the exchange back, so the page warns and the change keeps the snapshot `snapshot` says it will (false without the local key service, or with local key mode on). `bidcos_lost` says the change takes rfd's local radio (a module through multimacd, or the HM-CFG-USB-2) away; `devices` are then rfd's paired devices (`listDevices`, the central's own `*-RCV-50` left out). 422 for a choice the system cannot carry (HmIP off, an identity that is not a detected module for that process). |
| `PUT /radio/connections` **`system:write`** | `{hmip, bidcos, confirm}` → 202 with the status; the change runs detached and the page polls `GET /radio/connections` while `running` is set. **A change that moves HmIP-RF to another module** (the preview's `hmip_move`, openccu-lite B-285) needs `confirm: true` (openccu-lite task 317, D-120: hmipserver rewrites its identity files for the new module) - without it `409 confirm-required` with `hmip_move` (and `devices: []`), nothing written; when a snapshot of the module left from a switch to local key mode is still kept, the preview's `hmip_move.snapshot_blocked` is true and the change is refused with **`409 snapshot-blocked`** (`detail: {sgtin}`, nothing written; openccu-lite B-301, the maintainer: the refusal stays) - the page explains it and offers `DELETE /radio/hmip/local-key/snapshots/{sgtin}` in a danger dialog, then asks the change again; it first keeps a **module-move snapshot** of the module it leaves - its `.ap/.apkx/.bbkx`, `/etc/config/hmip_address.conf`, every `<SGTIN>.dev` device file and the connection choices from before - under the local key mode's snapshots (`kind: "module-move"`; a failure ends the change before anything is written; nothing is taken with local key mode on, where no key server is involved).
