It writes the files whose choice changed (through the helper), stops hmipserver, rfd and multimacd, restarts `occu-init-rf-hardware` (`occulited radio run`: detection, plan, render) and starts the three again - a daemon the new plan does not need is skipped by its unit's condition and ends inactive, not failed. **From before the stop until its own starts are done the radio units refuse every start from outside the change** (openccu-lite B-307): the change writes `/run/occulite/radio/changing` (through the helper; its first line is a deadline in whole seconds of `/proc/uptime`, the change's timeout and a minute), and the units' `ExecCondition=+/usr/libexec/occu/lite-radio-gate` skips a start while it is there and the deadline has not passed - so a user's `systemctl start`, a unit that wants a radio daemon or a `Restart=` cannot start a daemon on the old plan's files, or rfd and hmipserver before multimacd. Once the new plan is written the change lets each unit through right before it starts it, in boot order (a line with the unit's name in the marker), removes the marker after its last start, and removes it on every way out; a change cut off by a restart of occulited has it removed at the next start; hs485d or hmlangw, which the change does not stop, are started afterwards when the plan runs them and their last start was skipped. The hotplug's re-plan (`occulited radio hotplug`) holds the units the same way, for at most three minutes. Meanwhile addon starts through the API wait (`POST /services/{id}/{action}`). The daemons are started on a failure too, and a change cut off by a restart of occulited is closed as failed at the next start and the daemons started. **422 `hmip-firmware`** with `detail: {module, version, minimum}` for a move the preview's `hmip_move.refused` names (openccu-lite B-289) - nothing is written or kept. **409 `confirm-required`** with `devices` when the change takes the local BidCos-RF radio away while devices are paired (or rfd did not answer) and `confirm` is not set - nothing is written. 409 `busy` during a change or a coprocessor flash; 503 `unavailable` without a plan. **HmIP only** (openccu-lite task 322) is `bidcos: "none"` without a LAN gateway: rfd (and multimacd, but for the HM-MOD-RPI-PCB) off, hmipserver directly on the module, BidCos-RF out of the interface list; the pages call the choice *Off (HmIP only)* then and *No local radio (LAN gateway only)* with a gateway. The welcome page offers it once - only where rfd runs on a local module or USB adapter, no LAN gateway is configured, the choice is automatic and rfd lists no paired device; nothing preselected - and on *Done* sends `{hmip: <the current choice>, bidcos: "none"}`, polls until `running` is null and `last.ok`, and only then starts the local key step's `PUT /radio/hmip/local-key`. The system never picks it by itself. |
| `POST /radio/security-key` **`system:write`** | `{key}` sets the system security key (Zentralenschlüssel) as `cp_security.cgi` did: at least 5 of `[0-9a-zA-Z_]`; 409 `same-key` when it is the current one (`crypttool -v -t 3`); rfd `changeKey` (re-keys every AES-capable BidCos device; the error names the WebUI's hint about devices that did not get the previous key yet); then `crypttool -S` under the next index. Response `{security_key_set: true}`. The UI confirms with the cost spelled out (HmIP devices have to be taught in again). |
| `POST /radio/subscribers/remove` **`system:write`** | **Admin**: `{interface, id, url}` removes one subscription from an interface process with the XML-RPC `init(url, "")` every client uses to deregister, sent over the loopback to the process URL of `InterfacesList.xml` (the one `internal/interfaces` calls). Only a pair that is in the process's handlers file right now is sent: 422 `not-subscribed` when it is not (a stale page), 422 `unknown-interface` for a name that is not in `InterfacesList.xml`, 400 when a field is missing, 403 for a user, 502 `interface-call` when the process does not answer or faults (10 s), 501 without interface processes (a development root). After the call the handlers file is re-read until the pair is gone, for at most 3 s (hmipserver writes `LegacyService.handlers` a moment after `init` answers): `{removed, subscribers}` — `removed: false` when the entry stayed; `subscribers` is the interface's list as the file has it now. One journal line (`radio: subscription removed`, or `removing a subscription failed`) with the user, interface, id and URL. Measured 2026-09-12 on the Pi 4 and the Charly (3.89.8): the entry goes on rfd (`http://`, `xmlrpc_bin://` and `binrpc://` URLs; the URL has to match exactly, scheme included — an `http://` URL does not remove a `binrpc://` entry on the same port), on hmipserver's legacy face and on the VirtualDevices process; `init` with an unreachable remote callback answers at once; hmipserver does not keep a new registration whose callback refuses the connection. |
| `GET /radio/subscribers/reachability` **`system:read`** | The follow-up, any session, like `GET /radio`: whether each subscriber's callback accepts a TCP connection — the Interfaces page's *not reachable* hint, which nothing acts on. `{subscribers: [{interface, id, url, reachable?, reason?}]}` for every entry of the handlers files of the interfaces in `InterfacesList.xml`, in their order. occulited connects to the callback's host and port (loopback or LAN), sends nothing and closes the connection: `reachable: true`, or `false` with `reason` `refused`, `timeout` (nothing within 1 s) or `unreachable` (any other error). Only the addresses in the handlers files are connected to; nothing is taken from the request. Each address once however many entries name it, 16 at a time, 1 s per connect, 5 s per request; a verdict is kept for 30 s per address (a request that went away keeps none). `http://` and `https://` without a port are probed on 80 and 443; an `xmlrpc://`, `xmlrpc_bin://` or `binrpc://` URL without a port has no `reachable`. The page asks once per page load and again only for entries that appear later. |
| `GET /radio/subscribers/stalls` **`system:read`** | The interface processes held by a callback listener that takes their call and never answers. occulited's subscriber notices two kinds: `delivery` - the process answers calls (ping, init) but a registration made afresh after an unanswered ping brings no callback (hmipserver: its init-time `listDevices` to a new listener runs on one shared worker thread; while that hangs no event reaches anybody, and it never times out - 41 min measured); `calls` - the process's own XML-RPC calls get no answer within 10 s (rfd inside an `init` whose `system.listMethods` callback hangs: its whole RPC server waits). The registering `init` of `VirtualDevices` has 30 s (openccu-lite B-270: hmipserver answers it only after its backend command, 10 s on a system without HmIP radio); an `init` that got no answer in time while the process's handlers file lists occulited's entry and was written since the `init` began counts as registered - no stall, no retry, one `Info` line per interface and run. `{stalls: [{interface, kind, since, checked_at?, listeners: [{address, id?, url?, connected, local, owner?, verdict}]}]}`. For each stalled interface occulited asks every listener: the handlers file's entries with `system.listMethods(<their own id>)`, the call the daemons make and as they make it (BIN-RPC for `binrpc://` and `xmlrpc_bin://`; node-red-contrib-ccu ends Node-RED on a call without the id) - `answers` (any byte back, or a close, within 5 s), `no-answer`, `refused`, `unreachable`. The loopback addresses the process holds an established connection to (`/proc/net/tcp{,6}`, by the user of the socket listening on the interface's port) that are not in the handlers file are never called, and one on a port of the system's interface processes (every `InterfacesList.xml` port, and the classic ports with their loopback backends: hmipserver's connection to rfd, B-228) is no candidate at all: `holds` when every registered listener answers (the one the process waits on), else `unregistered`. occulited's own entries and LAN addresses that are not in the handlers file are left alone; `owner` is the user of a local listener's listening socket (an addon's user). A check runs in the background when a stall is new or its result is older than a minute; `?check=1` waits for it (20 s at most). Empty when nothing is held. **Kind `listener`:** a listener whose registration at hmipserver finished and that later takes an event without answering holds only itself (hmipserver delivers each listener's events on a worker pool of its own), so the subscriber sees no stall - but Vert.x's blocked-thread checker logs `Thread <id>_WorkerPool-1 has been blocked for N ms` with a stack trace every second until the connection ends. occulited reads this boot's journal of `hmipserver.service` for that line (`journalctl -g '_WorkerPool-[0-9]+ has been blocked for'`, the last 60 s, at most 500 lines) on every read of this list and of the warnings; the id names the listener, its handlers entry (`HmIP-RF`'s or `VirtualDevices'`) its address. Such a listener has `verdict: "blocked"` and `blocked_for` (seconds, from the newest line); an interface with no subscriber stall gets a view of kind `listener` (`since` = the newest line less its `blocked_for`, `checked_at` = now), one that is stalled as well gets the listener added to (or replacing the probe's entry in) its list. A pool whose id is on no list any more (a deregistration does not release it) is named by its id with an empty `address`: nothing to end. occulited's own ids and the shared pool's `vert.x-worker-thread-N` (the init-time hang, which names nobody) are never named. occulited logs the find once per episode. |
| `POST /radio/subscribers/drop` **`system:write`** | **Admin**: `{interface, address}` ends the held process's connections to one listener the last check found `no-answer` or `holds`, or hmipserver's log names `blocked` (422 `not-stuck` otherwise - the address is never taken from the request alone). The listener's handlers entries go first (`init(url, "")`, 3 s; once more for 10 s after the drop when the process did not answer), then the helper's `rpc-drop`: the port must be a loopback URL's of `InterfacesList.xml`, the process is the owner of the socket listening there, and only its established outgoing connections to that address are shut - a child `occulited rpc-drop <pid> <fd>` takes a duplicate of the process's socket with `pidfd_getfd` and `shutdown(SHUT_RDWR)`s it (the kernels have no `SOCK_DESTROY`); the process's read ends and it goes on. For up to 15 s after it (5 s without one end it early) a new connection of the process to the same listener is ended as well, so one request covers rfd's calls again. The helper's unit takes `pidfd_getfd` out of the `@debug` set it filters; where a unit does not, the filter kills the child (not the helper) and the answer is 409 `drop-not-allowed` - a restart of the interface process ends it too. `{connections, deregistered, entries}`; 502 `drop-failed` otherwise. Measured 2026-09-25 on the Pi 4 (dev.24): a deregistration alone releases neither process; after the drop hmipserver delivered at once and did not call the listener again; rfd called the listener again at once three times, then registered it anyway, timed its event delivery out after 10 s and dropped it from `RFD.handlers` itself. A listener that finished its registration and hangs later holds only itself on hmipserver (a worker pool per listener) - no stall, but a stack trace per second in the journal until its connection ends. |
| `GET /usb` **`system:read`** | The USB devices read out of `/sys/bus/usb/devices` (no `usbutils` in the image; nothing shells out, no helper): `{devices: [{path, parent, bus, dev, vendor, product, vendor_name?, product_name?, manufacturer?, serial?, speed?, class, hub, driver?, nodes: [], radio: null | {device_node, device_type, protocols: []}}]}`. `path` is the sysfs name and the topology (`1-1.3`), `parent` the hub it hangs on (`""` for a root hub), `nodes` what it produced under `/dev` (tty, hidraw, block, raw-uart), `driver` what claimed its interfaces. `radio` is the join against `/sys/class/raw-uart/*/device_type` (`<product>@usb-<controller>-<port path>`: the port path is the device's devpath, the controller the bus's root-hub serial) with the stacks `/var/hm_mode` gave the node. Four built-in names for the eQ-3 and HB-RF-USB ids; everything else shows what the device says. Role `user`. |
| `GET /usb/storage` **`system:read`** | The USB sticks mounted at `/media/usb1`…`8` (the fork's `occu-usb-mount@` units run upstream's usbmount in the host's namespace; the first stick is linked as `/media/usb0`; FAT, exFAT and NTFS mounted root:`usbstorage` with umask 0007 - occulited and the addons that declare the group read and write them, openccu-lite B-259): `{sticks: [{mount, path, linked, device, fstype, label?, label_id?, vendor?, model?, read_only?, total_bytes, free_bytes}]}` from `/proc/mounts`, udev's database (`/run/udev/data/b<maj>:<min>`: `ID_FS_LABEL_ENC` unescaped as `label`, `ID_FS_LABEL` as `label_id` - the form the journal's stick target names a stick by - `ID_VENDOR`, `ID_MODEL`) and `statfs`; `read_only` from the stick's own filesystem (mountinfo's super options — occulited's namespace sees `/media` read-only). `path` is what a target should use: `/media/usb0` for the linked stick, the mount point otherwise. The Backup page's directory form lists them as buttons that fill in `<path>/backup`; a read-only one is not offered. A USB directory target (under `/media/`, not `/media/net/`) whose stick is not there is **`no-medium`** (the follow-up): the nightly run skips it before anything is made — its `last.json` says `no-medium`, step `medium` — the other targets get their copy and the create unit succeeds; when it is the only target, no backup is made at all. The test and the retry's probe answer `no-medium` the same way, and `backup-delivery` names the target. |
| `GET /storage/usb` **`system:read`**, `POST /storage/usb/{disk}/format`, `POST /storage/usb/{disk}/eject` **`system:write`** | System → Storage (phase 1). `GET` → `{disks: [{name, vendor, model, serial, size_bytes, removable, loop?, partitions: [{name, fstype, label, label_id, mount, read_only, size_bytes, total_bytes, free_bytes}], uses: [{kind: journal\|backup, name, id}]}], filesystems: [exfat, ext4]}` — every whole disk on the USB bus (sysfs), mounted or not; `uses` the journal's `usb:<label>/` target by label and a backup directory target by the mount its path lies on; `filesystems` what the image can make. `format {fs: exfat\|ext4, label}` (label required: exFAT ≤ 15, ext4 ≤ 16 of letters, digits, `-`, `_`; 422 `invalid`): the helper (operation `usbformat`) checks the disk — a whole disk on the USB bus, no filesystem of it mounted outside `/media`, no swap; loop devices only with the test marker `/usr/local/etc/occulite/storage-test-loop` — stops its `occu-usb-mount@` units (the journal's last copy, the unmount), writes one MBR partition (type 07 for exFAT, 83 for ext4) with `sfdisk --wipe always`, runs `mkfs.exfat -n <label>` (exfat-utils) or `mkfs.ext4 -F -q -m 0 -L <label>` and starts the partition's mount unit again; the answer is the list with `formatted`. `eject`: the same stop and unmount (`usbeject`), then the list with `ejected`. 404 for a disk that is not a USB stick, 409 `busy` while a backup is written to it, 422 `refused` when the helper refuses, 501 without the helper; a journal line with who formatted which stick (model, serial, size) to what. |
| `GET /storage/shares` **`system:read`**, `POST /storage/shares`, `PUT`/`DELETE /storage/shares/{name}`, `POST /storage/shares/{name}/test`, `…/mount`, `…/unmount` **`system:write`** | System → Storage's network shares (phase 2). `GET` → `{container, kinds: {nfs, cifs}, shares: [Share]}` — `kinds` "" when the kind works here, `container` or `tool` (mount.cifs / mount.nfs missing; NFS by an address works without mount.nfs) when not. **`Share`:** `{id, kind: nfs\|cifs, server, path, version, seal?, read_only, user?, domain?, has_password, created, where, state: {state, detail?, checked_at?, next_retry_at?, mounted, source?, options?, free_bytes?, total_bytes?, unsupported?}}` — `id` is the name (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]{0,15}$`: the mount point `/media/net/<id>`, the units `media-net-<id>.{mount,automount}` and the location id `share:<id>`); `path` the NFS export (absolute) or the SMB share (optionally with a folder in it); `version` NFS `""`/`4.2`/`4.1`/`4`/`3`, SMB `""` (3.0 or newer)/`3.1.1`/`3.0`; `read_only` mounts `ro` (`nosuid,nodev,noexec` always); SMB's `password` is write-only (`shares/<id>/cifs.cred`, 0600; an edit without it keeps it). `state.state`: `mounted` (with the free space, read through a 5 s probe), `idle` (the automount armed, nothing mounted — the normal state), the last check's failure (`unreachable`, `auth-failed`, `stale`, `read-only`, `full`, `error`, with `detail` in the mount's own words and the next retry: 1, 2, 5, 15, 30 min), or `unsupported`. `POST` takes a `Share` with its `id` (201 `{share, apply_error?}`; 409 `exists` for a taken name, also a task-86 mount target's id; 400 `invalid`); `PUT` changes everything but the name and the kind (400 for a rename). `test`: mounts it (the first access through the automount, ≤ 30 s), writes, reads back and deletes a 1 MiB probe file as root through the helper's write test — a read-only share is mounted and listed instead (`state: readable`) — and unmounts it again when it was not mounted before → `{ok, state: writable\|readable\|unreadable\|read-only\|full\|unreachable\|auth-failed\|stale\|error, step, error?, free_bytes, total_bytes, write_mbps?, fs_type?, read_only?}`. after a successful write the daemon, as its own user (`occulite`), lists the share's root (and a folder one level down) and opens a few files there: a permission error answers `ok: true, state: unreadable, step: read-back` with the error — root can write, and the system cannot read what root wrote (an NFS export that maps root to nobody: map root to root, TrueNAS *Maproot User* root, or all users to one account, *Mapall User*); the journal's copies there could not be shown. `mount`, `unmount` → the `Share`. `DELETE` stops and removes the units and the credentials — never a file on the share. The shares' units are rendered again at every start of occulited (`/run` is empty after a boot); a watchdog checks every minute whether a mounted share's **server** takes a TCP connection (NFS 2049, SMB 445; 5 s) and unmounts a share whose server does not answer (the next use mounts it again) — it never touches the mount point, whose every path walk resets the automount's idle timer, so an unused share unmounts after its 5 idle minutes; the Status page's journal warnings read the setting without measuring a share target either. A page that shows a mounted share's free space (Storage, the location picker, the Journal panel) does measure it while it is open. |
| `GET /storage/locations?use=journal\|backup\|store`, `GET /storage/dirs?use=…&location=…&prefix=…` **`system:read`** | Phases 3–4: the one location picker's two reads. A **location** is stored as an id and a folder, never a mount path — `userfs:<folder>` (`/usr/local/<folder>`), `usb:<label>/<folder>` (the stick whose `ID_FS_LABEL` is `<label>`, wherever it is mounted), `share:<name>/<folder>` (`/media/net/<name>/<folder>`; the share's root when the folder is empty); a folder is up to four levels of letters, digits, `. _ -`, none hidden, no `..`. **`locations`** → `{use, locations: [{id: "userfs"\|"usb:<label>"\|"share:<name>", kind, name, detail?, state, state_detail?, free_bytes?, total_bytes?, read_only?, uses: [{kind: journal\|backup\|store, name?, id?, folder?}], allowed, code?, reason?, userfs_prefix?, fixed?, default?}]}` — the userfs (`state: present`), every mounted stick (a stick without a label is `id: "usb:"`, never allowed, `code: no-label`), every share (its `state` as `GET /storage/shares` has it, free space while mounted); `uses` is the journal's target, the backup targets (a directory target by its location, or an older path by the stick it lies on; a share target by its share) and the database's location. `allowed`/`code`/`reason` are the use's rule: the **journal** takes the userfs (`fixed` to `var/log/journal`), a stick or a share (`code: ram-sync-only`); **backup** the userfs below `backup` (`code: lost-with-system`), a stick or a share (SFTP is a target kind of its own); the **database** (`store`) the userfs below `etc/occulite` only — a stick `code: usb-not-yet`, a share never (`code: no-share-for-store`: bbolt maps its file into memory and needs locking and fsync that NFS and SMB do not guarantee). Any read-only location is `code: read-only`. `default` is the folder suggested for a new choice. **`dirs`** → `{dirs: [folder…], exists}`: the directories under the prefix's parent whose names begin with its last part (case-insensitive), as folders of the location, sorted, at most 50 — directory names only (no file names, no hidden ones, no `lost+found`), no link followed (a parent that is a link answers nothing), four levels at most; on the userfs only below the use's part (above it the answer is that part alone); a share is read through a probe that may mount it (≤ 30 s; `503 stale` when it does not answer); 404 for a stick that is not plugged in or a share that does not exist; `exists` whether the prefix itself is a folder there — a prefix ending in `/` names its folder (the picker says "a new folder: made when first used" otherwise). only a folder that does not exist yet (or a link on the way) answers the empty list; a share that is not mounted after the access answers **`502`** with its mount unit's reason (`auth-failed`, `unreachable`), a folder the system may not read `502 read-only`, any other error `502` with its state (`error` = the state, `detail: {state, error}`); the picker shows the reason at the folder field instead of the new-folder hint. |
| `GET /storage` **`system:read`** | Role `user`: the Status page's storage health panel. `{verdict: good\|watch\|replace, reasons: [Reason], devices: [Device], checked, kernel_log, health_source: device\|host, log_files: [LogFile], log_files_more?}`. A `Device` is a whole disk of `/sys/block` — `mmcblk*`, `sd*`, `nvme*n*`, `vd*`; partitions, an eMMC's boot areas and RPMB, loop, ram and zram are not disks, and a disk of size 0 (an empty card reader) is left out: `{name, kind: sd\|emmc\|usb\|sata\|nvme\|virtio\|other, virtual?, vendor?, vendor_id?, model?, capacity_bytes, manufactured?, age_months?, mounts: [], emmc?: {life_time_a, life_time_b, pre_eol?: normal\|warning\|urgent}, smart?: {available, passed?, message?, model?, power_on_hours?, wear_percent?, reallocated_sectors?, pending_sectors?, media_errors?, temperature_c?, read}, filesystems: [{name, mounts, errors, first_error?, last_error?, lifetime_write_bytes}], io_errors, last_io_error?, last_io_error_at?, writes: {since_boot_bytes, per_day_bytes?, per_day_days?, per_day_basis?: samples\|boot}, verdict, reasons}`. **`kind`** is the MMC card's `type` (`SD`, `MMC`), the name (`nvme`, `vd`), or for an `sd*` disk its sysfs path (`/virtio`, then `/usb`, then `/ata`); **`virtual`** marks virtio and a hypervisor's model on another bus (QEMU, VBOX, VMware, Virtual disk, Msft Virtual) — the OVA's disk is `sda` behind virtio-scsi. **`vendor`**: an SD card's or eMMC's CID manufacturer id (`vendor_id`, `0x000003`) in a short table of the documented ones (SanDisk, Samsung, Kingston, Transcend, …; SD cards and eMMC number them differently), a SCSI disk's sysfs vendor except libata's `ATA`. **`manufactured`** is the CID date as `YYYY-MM`, `age_months` counted from it. **`mounts`** are the mount points in this process's mount namespace whose mount root is `/` (the unit's bind mounts of `/usr/local/etc/occulite` and `/var/log/journal` are directories of a filesystem already listed). **`emmc`** is the raw `life_time` (types A and B: `0x01`…`0x0A` = up to 10…100 % of the rated life used, `0x0B` past it, 0 undefined) and `pre_eol_info`; a plain SD card has neither file. **SMART** is read for a `usb`, `sata`, `nvme` or `other` disk that is not virtual, through the privilege helper's `smartctl` operation (exactly `smartctl -j -H -A -i /dev/<disk>`, [security.md](https://github.com/hobbyquaker/openccu-lite/blob/main/docs/security.md)), at most once an hour per disk and cached, failures included; a disk without SMART says `available: false`, with smartctl's own `message` when it gave one. **`health_source`** says who watches the disks' health: `device`, the system itself, or `host` — a container (`/run/systemd/container`, `container=` of PID 1, `PLATFORM=lxc\|oci`), the VM product (`PLATFORM=ova`), a system whose disks are all `virtual`, or one where the helper answered that `smartctl` is not installed (only the hardware products build smartmontools; that answer is *not available*, neither a refusal nor a failure, and it is not asked again). With `host` no SMART is read, no disk carries `smart`, nothing about a missing SMART becomes a reason, and the verdict comes from the other facts; the panel says *Disk health is monitored by the host* in one line. A USB stick or an SD card on hardware stays `device`, with its `available: false` or its card facts. `wear_percent` is NVMe's *percentage used*, or 100 minus the normalized value of a SATA SSD's remaining-life attribute (231 `SSD_Life_Left`, 233 `Media_Wearout_Indicator`, 177 `Wear_Leveling_Count`, 202 `Percent_Lifetime_Remain`, 169 `Remaining_Lifetime_Perc`, matched by id and name). **`filesystems`** are the disk's mounted ext4 filesystems from `/sys/fs/ext4/<partition>/` (`errors_count` with its first and last time survives reboots and fsck; `lifetime_write_kbytes` counts since the filesystem was made). **`io_errors`** counts the kernel's storage error messages of this boot — `journalctl -k -b -p 3` (the unit is in `systemd-journal`), `dmesg` on busybox, read at most every five minutes — attributed through the disk, a partition or the MMC host the card hangs on: `I/O error, dev X`, `critical medium`/`critical target`/`timeout error, dev X`, `Buffer I/O error on dev X`, `mmcblkN: error …`/`recovery failed`/`timed out`, `mmcN: … timeout`/`error -N`/`never left busy`/`CRC`; a message about anything else (a loop device, the Pi's SDIO wifi on `mmc1`) counts nowhere, and `kernel_log: false` says the count is unknown, not zero. **`writes`**: `since_boot_bytes` is field 7 of `/sys/block/<disk>/stat` × 512; `per_day_bytes` comes from a daily sample in `<state-dir>/storage-writes.json` (`{devices: {<disk>: {id, samples: [{t, boot, boot_kb, lifetime_kb}]}}}`: a sample when the newest is 23 h old, checked at start and hourly; kept 35 days; another card in the slot starts a new series), over the samples of the last 28 days plus the current reading — within one boot the disk's counter, across a reboot the ext4 lifetime counter (`per_day_basis: samples`, `per_day_days` the span) — or, before there is a day of samples, since the boot once the system has been up an hour (`boot`). A `Reason` is `{level, code, device, count?, percent?, filesystem?, years?, bytes_per_day?}`; a device's `reasons` and the report's are the ones of its verdict's level. The verdict rules are in *Storage health: the verdict* below. **`log_files`** is a hint beside the verdict and takes no part in it: the files written beside the journal, the largest first, at most 20 (`log_files_more` counts the rest) — `{path, size, userfs, addon?, growing, grown_bytes?}`. The rule is the fork's `scripts/lite-log-inventory.sh`: every regular file under `/var/log` outside the journal, and every `*.log`, `*.log.N`, `*.log.NN` under `/run` and `/usr/local`; left out are the journal's directories, `node_modules`, npm's `_cacache` and `/usr/local/tmp/recovery-*.log` (the recovery's install log, the documented exception). `/tmp` is not looked at: occulited's unit has `PrivateTmp`. `userfs` is a path under `/usr/local` (the card; otherwise RAM); `addon` the id of a path under `/usr/local/addons/<id>/`; `growing` a file longer than at the previous scan, by `grown_bytes`. The scan runs at most every 10 minutes, at most 8 levels deep and 200 000 entries. **A directory occulited cannot open** (RedMatic's `var/npm-cache/_logs` is `drwx------ addon-redmatic`) is listed through the privilege helper's `listlogs` operation ([security.md](https://github.com/hobbyquaker/openccu-lite/blob/main/docs/security.md)): the paths, sizes and times of its log files, never their content, only under `/usr/local/addons/` and `/var/log/` (every file only under `/var/log/`), at most 32 such directories a scan; the answer is taken under the rule above, and a refused or failed listing leaves the directory out. 501 `unsupported` without the service. |
| `GET /radio/firmware` **`power`** | **Admin** (the GET too): `{modules: [{protocols, device, device_node, device_type?, family?, dir?, running_version, files: [{name, path, source: "shipped"\|"uploaded", version?, size, sha256, direction: "upgrade"\|"downgrade"\|"same"\|"unknown"}], newest?, verdict, note?, flashable}], force_no_update, forced_version, firmware_staged, radio_busy, radio_busy_interface?, upload_dir, running: Attempt\|null, last: Attempt\|null}`. One module per device node of `/var/hm_mode` (a dual module lists both protocols). `family` is `hmip` (RPI-RF-MOD, HmIP-RFUSB: `hmip-copro-update.jar`) or `legacy` (HM-MOD-RPI-PCB: `eq3configcmd update-coprocessor`); `verdict` is `up-to-date`, `newer-available`, `older-only`, `no-file`, `unknown` or `skipped` (HMIP-RFUSB-TK, HM-CFG-USB-2; `note` says why). Files come from `/firmware/<dir>/` (the version from the name, or from the directory's `fwmap` for the unversioned legacy file) and from `/usr/local/etc/config/radio-firmware/<dir>/`. `running_version` is `/var/hm_mode`'s, or the version read after the last successful flash when the snapshot still carries the old one (`note` says so). An `Attempt` is `{module, device_node, file, file_version, started, finished?, ok, error?, before?, after?, exit, run_id?}`. **Its lines are in the journal** - the phases occulited went through, then the flasher's output: one entry per line with `OCCULITE_RUN=radio-firmware`, `OCCULITE_RUN_ID=<run_id>`, `OCCULITE_RUN_MODULE`, `OCCULITE_RUN_FILE`, identifier `radio-firmware`, priority `err` for a failure and `warning` for a warning; the flasher's output carries `OCCULITE_RUN_SOURCE=flasher`, a version read's `version-read`. The page reads them with `GET /log?run=<run_id>`. `running.json` and `last.json` keep the attempt without lines; a file of an older binary that kept them has its lines written into the journal at the next start, under a run id of the attempt's start, and is written again without them. Off a systemd system (or with `--root`) the lines go to occulited's own log with `run` and `run_id` as attributes. verdict `unusable` - an HmIP-RFUSB the radio detection found on a raw-uart node (`/run/occulite/radio/modules.json`, device_type `…HmIP-RFUSB@…`, not the TK) whose probe did not answer, on a node no role uses (`/var/hm_mode` does not name it): listed with `running_version` empty, the newest file and `flashable`; its flash goes on past the version it cannot read before (the attempt's `unusable: true`), as S48 did. A stick that answered and is merely not chosen is not listed. |
| `POST /radio/firmware/upload?module=<device>[&name=]` **`power`** | Multipart `file` (its name from the part) or a raw body with `?name=`. `.eq3` only, the name in the module's own pattern (`dualcopro_update_blhmip-<version>.eq3`, `dualcopro_si1002_update_blhm-<version>.eq3`) so the version is known, at most 4 MiB and ten uploaded files per module; staged as the daemon's user and moved into place by the helper (the directory is inside its `/usr/local/etc/config/` prefix), with a `.nobackup` beside it so the nightly backup skips the images. Answers the `FirmwareFile`; 422 `firmware-rejected` with the reason. |
| `DELETE /radio/firmware/{module}/{name}` **`power`** | Removes an uploaded file; a shipped one is refused (422). |
| `POST /radio/firmware/flash` **`power`** | `{module, file}` → 202 with the status, the run detached; the page polls `GET /radio/firmware` while `running` is set. The run: stop hmipserver, rfd, multimacd (the reverse of the boot order, the ones that run; hs485d holds no raw UART and is left alone) → wait until `/dev/mmd_*` are gone and the raw-uart's `open_count` is 0 → read the version (`detect_radio_module` field 6, or `eq3configcmd update-coprocessor -c -v`) → flash through the helper's `flashcopro` operation (the exact S48 invocation, 120 s; the legacy family's directory under `/run/occulite`, synthetic `fwmap` and forced 240 s retry) — **as the module's user, not root**: on a systemd system the helper runs the flasher in a transient unit (`systemd-run --wait --pipe`) with `User=multimacd`, the node's resource group (`raw-uart`) and `lock`, `DevicePolicy=closed` with that one node, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `PrivateTmp=`, no capabilities, `RuntimeMaxSec=` at the step's timeout; off a systemd system, or on a node without a resource group (root:root), as root with a line in the attempt's output saying so → read again (a node that vanished is re-scanned by product string for 30 s) → `systemctl restart occu-init-rf-hardware.service` so `/var/hm_mode` is rewritten → start multimacd, rfd, hmipserver. The daemons come back on failure too. **From before the stop until those starts are done the radio units refuse every start from outside the flash** (openccu-lite B-308): the gate of the connection change (`/run/occulite/radio/changing`, its deadline the flash's 15 minutes and one more), each unit let through right before the flash starts it, the marker removed afterwards and on every way out (and at occulited's next start after an interrupted flash); a radio unit the flash did not stop whose start the gate skipped meanwhile is started afterwards when the plan runs it. Refused (422; 409 `flash-refused` for a run in flight): a running flash or radio connection change, a busy radio (≥ 80 % duty cycle), `/etc/config/force-no-coprocessor-update`, a staged system update, a file that is not the module's or carries no version. A flash in flight when occulited restarts is closed as failed at the next start and the daemons started. |
| `GET /services` **`system:read`** | Managed services (`rfd`, `hs485d`, `hmipserver`, …) and addon services with running state and pid (a generated `addon-<id>` unit carries the addon's **`images`** as `GET /addons` does, openccu-lite task 100); `systemd: true` when the system runs systemd. Without systemd (the development mode) the list is empty; with it: `systemctl list-units`, where `kind: "addon"` marks the generated `addon-<name>` units and units an addon ships under `/usr/local`, and `enabled` is the verdict on the unit file state (false for `disabled`, `masked`, `masked-runtime`, true for everything else). **`failed`** (systemd's `ActiveState` is `failed`) and **`skipped`** (an inactive unit a condition kept from starting: `Result=exec-condition`, or `ConditionResult=no` with a condition check on record, `ConditionTimestampMonotonic` > 0 — a unit that was never started says `ConditionResult=no` too) come from the same one `systemctl show` and are absent when false; the Services page marks only `failed` red. **`note`** is the radio plan's reason on a skipped interface daemon (`multimacd`, `rfd`, `hmipserver`, `hs485d`, `hmlangw`), e.g. *off by choice: no local radio, and no LAN gateway is configured*, so the page says why it is off. **`unit_file_state`** (systemd only) is that state raw: `enabled`, `enabled-runtime`, `alias`, `indirect`, `generated` (every `addon-<name>` unit), `static` (no `[Install]` section — `systemctl enable`/`disable` refuse it), `disabled`, `masked`, `masked-runtime` (what `disable` below does). **`pid`** is systemd's `MainPID`; a running unit without one — a `Type=oneshot` unit whose script started a daemon, which is every generated addon unit — gets the **leader of its cgroup** instead: the pids in `/sys/fs/cgroup<ControlGroup>/cgroup.procs` (a file read, no helper call; `ControlGroup` comes with the one `systemctl show` for all units), and of those the process whose parent is not in the cgroup, the oldest by start time when there are several. With it come **`pids_more`** (how many other processes the cgroup holds, absent when 0) and **`procs: [{pid, cmd}]`** (all of them, the leader first, `cmd` from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` with the NULs as spaces, at most 32 entries). A completed oneshot (`oneshot: true`) has no pid, as before. **`stray: true`** marks an addon whose unit has nothing running while processes of the addon (its executable, `argv[0]` or an interpreter's script under `/usr/local/addons/<id>/`, or its user `addon-<id>`; a command line that only names a file there - an ssh `md5sum`, a `tail -f` - is not one, occulited B-30) run in another cgroup — started by an installer or by hand; `running` is true, and `pid`, `pids_more` and `procs` then describe those processes outside the unit, so a pid never points into the wrong cgroup unannounced. `procs` and `pids_more` are absent where the pid is a `MainPID`. A generated `addon-<id>` unit also carries its confinement: `policy_mode` (`root`/`confined`), `policy_source` and `undeclared`, the same three values as `GET /addons/{id}/policy`, so the page can say what an addon runs as and whether anyone declared what it needs without asking per addon. `GET /addons` carries them too. **`may_mount`** and **`remount_refused`** (absent when false; on `GET /addons` and `GET /addons/{id}/policy` too): no root addon unit has `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` — occulited's policy drop-in for a root addon is `[Service]` `CapabilityBoundingSet=~CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, rendered at every start of occulited and installed by the generator at the next boot — so `may_mount` marks the root addon whose declaration (the stored block or its stored manifest) names `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` and keeps the full set (its drop-in then has no `[Service]` section), and `remount_refused` marks an addon whose unit output in the current boot holds a refused `mount … permission denied` (one `journalctl -b -g` for all addons, cached 30 s): a hint that the addon still writes the CCU way and its writes went into the writable extension directory (`GET /radio/device-descriptions`), not an error. **`poll_seconds`** is how long the Services page waits between two polls: eight times the recent cost of the listing (each listing moves the average by a third), at least 5 and at most 30 s; the page times the next poll from the end of the last one and still does not poll from a hidden tab. **On systemd the units' `systemctl show` is cached**: systemd's cost is per unit, not per property (the Charly, 61 units: 0.98 s for every property, 0.94 s for `UnitFileState` alone), so a listing runs `list-units` and shows only the units whose `active`/`sub` changed since the last listing, whose `MainPID` is no longer alive (a restart between two polls), or that a systemctl command of occulited named after `--` (start, stop, restart, mask, enable, …; a command without unit names, `daemon-reload`, means every unit), and every unit after five minutes or when the clock went back; a unit systemctl printed no block for is asked again next time. `cpu_seconds`, `memory_bytes` and the tasks behind the oneshot verdict come from the unit's cgroup at every listing — `cpu.stat` `usage_usec`, `memory.current`, `pids.current`, the files systemd reads for them; a file that is not there is `[not set]` as in systemd (neither measured system has the memory controller on its units, so `memory_bytes` is absent there before and after), and a cgroup that is gone holds no process. A system without the unified cgroup tree (`/sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers`) shows its active units at every listing. `GET /timers`' `UnitFileState` is cached the same way. Measured read-only: the old poll's commands took 1.01 s on the Charly and 0.48 s on the Pi 4, the new steady poll's (`list-units` and the cgroup files) 0.10 s and 0.06 s. **`starting: true`** marks a unit systemd is starting — `active` is `activating` in `list-units` and the sub-state is not `auto-restart` (a crash loop waiting for its next try is not a start) — so it is neither `running` nor stopped: hmipserver's JVM for about 40 s after the web UI is up at boot, an addon whose start script still runs. **`starting_s`** is how long, in whole seconds, from `InactiveExitTimestampMonotonic` (part of the one `systemctl show`, refreshed by the cache when the state changes) against `/proc/uptime`, the monotonic rule; absent when systemd gave no stamp. Both are absent when false. The Services page writes *Starting · 23 s* with the accent's dot and counts the seconds on between two polls. **`ended: true`** marks an `addon-<id>` unit whose addon keeps a daemon — its manifest's `runtime.daemon` (the stored block or the declared one), or learned: once occulited has listed the unit active with a process in its cgroup (not stray, not starting) it notes the addon in `<state-dir>/addon-daemons.json`, forgotten at the addon's uninstall and at an install that touches it — while the unit is a finished oneshot (`oneshot: true`, not failed, result `success`): the daemon ended and the unit still says `active (exited)`. `running` is then false (the pages offer *Start*); **`ended_at`** is the time of the unit's last journal line of this boot and **`ended_log`** those last three lines (`journalctl -b -u … -n 3`, a binary message skipped, 300 characters each, cached 30 s per unit). A prepare-only addon — never seen with a process, not declared — stays `oneshot` (*Completed*). The pages show it red as *Exited* / *Beendet*; `GET /addons` carries the same three fields. Only without systemd (the development mode) is nothing listed at all. |
| `POST /services/{id}/{action}` **`system:write`** | `action` = `start`, `stop` or `restart`: transient control (`systemctl`; without systemd, the development mode, every action is 422). **`start` on an `addon-<id>` unit that is an active (exited) oneshot with no task left** — an addon whose daemon ended, or one that only prepared things — is a `systemctl restart`: a start of an active unit does nothing. **`restart` on a stray addon** (systemd: `addon-<id>`, its unit active with no task of its own or not active, and processes of the addon in another cgroup) is not a plain `systemctl restart`, which would restart the empty unit and leave the daemon where it is: the unit is stopped, the addon's processes outside the unit are stopped by pid (TERM, five seconds, KILL, through the helper's `kill`; only processes whose command line is under `/usr/local/addons/<id>/`), and the unit is started; `output` then says how many were stopped. A unit with tasks of its own gets the plain restart even when a process of the addon runs elsewhere (a CGI, a cron job) — that one is not touched. On systemd also `enable` and `disable`, which switch the unit off and on **across a boot without writing to the rootfs**: `disable` is `systemctl --runtime mask --now`, which overrides whatever the image ships and stops the unit; `enable` is `--runtime unmask` plus a start, and a `--runtime enable` as well when the image does not ship that unit enabled. A runtime mask lives in `/run` and is gone after a boot, so occulited keeps the switched-off (and runtime-enabled) units in `<state-dir>/unit-switch.json` on the userfs and applies them again at every start; occulited's own unit is never masked by that replay, because the daemon would stop itself at every boot. What this does **not** do is edit the shipped enablement in `/etc/systemd/system/<target>.wants/` — the rootfs is read-only, `systemctl enable/disable` answers *"Read-only file system"* there, and not editing it is what lets a firmware update bring its new units enabled by default. `GET /services` reports such a unit as `enabled: false` (`systemctl is-enabled` says `masked-runtime`). Without systemd every action is 422. `id` may name a `.timer`: a shipped timer is switched with the same runtime mask. An **own timer** `local-<name>.timer` is not — its unit file sits at the very path a runtime mask would take, and systemctl refuses to mask over a file: `disable` is `systemctl --runtime disable --now` plus a marker beside the stored files, `enable` is `--runtime enable --now` and removes the marker, and the replay at start honours the marker (nothing goes into `unit-switch.json`). `enable`/`disable` on an own timer's `local-<name>.service` is 422 — the timer is its switch; `start`, `stop` and `restart` work on it as on any unit. The UI warns before stopping an interface. `stop` and `disable` of the units the web interface runs through - `lighttpd`, `occulited`, `occulited-helper` - answer 409 `cuts-ui` (restart them, or stop them over ssh); `GET /services` marks them `ui: true`, and `lighttpd` is in the `core` category with the daemons and occulited, so the page shows it with system services hidden. **A `start` or `restart` of an `addon-<id>` unit waits while a radio connection change or a coprocessor flash runs** (openccu-lite B-307; also `try-restart`, `reload-or-restart`): the radio daemons are stopped on purpose then, and the addon would come up without its interfaces (an addon unit never starts them: openccu-lite B-308). A request that ends while it waits is answered **503 `radio-busy`** and nothing is done. The same wait holds for `POST /addons/{id}/enable` and `POST /addonctl`, and the addon supervisor restarts no addon meanwhile (it counts an ended daemon afresh once the change is done). |
| `POST /addonctl` *(open)* | `{action: start\|stop\|restart}` with `Authorization: Bearer <addon control token>` — registered without a scope: the addon control token is the credential, and the handler checks it. The one route an addon's own rc.d wrapper may call as the addon's user: it acts on **`addon-<id>.service` of the addon the token belongs to** and on nothing else — the token is not a session and opens no other route. Tokens are minted at every start of occulited and after an install or a policy change, one file per addon at `/run/occulite/addon-tokens/<id>`, mode 0600, owned by the addon's user (root for a root addon). The path is outside the session check; the handler checks the token. 401 without or with an unknown token, 422 for another action, 501 off the systemd product. |
| `GET /journal`, `PUT /journal` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | And 216 (modes `ram`, `ram-sync` and `persistent` on the userfs; `ram-sync` to a USB stick): `{storage: ""\|"ram"\|"ram-sync"\|"persistent", target: "userfs"\|"usb:<label>/<dir>", target_label?, target_dir?, target_path?, runtime_max_use, system_max_use, system_max_file, rate_limit_burst, sync_interval, target_max_use, target_max_age, persist: ""\|"1"\|"0", platform, default_storage: "ram"\|"persistent", default_persistent, persistent, effective: "ram"\|"ram-sync"\|"persistent", reboot_pending, fallback?, ram_usage, target_usage, target_free, target_ok, usage, last_sync, last_sync_result?, last_sync_copied, last_sync_error?, last_sync_reason?, next_sync}` — `/etc/config/journal` on the userfs, the file `lite-journal-persist` reads at boot. **The setting:** `storage` empty means the product's default (`default_storage`: `persistent` on `ova`, `oci` and `lxc`, `ram` everywhere else; `default_persistent` follows it). A valid `STORAGE=` wins; otherwise `PERSIST=1` reads as `persistent` and `PERSIST=0` as `ram`. `persist` is **deprecated**, derived from `storage` (`1` persistent, `0` ram and ram-sync, `""`). `target` is `userfs` (`/usr/local/var/log/journal`), for ram-sync only a network share as `share:<name>/<dir>` (below), or, for ram-sync only, a USB stick as `usb:<label>/<dir>`: `<label>` is udev's `ID_FS_LABEL` (`GET /usb/storage`'s `label_id`: letters and digits of any script and `# + - . : = @ _`, at most 64), `<dir>` up to four levels of letters, digits, `. _ -` (no `..`, no hidden part); `target_label` and `target_dir` are its halves, `target_path` where the copies go now (`/usr/local/var/log/journal`, or `<mount>/<dir>` while the stick with that label is mounted at `/media/usb1`…`8`, absent while it is not). With a stick nothing is mounted over `/var/log/journal`: journald stays in RAM, the fork's `lite-journal-sync` copies to the stick at the interval, early, at `POST /journal/sync`, when `occu-usb-mount@` mounts it (reason `plug`, at once - so a boot without the stick reaches it when it comes) and before that unit unmounts it (`unplug`; `shutdown` while the system goes down, after `occu-journal-sync`'s own last copy); without the stick a copy is skipped. `/log`, `/log/download`, `/boots` and the boot starts then read the RAM journal and the copies on the stick as `journalctl --file` arguments, merged by time (following, `/log/stream`, stays on RAM); an addon's last lines, the ssh logins and the kernel's errors of this boot read the same way; `runtime_max_use` is journald's RuntimeMaxUse, the RAM limit; the sizes are journald's (`32M`), the burst a count. **ram-sync**: journald writes RAM only (`Storage=volatile` in its runtime drop-in) and `occu-journal-sync.service` copies the closed journal files to `/usr/local/var/log/journal` every `sync_interval` (`30min`, `6h`, `1d`: 15 minutes to 7 days, empty = 6h), **early** when the files under `/run/log/journal` take 80 % of the RAM limit (`runtime_max_use`, or journald's default of 10 % of `/run`'s filesystem, at most 4G; at most one copy every 15 minutes), and when it stops — at shutdown, at every reboot, and at `POST /journal/sync`. The userfs directory is bind-mounted over `/var/log/journal` as in persistent, so `/log`, `/log/stream`, `/log/download` and `/boots` read the copies and RAM together, earlier boots included. `target_max_use` (a size, empty = 64M) and `target_max_age` (`12h`, `30d`, `2w`; empty = none) trim the copies (`journalctl -D … --vacuum-size`/`--vacuum-time`). **In effect now:** `persistent` is whether `/var/log/journal` is mounted from the userfs (in persistent and in ram-sync); `effective` is `ram` without that mount, `ram-sync` with it while journal files are in `/run/log/journal` (the files, not the directory, which journald leaves behind after the flush), and `persistent` once journald has flushed onto it; with a stick target it is `ram-sync` while the stick is plugged in and journald writes RAM, `ram` otherwise; `reboot_pending` is true when the setting (or the default) says `ram` while the mount is still in place — journald still on the userfs, or the copies still readable; `fallback` is the boot script's reason from `/run/occu-journal/fallback` when the chosen ram-sync or persistent could not be set up and nothing is mounted (only once the script has tried) - with a stick target, while the stick is not plugged in; `ram_usage` is the space the files under `/run/log/journal` take, `target_usage` those under `/var/log/journal` when mounted, otherwise under `/usr/local/var/log/journal` — **allocated blocks** (st_blocks × 512), as `journalctl --disk-usage` and journald's limits count them (not the preallocated file lengths, which are about twice the use); `target_free` is statfs's available bytes on `/usr/local`; with a stick target the figures are the stick's directory and filesystem, `null` while it is not plugged in. The three figures are `null` when they cannot be measured (a directory that exists but cannot be listed, a failed statfs) and `0` when there is nothing. `target_ok` (a stick: plugged in and not read-only): `/usr/local` is its own mount (in a container, any mount holding it) on a filesystem whose super options are not `ro` — read from mountinfo, since occulited's own namespace sees it read-only. `usage` is `journalctl --disk-usage`. `last_sync` (RFC 3339, or `null` before any copy), `last_sync_result` (`ok`, `failed`), `last_sync_copied`, `last_sync_error` and `last_sync_reason` (`interval`, `early`, `shutdown`, `manual`, `plug`, `unplug`; left out when the script did not say) come from `/run/occu-journal/sync.state` — before the first copy of this boot from `.occu-sync.state` beside the copies (`/usr/local/var/log/journal/`, or the stick's directory), which the script also writes, so the previous boot's last copy (usually its copy at shutdown) is shown; `next_sync` from `sync.next` while ram-sync is in effect, `null` otherwise — computed at every read from `sync.due` (the same time on `/proc/uptime`) when the script writes one, so a clock step is followed at once; the script writes both only once `occu-clock-valid`'s gate has passed (`/run/occulite/clock-state`; in a container at once; after 240 s of uptime at the latest), so a boot without a real-time clock never shows a pre-NTP date. **`PUT`** takes `storage`, `target`, `runtime_max_use`, the three sizes, `sync_interval`, `target_max_use` and `target_max_age`; it writes `STORAGE`, `TARGET`, `SYNC_INTERVAL`, `TARGET_MAX_USE` and `TARGET_MAX_AGE` and keeps `PERSIST` in step for an older image (`persistent` → `1`, `ram` and `ram-sync` → `0`, empty → no line); a cleared value takes its line away, other lines stay. The legacy `persist` is honoured only when the body has no `storage` key at all. Then it restarts `occu-persist.service` and answers the GET view: a switch **to** persistent mounts and flushes at once; a switch **to** ram-sync mounts, restarts journald into RAM and starts the copies at once; a switch **to** RAM applies at the next boot (the mount stays; from ram-sync the copies stop at once, after a last one); the sizes and the interval apply at once. `422 invalid`: a `target` other than `""`/`"userfs"`/`usb:<label>/<dir>`, `persistent` with a stick (*journald would write the stick itself and keep its files open when it is pulled*), an empty `storage` with a stick where the product's default is persistent (the VM), an unknown storage, a bad `persist` in a body without `storage`, a bad size or count, an interval that is not minutes, hours or days or lies outside 15min–7d, an age that is not hours, days or weeks; 400 for a body that is not a JSON object; 501 `not-systemd` off systemd. | **A network share**: `target: share:<name>/<dir>` names a share of `GET /storage/shares` (the PUT refuses one that does not exist or is read-only, `422`; persistent is refused as on a stick, and so is the VM's persistent default); the answer carries `target_share` and `target_dir`, `target_path` is `/media/net/<name>/<dir>`. Nothing is mounted over `/var/log/journal`; `lite-journal-sync` reaches for the share at each copy — the automount mounts it (≤ 30 s) and only a network mount at `/media/net/<name>` is written, never the tmpfs under it — and a share it cannot reach skips the copy with nothing leaving RAM, a failed result and the fallback (`effective: ram`, `journal-target` variant `share` with `share` and `dir`); the next copy that reaches it clears both. The figures are the share's while it is mounted (read through a probe) and `null` while it is not; `target_ok` is false for a read-only mount. a `PUT` whose copies go to a share, or to a stick that is plugged in, write-tests the folder first as root through the helper (made when missing, 1 MiB written, read back, deleted; the share mounted for it and left as it was): when the target or the switch to ram-sync is new and the test fails the answer is **`422`** with the state as `error` (`read-only` — with the root_squash hint for NFS — `unreachable`, `auth-failed`, `stale`, `full`, `error`), `detail: {state, step, error, kind}`, and nothing is written; a save that keeps target and mode (the sizes, say) is taken, and `target_ok` is `false` while the last such test failed. a folder root can write and the system's own user cannot read back (the same look as the share's Test, state `unreadable`) is taken — the copies are written — and the view carries **`target_unreadable: true`**, from that test and from a look at the folder while the share is mounted (`GET /journal` only, not the setting the warnings read). The Log page reads the copies on a share while it is mounted (an idle share is not mounted for a read). `occu-journal-sync.service` is ordered after `remote-fs.target`, so its last copy at shutdown runs before the shares are unmounted.
| `POST /journal/sync` **`system:write`** | **Administrator**: *Copy now*. Only while ram-sync is chosen and in effect — `409 not-ram-sync` otherwise (nothing to copy in RAM only, nothing in RAM in persistent; with a USB stick target also while the stick is not plugged in, and the message names it; a network share that the last copy could not reach may be retried): restarts `occu-journal-sync.service`, whose stop is the copy (serialised with the interval's copy by a lock), and answers the `GET /journal` view with the copy's `last_sync_*`. `422 service-control` when systemctl fails; 501 `not-systemd` without journald or a service manager. |
| `GET /datastore`, `PUT /datastore` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | Occulited's database file (bbolt; occulited alone opens it; its path and layout are no contract) — the health history of `GET /radio/health`, the state store (`GET /api/rpc/v1/state`, bucket `state`) and the datapoint history, one file. `{mode: ""\|"ram"\|"ram-sync"\|"persistent", location, default_location: "userfs:etc/occulite/data", sync_interval, default_mode: "ram-sync"\|"persistent", default_sync_interval: "1h", sync_intervals: ["15min","1h","6h","24h"], platform, effective, open, path, size, rows_per_series: 500, last_sync, last_sync_error?, next_sync, error?, kept: {state?, history?}}` (`kept`: how many datapoints the state store and how many series the datapoint history hold). **The setting** is occulited.json's `store: {mode, sync_interval}`: `mode` empty means the product's default (`persistent` on `ova`, `oci` and `lxc`, `ram-sync` everywhere else); **`ram`** opens no file (memory only, empty after every restart); **`ram-sync`** keeps the history in memory and commits what is new to the file every `sync_interval` (`15min` to `1d`, empty = `1h`) and when occulited stops; **`persistent`** commits every sample round (once a minute) and every changed device value of the state store at once (after a 2-second gathering delay), and the state store's timestamp-only refreshes — 97 % of the events on the Charly — every `sync_interval`, which therefore applies to `persistent` as well. **`location`** (a location id and a folder, never a raw mount path) is `userfs:<folder>` inside `etc/occulite` (what occulited may write); empty = `default_location`; **`share:…` is refused** (bbolt maps its file and needs locking and fsync that SMB/NFS do not guarantee); **`usb:<label>/<dir>` takes a snapshot only**, in ram-sync only (the mode chosen or the product's default — `422` otherwise): the live file stays at `<state>/data/occulited.db`, and after every write of the interval, after a PUT and at a clean stop a consistent copy (bbolt `Tx.WriteTo` into `/run/occulite/store-snapshot.db`) goes through the helper's `storecopy` to `<the stick's mount>/<dir>/occulited-store.db` (a partial file, synced, renamed; only a folder on `/media/usb1…8` with a stick mounted there, no link on the way); at the open — and at the first write after the stick came, when it was missing at the open — a snapshot on the stick newer than the local file by more than 5 s is loaded in its place and merged with what memory holds (a new card, a userfs made anew); a stick that is not plugged in keeps everything on the userfs (`copy: {label, folder, present, dir?, last_copy, last_error?, loaded?}` in the answer, the Status warning `store-target`); a location in the file that is not usable falls back to the default with a warning. A new location at runtime writes and closes the old file and opens the new one, written whole. The PUT (`{mode, sync_interval, location}`, all may be empty) applies at once: into `ram` the file is written a last time and closed, out of it the file is opened, merged with what memory holds and written whole; `422 invalid` for another mode or an interval out of range. **The file** is `<state>/data/occulited.db` in a directory tagged `.nobackup` — no backup carries it (the history is not configuration); a file bbolt cannot open is moved aside to `occulited.db.corrupt` and a fresh one made (a warning in the journal); a directory that cannot be written leaves the history in memory, `open: false` and the reason in `error`. `size` is the file's size in bytes, `last_sync` the last commit of this run, `last_sync_error` why the last one failed (the rows stay in memory and are tried again), `next_sync` the next interval commit (ram-sync: everything; persistent: what was deferred). The Log settings' *History* tab picks `location` with the location picker (`GET /storage/locations?use=store`: the userfs below `etc/occulite`, a stick with the note `copy-only` — choosing one switches the mode to ram-sync and offers no other — a share shown with the reason, not offered); the default folder is sent as the empty setting. **32-bit (`armv7l`):** bbolt maps the whole file into the address space; the file is bounded by 500 rows × the series (tens of kilobytes for the health history), far below that limit. The Log page's settings show it as the tab *History* (`/system/log?settings=history`). |
| `GET /datastore/history`, `PUT /datastore/history` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | The datapoint history's list (`GET /api/rpc/v1/history`). `{datapoints: {<name>: "sampled"\|"event"}, defaults: [...], add: [...], remove: [...], series, capped, rows_per_series: 500, max_series: 1000}` — `datapoints` is what is recorded now: the built-in `defaults` (what the App's cards draw) plus `add` minus `remove`; `series` how many series are kept, `capped` how many reports the series cap turned away since the start. `PUT {add, remove}` (datapoint names, `A-Z 0-9 _`; `422 invalid` otherwise) applies at once and is kept in occulited.json's `store.history_add`/`store.history_remove`; an added name is sampled when it names a temperature, humidity, moisture, level, illumination, power, voltage, current, energy, concentration, wind or rain counter, an event otherwise. A removed name's series stays readable until the ring overwrites it. The *History* tab shows it under *Recorded datapoints*. |
| `GET /loglevels`, `PUT /loglevels` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | `{rfd, hs485d, multimacd, hmip, loghost, lighttpd: {request_handling, condition_handling, file_not_found, access_log}, occulited: {level, debug_areas}, restart: [], applied: [], errors?: {}}`. `rfd` and `hs485d` are OpenCCU's numbers — 1 debug, 2 info, 4 warning, 5 error — from `LOGLEVEL_RFD`/`LOGLEVEL_HS485D` in `/etc/config/syslog`. **The number is eQ-3's own and 1 is the most verbose, not a syslog severity** (measured level by level on 2026-09-22 with the same provocation each time: 20 journal lines at 1, 12 at 2, 9 at 3 and 4, 8 at 5, 4 at 6 and 7, the four error lines in every one of them). The scale runs 1–7; the page offers the four above and shows a stored number outside them without letting it be chosen again. **`multimacd`** is `LOGLEVEL_MULTIMACD`, multimacd's own level and **only `1` (debug) or `2` (info)** (openccu-lite task 297): at 3 and above multimacd logs nothing at its start, and `occulited radio ready multimacd` reads its version line (`Vapp=…`) to tell a module that answered from one that did not (B-275) — a failed `GetVersion` fails the unit's start, and systemd restarts it. It **no longer falls back to rfd's level**: a file without the key, or with anything but `1` (a 0 or 3–7 an older system stored, a word), reads as `2`, and the boot's render (`multimacd.env`, the plan's command line) reads it the same way, still held at 2 or more verbose as a guard. A `PUT` always writes the key (a stale value is replaced by what the page showed); a body **without** the key keeps what is stored; any other value, `null` (the old "same as rfd") included, is refused with 422. **`occulited`** is occulited's own level: `level` `debug`, `info` (default), `warn` or `error`, and `debug_areas`, a subset of `acme`, `radio-firmware`, `addons`, `metadata`, `http`, `auth`, `led` (in that order) whose lines are written from debug up whatever the level. It lives in `occulited.json` (`log_level`, `log_debug_areas`, config.md), **applies at once without a restart** (the next line of every logger follows it) and is handed to the privilege helper, which logs at the same level; a body without the key keeps it. `hmip` is a log4j2 name (`TRACE DEBUG INFO WARN ERROR`, `LOGLEVEL_HMIP`), patched into `/var/etc/log4j2.xml` when hmipserver starts. A file without the keys reads as `rfd: 5`, `hs485d: 5` and **`hmip: "WARN"`** — hmipserver's default since 2026-09-12 (it was `ERROR` before), the level the image's init script and log4j2 template fall back to as well. `loghost` is `LOGHOST` in the same file: a host, `host:port`, `[v6]:port` or a bare IPv6 address. occu-syslog-forward (`occulited syslog-forward`) follows the journal and sends **every entry** there — syslog, stdout, native and kernel alike, hmipserver's and the addons' included — as RFC 5424 over UDP, port 514 unless one is given: `<PRI>1 TIMESTAMP HOST APP PROCID - - MSG` with PRI from `SYSLOG_FACILITY` (0 for the kernel, user without one) and `PRIORITY` (info without one), the realtime in the system's zone with microseconds, `_HOSTNAME`, `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER` (or `_COMM`), `SYSLOG_PID` (or `_PID`), at most 2048 bytes. Its cursor lasts until the reboot, so a restart continues after the last entry sent; a start without one sends this boot's last 10 minutes (by the boot's monotonic clock). A failing send is retried with the same entry. It binds no socket of journald's (busybox syslogd on `/dev/log` did, and took the local syslog messages away). `lighttpd` has no level, only `debug.*` switches, written as the drop-in `/etc/config/lighttpd/occulite-debug.conf` (no switch on = no file). **`access_log`** (off by default) is a drop-in of its own, `/etc/config/lighttpd/occulite-accesslog.conf`, whose line is `accesslog.filename = "\|/usr/bin/systemd-cat -t lighttpd-access -p info"`: every request becomes a journal entry with identifier `lighttpd-access`, meant for debugging (off = no file; read as on when the file has an `accesslog.filename` that pipes to systemd-cat). A pipe, because lighttpd's `accesslog.use-syslog` logs under `lighttpd`, the error log's identifier (measured on 1.4.82); the image loads `mod_accesslog` without a destination, which logs nothing. Every other line of `/etc/config/syslog` is kept. `PUT` writes the three files, sends the level to rfd/hs485d live over their `logLevel` method (`applied`; a failure lands in `errors` and puts the unit into `restart`), and lists in `restart` the units that only pick the change up at their next start: `multimacd` (when its own level changed; an rfd change never restarts it), `hmipserver` (its level; not `LOGHOST` — it logs to the journal), `occu-syslog-forward` (`LOGHOST`), `lighttpd` (the debug switches and `access_log`). Restarting is the caller's, through `POST /services/{id}/restart`, and for `multimacd` through **`POST /radio/restart`**, since multimacd cannot start again under rfd and hmipserver. A changed `occulited` is stored and applied before the answer and listed in `applied`; when it cannot be stored, the daemons' levels are still written and `errors.occulited` says why. Everything is checked before anything is written: 422 `invalid` for a level outside the scale (for multimacd anything but 1 or 2), a `loghost` that is not a host with an optional port or an IPv6 address, an unknown `occulited.level` or area; 501 `not-available` for a change of `occulited` on a daemon without the hook (development). |
| `POST /radio/restart` **`system:write`** | **Administrator**: restarts the radio stack in order — of `hmipserver`, `rfd` and `multimacd`, the units that run stop in that order and start again as `multimacd`, `rfd`, `hmipserver`; a unit that did not run (multimacd *not required* on a single-protocol module) is left alone, `hs485d` never touched. This is what applies a new multimacd level; no device can be reached until hmipserver is back. A stop that fails ends the stop phase, and whatever was stopped is started again either way. `200 {stopped: [], started: [], errors?: {}}`; `422 service-control` with the same fields and `message` when a stop or start failed; 501 without a service manager. |
| `GET /radio/hb-rf-eth` **`system:read`**, `PUT` **`system:write`**, `POST /radio/hb-rf-eth/find` **`system:write`** | **The network radio board (HB-RF-ETH):** one radio module on the LAN that the kernel module `hb_rf_eth` makes a local `raw-uart`. **GET** `{address, connected, module_loaded, retrying, reconnecting?, lost_since?, restarts?, tries, last_try?, last_error?, board?, board_error?}` — `address` is `/etc/config/hb_rf_eth`'s first line (OpenCCU's file; `""` = none), `connected`/`module_loaded` the kernel module's `is_connected`, `retrying` true while a configured board is not connected; `board` what the board says about itself (its `/sysinfo.json`, no login): `{address, serial, firmware (installed), module_type (RPI-RF-MOD\|HM-MOD-RPI-PCB), module_serial, module_sgtin, bidcos_address?, hmip_address?, connected_to?, state: this\|free\|other}`, or `board_error` when it does not answer. **PUT** `{address, take?}`: an **IPv4 address** (the module parses it with `in_aton`: no name, no IPv6 — `422 invalid`) or `""` to remove the board; a board that answers and is connected to **another** system is refused with **`409 in-use`** (`{error, message, board}`) unless `take: true` — there is no pairing, the last system to connect takes the board and the other loses its radio; a board that does not answer is set all the same. The file is written through the helper, and the save starts the radio hotplug (`occu-radio-hotplug.service`, `occulited radio hotplug`), whose rescan connects the board (or lets a removed one go: `-` to `connect`, `rmmod`), lists its module as a new node and restarts only the daemons whose plan changed — no reboot. **A board unreachable at boot, or set a moment ago:** occulited starts the hotplug every 30 s (every 2 minutes after ten tries) while the board is configured and has not connected at that address, and the Status page carries the warning **`hb-rf-eth`** (variant: the address). **A board that was connected and lost its link** is the kernel's `autoreconnect`: no hotplug while its module is loaded (`reconnecting: true`, `lost_since`; the warning's `params.reconnecting` says so), and a rescan in the meantime keeps its node; 30 s after the kernel has it back occulited asks the daemons on it (`listBidcosInterfaces`, the module `CONNECTED`) and restarts them in order on a No or three unanswered asks (`restarts` counts it) - for a board whose power loss reset the module. **find** (`{boards: [board…], error?}`): one mDNS query for `_raw-uart._udp.local.` (unicast answers asked for, from an ephemeral port — nothing listens on 5353; the network discovery's owned rule for mDNS answers - udp from source port 5353, from the local networks - lets the board's unicast answer in with the firewall on), 2.5 s, then each board's `/sysinfo.json`, the configured board included when mDNS does not name it (another subnet). The board's firmware is shown as installed only: occulited asks no update server for it; the board's own page updates it. |
| `GET /radio/device-descriptions` **`system:read`** | Rfd's device description directory `/firmware/rftypes` as the image mounts it writable at boot (`occu-extension-dirs.service`, `lite-extension-dirs`): `{path, available, mode, entries, image, added, replaced, removed, state}`. `available` says the image has the unit (the script is there); without it the rest means nothing. `mode` is `overlay` (the image's directory as the lower layer, upper and work on the userfs under `state`, `/usr/local/etc/config/extensions/rftypes`), `copy` (a kernel without overlayfs: a copy of the image's files on the userfs bound over the directory, refreshed from the image at every boot) or `none` (not mounted). `entries` is what rfd sees, `image` how many entries the image's directory had when the unit mounted it (its `image-names` file, written before the mount hides the lower layer), `added` the writable layer's names the image does not have (the addons' links), `replaced` image names the layer shadows with a file or link of its own, `removed` image names the layer hides (an overlay whiteout) or the copy lacks — the next boot puts those back. Read by occulited itself, no helper call. The Interfaces page's *Device descriptions* section. |
| `POST /radio/device-descriptions/reset` **`system:write`** | The image's files win again — `lite-extension-dirs reset /firmware/rftypes` through the helper (unmount, the whiteouts and the replacements of image names removed from the writable layer, the additions kept, mounted again), then rfd restarted when it runs (it reads the directory at its start only). Answers the `GET` view with `restarted: true` when rfd was restarted; 422 `device-descriptions` with the script's words when the unmount is refused (busy) or the image has no unit. |
| `GET /services/{id}/unit`, `PUT /services/{id}/unit` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | **Edit a unit on a read-only rootfs**, systemd only (busybox answers 501). `GET` returns `{unit, effective, override, path}`: `effective` is `systemctl cat` — every fragment and drop-in systemd applies — and `override` is the drop-in occulited keeps for it, empty when none. `PUT {"override": "..."}` stores the text under `<state-dir>/unit-overrides/<unit>.conf` on the userfs, writes it to `/run/systemd/system/<unit>.d/50-occulite.conf` through the privilege helper, and `daemon-reload`s; an empty override removes both. The stored overrides are re-applied at every start, because `/run` does not survive a boot — the same mechanism as the enable/disable switch. **What is edited is the override, not the unit file**: a drop-in adds and overrides directives and cannot delete them, and list-valued ones (`ExecStart=`, `Environment=`, `After=`) accumulate unless reset with an empty assignment first — so the page shows the effective unit read-only and edits the override, as `systemctl edit` does. `id` may be a service id, a `.service` or a `.timer`. The override must carry a `[Section]` header and is capped at 64 KiB; systemd itself is the authority on the directives. The shipped unit is never written, so a firmware update brings its new units intact. |
| `GET /log[?tag=&unit=&severity=&since=&until=&q=&limit=&kernel=&boot=&run=&before=&after=&head=]` **`logs:read`** | Log lines, newest last, filtered by tag (`occulited`, `rfd`, an addon), unit, severity (at least), substring — the filters combine (a unit **and** a tag narrow each other). `source` says where they come from: `syslog` (busybox `/var/log/messages`, `unit` and `since` ignored) or `journald` (`journalctl -o json`; `unit` is a unit name without `.service`, `since`/`until` any `journalctl --since`/`--until` expression such as `-1h`, `2026-09-06 12:00`, `@1757350800` (epoch seconds — what the UI sends for a picked range, so the browser's and the system's time zones cannot disagree); lines carry `unit`, an RFC 3339 `timestamp` and the journal `cursor`). `occulited` itself logs there when its stderr is not a terminal (`--log auto|stderr|syslog`; under systemd the unit's stderr is the journal). **`kernel`** picks the Log page's source: absent is every message, **`kernel=0`** everything but the kernel's (the other journal transports as journalctl matches, `_TRANSPORT=journal`, `stdout`, `syslog`, `driver` and `audit` — journalctl ORs matches on one field and ANDs them with `-u`, `-t` and `-p`, measured on the x86_64 VM: 775 lines plus the boot's 654 kernel lines are its 1429; busybox syslog is that already), and **`kernel=1`** is the kernel log: on journald the kernel transport (`journalctl -k`, this boot unless `boot` says otherwise); elsewhere the ring buffer through `dmesg -r`, `source: dmesg` — this boot only, as far back as the buffer holds, filtered by tag, severity and text, the rest ignored. A systemd system whose journal holds no kernel line of this boot at all (a container, where journald does not read the kernel's messages) answers with the ring buffer too when `dmesg` is allowed there; a filter that matched nothing is not that case — the journal is asked once more without the filters first. **`boot`** narrows the lines to one boot of the journal (`journalctl --boot=`): a boot id of 32 hex digits (upper case and `/proc`'s UUID form with dashes are taken), `0` for this boot or `-N` for the Nth before it; absent means every boot the journal holds (the kernel log: this boot). **An offset is resolved to the boot's id** through the list `GET /boots` keeps, and journalctl gets that id (on a system without a real-time clock whose journal is kept, the running boot's first entry carries the image's date, and `journalctl -b -1` found no boot while `--list-boots` listed one at `-1`); an offset the list does not hold, or a list that cannot be read, goes to journalctl as it came. `/log/stream` and `/log/download` resolve it the same way. A positive offset, which journalctl counts from the oldest boot, and anything else is `422 invalid`. Busybox syslog ignores `boot`; dmesg has no lines for another boot. Every line carries **`monotonic_us`**, the time since its boot began in microseconds: for a kernel line the kernel's own stamp (`_SOURCE_MONOTONIC_TIMESTAMP`, what dmesg prints as `[   12.345678]`; journald reads the early messages seconds later) - 0 as well, which the JSON leaves out (the kernel prints its first messages at `[0.000000]`) - otherwise journald's `__MONOTONIC_TIMESTAMP`. **A kernel line's `time` and `timestamp`** are its boot's start plus that stamp, not the moment journald read it (the Pi 4's first kernel lines said 13 March): this boot began now − `/proc/uptime`, an earlier boot at its last entry's `__REALTIME_TIMESTAMP` less its `__MONOTONIC_TIMESTAMP` (`journalctl -n 1 --boot=<id>`, once per boot per read, at most 16); a boot that cannot be looked up keeps journald's time. `/log/stream` and `/log/download` place them the same way. **`run`** is one run's lines - a radio firmware flash or an ACME attempt, by the `run_id` its attempt names: the journalctl match `OCCULITE_RUN_ID=<run_id>`, ANDed with the other filters; on busybox syslog the lines whose message carries `run_id=<run_id>` (occulited writes a run's lines into its own log there, the id as an attribute). Anything but a run id (`YYYYMMDDThhmmss-` and eight hex digits, the run's start in UTC) is `422 invalid`, since it becomes a journalctl match.
