For those: `lite-addon-rc adopt <ids>` (the wrapper in front again), `daemon-reload`, the control tokens refreshed; then, unless the installer asked for a reboot or failed, each new addon and each touched addon **that runs afterwards** (its unit active, or a process of it alive) is stopped in its unit and waited for until the unit's cgroup is empty (its `cgroup.procs`; a KILL by pid to what is still there after five seconds), its processes still in the install scope are stopped by pid (TERM, five seconds, KILL, through the helper's `kill` — only pids whose cgroup is this scope and that are the addon's by command line, executable or user; **a root process whose title names nothing** (RedMatic's `node-red`, B-59) is the addon's by its executable, which an unprivileged occulited cannot read for a root process: the settle step and a Restart ask the helper's `procexe` operation for that one link (`/proc/<pid>/exe`, nothing else; root's processes only, never for the Services page's listing)), and its unit is started, so the daemon an update script started from the scope ends up in `addon-<name>.service`. Each touched addon whose stored policy is confined gets its directories chowned to its user again — the three standard ones and its data directories, the step `PUT /addons/{id}/policy` runs — without its policy being rewritten, so what its update script wrote as root does not stop its next start as `addon-<id>`: one that is started again gets them after that stop and before its start, once its old processes are gone, because a root daemon writes on its way out (Mosquitto its persistence); one that is not started now (not running, a reboot, a failed installer) gets them between the adopt and the `daemon-reload`. A new addon's processes in the scope are stopped before its policy chowns it. A failed chown is reported as `[systemd] <id>: its files could not be given to addon-<id>, it may fail to start: …` and the start still runs. Ten seconds after a confined addon was started this way, the check looks at its files again and the warnings are evaluated. **An updated confined addon its update script started in its unit is left running**: its unit is `active` and left the inactive state after the installer began (`InactiveExitTimestampMonotonic`), no process of the addon runs outside the unit, and a dry run of the helper's ownership walk finds every entry its user's - `[systemd] <id> was started in its unit by its update and its files are its user's: left running`, and the check looks again ten seconds later; anything else goes the way above. The output says `[systemd] <id> restarted in its unit` (with the number of processes stopped in the scope when there were any), `[systemd] started <ids> in their units` for new ones, and `[systemd] <id> is not running and was not started` for an updated addon that is stopped afterwards — that one is not started. When an updated addon's unit does not start and a process of it still runs outside the unit, the output adds `[systemd] warning: <id> still runs outside its unit (pid …) - not under the system's control and not confined; Restart on the Services page or a reboot puts it back` (B-59). **The scope itself is never stopped** (an installer that restarted lighttpd leaves lighttpd in it). **A fresh addon's processes in the install scope are stopped whatever the installer's exit**: with a reboot asked for or a failed installer nothing is started, but a daemon the `update_script` started as root - through the addon's own script, before the wrapper existed - is stopped by pid, and the output says `[systemd] <id>: N process(es) its installer started outside a unit were stopped; its unit starts it`. Uninstall stops the unit first, then reloads so it disappears. **A confined addon's `uninstall` runs as the addon's user**: through the helper's `RunAs` with the policy's uid, gid and its one group, the firmware `PATH` and `HOME=/usr/local/addons/<id>`, not in the install scope (`systemd-run --scope --uid` keeps the caller's groups); a root addon's runs as root in the scope as before. Afterwards root removes what the script could not as its user: the rc.d entry and its `.script` (as always), the `www/<id>` link or directory, and `/usr/local/addons/<id>` and `/usr/local/etc/config/addons/<id>` when the script emptied them - a directory with anything left in it stays. The same credential runs a confined addon's `info` for every listing (`GET /addons`, `GET /nav`, the warnings, the daily update check) and its CGIs. |
| `POST /addons/{id}/ownership` **`addons:write`** | **Administrator**, systemd only: gives a confined addon its directories back — the helper's ownership walk over its three standard directories (by descriptors, no link followed, only entries with another owner changed) and the data-directory take-over that `PUT /addons/{id}/policy` and an install through occulited run — and checks it again. `{id, root_owned, started, start_error?}`: `root_owned` a root-owned entry still there afterwards, `""` when none is. **An addon whose unit had failed is started once afterwards**: when `addon-<id>.service` is in `ActiveState=failed` and no process of the addon runs outside it, the normal `systemctl start` runs after the chown — `started: true`, or `start_error` with systemctl's message. A running addon and one that is stopped (`inactive`) are left alone. 409 `not-confined` (no policy, or mode `root`), 422 `ownership` when a chown failed, 501 `unsupported` without the systemd addon manager. The Status page's `addon-ownership` warning offers it as *Fix ownership*. |
| `GET /addons/{id}/images/{kind}` **`system:read`** | An installed addon's icon or logo (openccu-lite task 100): `kind` is `icon`, `icon-dark`, `logo` or `logo-dark`, as the addon's **stored manifest** declares them (`ui.icon`, `ui.icon_dark`, `ui.logo`, `ui.logo_dark`; [manifest-format.md](manifest-format.md)). **The install's copy** comes first (occulited task 11): the image the install took out of the package archive at the declared path from the package root, kept root-owned as `addon-policy/<id>.image-<kind>` beside the stored manifest and served only while that manifest declares the kind (a regular file, at most 256 KiB, an image by its content, as below). **Without one** (an addon installed before, or whose manifest is the catalogue's) the file is read out of the addon's tree at the declared path less its first segment (the directory the package carries the addon in, which the installer copies to `/usr/local/addons/<id>`): `mosquitto/www/icon.svg` is `/usr/local/addons/mosquitto/www/icon.svg`. It is opened through `os.Root` on the tree - a link out of it is refused, never followed - and through the privilege helper's named `addon-image` read where the tree is closed to occulited (a confined addon's, openccu-lite B-252); the helper itself resolves the kind from the stored manifest and answers only a regular file of at most 256 KiB whose **content** is an image (SVG, PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP), so nothing else of the tree comes out through it. The answer carries the type the content says, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'` (an SVG with a script or an external reference is a picture and nothing more, wherever it is opened), `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600` and an `ETag` (`If-None-Match` → 304). `404 not-found` for an addon without a stored manifest, a kind it does not declare, a file that is neither kept nor in the tree, and a file that cannot be served (a link out, too large, no image) - the shell falls back to the next image either way. The shell shows these through `<img>` alone and never inlines them. |
| `GET /addons/{id}/update` **`system:read`** | Fetches the addon's `Update:` URL and compares versions. |
| `GET /addons/updates`, `POST /addons/updates/check` **`system:read` / `addons:write`** | The daily check over every addon with an `Update:` URL (what `checkAddonUpdates.sh` did into a ReGa variable): `{last, available, results: [{id, name, checked, info}]}`; `POST …/check` runs it now. The stored result follows installs at once: a catalogue install or update rechecks that addon, an uninstall drops it, an uploaded archive (which names no addon) runs the whole check, and a daily run that listed the addons before such a step keeps what the step left. |
| `POST /reboot` **`power`** | Administrator only, `{"confirm": true}`; the firmware's reboot, started one second after the answer. Refusals shared with the two routes below: 403 `forbidden`, 400 `confirm` without the flag, 422 `invalid-body` for a body that is not JSON. |
| `POST /halt` **`power`** | Administrator, `{"confirm": true}`: `systemctl poweroff` on systemd, busybox `poweroff` otherwise, through the helper (its allowlist carries `/sbin/poweroff` and `/bin/poweroff`); `200 {ok, halting}`, `501 unsupported` without power control. The system does not come back by itself. The shell's question and its shut-down page say how it starts again from `GET /system-update`'s `running.platform` and `container`, so no field of its own: a board without a power button (`rpi*` except `rpi5`, `tinkerboard`, `odroid*`; the CCU3 and the Charly are `rpi3`) is unplugged and plugged in again, a Raspberry Pi 5 has its button, `ova` and a container are started on their host, anything else gets the general text. |
| `POST /reboot/recovery` **`power`** | Administrator, `{"confirm": true}`: touches `/usr/local/.recoveryMode` and reboots into the recovery system; `200 {ok, rebooting, recovery}`. `409 update-staged` with `detail.file` while `/usr/local/.firmwareUpdate` exists (the recovery would install it), `501 not-available` on a container product, a 5xx when the reboot fails, with the flag removed again. |
| `GET /factory-reset` **`power`** | Administrator: what the Backup page's *Factory reset* section shows before it asks. `{hostname, armed, container, update_staged, interfaces: {<name>: {devices, known, error?}}, security_key_set, security_key_known, security_key_error?, hmip_local_key}`: `hostname` is the name the confirmation has to repeat (netconfig's `HOSTNAME`, else the kernel's); `armed` whether `/usr/local/.doFactoryReset` is set; `interfaces` is `listDevices` on every interface of `InterfacesList.xml` (10 s, in parallel) counted as devices — channels, rfd's `BidCoS-RF` central, the `-RCV-50` receivers and the HmIP module itself left out — with `known: false` and the reason when the interface did not answer (rfd or hmipserver down is *unknown*, never zero); `security_key_set`/`security_key_known` are the Status page's crypttool check; `hmip_local_key` whether `hmip_user.conf` carries a network key. No key leaves the daemon. |
| `POST /factory-reset` **`power`** | Administrator, `{"confirm": true, "hostname": "<typed>"}`: the name typed by the user has to be this system's (case and surrounding space ignored; a typed FQDN passes for the short name) — 400 `hostname` otherwise, and nothing happens. Touches `/usr/local/.doFactoryReset` through the helper (the marker the firmware's `S04CheckFactoryReset` / `occu-factory-reset.service` reads at boot: the userfs is made anew, `.doCoproFactoryReset` set for the radio module) and reboots; `200 {ok, rebooting, reset}`. 400 `confirm` without the flag, 422 `invalid-body`, 409 `update-staged` with `detail.file` while `/usr/local/.firmwareUpdate` exists (the recovery would install it at that boot), 501 `unsupported` without power control, a 5xx when the reboot fails, with the marker removed again. Nothing is kept. No boot-timing marker: the page counts down with the `restore` figures. |
| `DELETE /factory-reset` **`power`** | Administrator: removes a set marker (a reset armed whose reboot did not happen would wipe the userfs at the next boot, whenever that is); `200 {ok, armed: false}`, also when nothing was set. |
| `GET /boot-expect?kind=` **`system:read`** | Any session: how long a user-initiated reboot of `kind` (`reboot` when absent, `recovery`, `update`, `restore`, `halt`) is expected to take on this system, per phase in seconds: `{kind, product, expect: {down, http, ui, ready}, default: {down, http, ui, ready}, measured: {down, http, ui, ready}}`. `down` runs from the request until the system stops answering, `http` from there until lighttpd answers, `ui` until occulited answers, `ready` until rfd and hmipserver are up. `default` is the product's row of `internal/bootexpect/defaults.json` (by `/VERSION`'s `PLATFORM`; a product the table does not list gets the one its `default` names, `rpi4`); `expect` replaces each phase with the median of this product's newest records of that kind (up to three) that tell that phase, and `measured` counts them (0 = the default). `recovery` and `halt` use the reboot's figures and records. 400 `invalid` for another kind. Without the calibration the answer is the default. The web interface asks right before it writes its countdown and sends the reboot, the install or the restore. |
| `POST /boot-timing` **`system:write`** | Administrator: what the page observed during a reboot, epoch milliseconds of the browser's clock: `{kind, started, down?, http?, ui?, ready?}` — `started` when it sent the request, `down` the first poll without an answer, `http` lighttpd's first 503 after that, `ui` occulited's first answer, `ready` rfd and hmipserver running. At least one of `down`, `http`, `ui`; none before the one ahead of it and all within an hour of `started`, else 400 `invalid`. Attached to the reboot of the same `kind` that is being recorded or was recorded in this boot, requested within an hour of `started`: `200 {ok, attached: "pending"|"record"}` (`pending` while the record waits for the clock); 409 `no-record` when there is none; 422 `invalid-body` for a body that is not JSON; 501 `unsupported` without the calibration. |
| *(the calibration)* | `POST /reboot`, `/reboot/recovery`, `/system-update/install` and `/restore/apply` write `<state-dir>/boot-timing/pending.json` — `{kind, request_wall_ms, boot_id, product}`, the boot id from `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id` — right before the reboot starts, and remove it when the reboot (or the restore) fails; a `/reboot` while an update is set to install counts as `update`. At the next start (a different boot id) occulited waits, every 10 s for up to 10 minutes, for a clock it can trust: `/run/occulite/clock-state` says `rtc` or `ntp`, or `chronyc -n tracking` says `Leap status : Normal` (a system without an RTC starts with a wrong one). Then it records `kernel_start_wall_ms` (now − `/proc/uptime`), `to_kernel_s` (from the request), `http_mono_s` (lighttpd.service's `ActiveEnterTimestampMonotonic`, through `systemctl show`), `ui_mono_s` (occulited listening) and, once rfd and hmipserver are each active or will not be (not loaded, condition unmet, disabled, masked, failed), `ready_mono_s` (the later of the two; absent when neither ran), with the derived `phases` — `down` (the browser's), `down_http` (request to lighttpd), `http` (`down_http` − `down`), `ui` (occulited after lighttpd, 0 when it was first), `ready` (after the later of the two) — and the browser's checkpoints. `<state-dir>/boot-timings.json`, `{records: {<kind>: [record, …]}}`, keeps the last three per kind and is written atomically. A marker of this same boot is left alone (occulited restarted, the reboot has not happened) unless it is over an hour old; an implausible gap (the kernel before the request or more than an hour after it) and a clock never trusted drop the measurement, never the reboot. A `restore` marker also gets a copy as `/usr/local/tmp/.occulite-boot-timing` (staged by the daemon, moved by the helper): the restore at boot deletes the state directory before it unpacks the backup, the marker with it, and only `/usr/local/tmp`'s dotfiles survive — the next start reads the copy when the state directory has no marker, and the marker's removal removes both. |
| `GET /catalog` **`system:read`** | `{catalog: {format, checked?, releases_error?, source?, bundled_date?, check_error?, addons: [...]}, installed, arch, daily}` — **`source`** (B-52) `published` when a published catalogue file (fetched by a check, or the copy the last one kept) is part of the list, `bundled` when the list is the image's own copy alone - a fresh system, or one whose checks never reached the published file - and absent without entries; **`bundled_date`** the time of that copy's file (the page says *the built-in list of 4.10.2026*); **`check_error`** `{at, message, host?, failures, since}` (B-52): the last check failed - the published catalogue file could not be fetched (`host` the one that failed, `message` `<host>: <cause>`; the kept or the bundled copy stands in), no addon's manifest could be read from its repository - GitHub's rate limit, say; the adapters beside the catalogue file do not count - (`no addon's manifest could be read (<owner/repo>: <error>)`), nothing loaded at all, or the check did not finish within its 90 s; `failures` counts the failed checks in a row, the user's and the daily run's, `since` is the first of them; kept across a restart; a check that works removes it, and so does a daily fetch of the published file that works. `checked` is the last check that **worked**: a failed one leaves it, and the journal says `catalog: the check failed`. — **`releases_error`** `{code, repo, message, at, retry_at?, retry_minutes?}` (B-21): a release list the last check (the user's or the daily one) could not read — `code` `github-rate-limit` (403 with the budget spent, 429; `retry_minutes` from `Retry-After`, else `X-RateLimit-Reset`, counted when the view is answered) or `releases-unreachable`; the versions and update hints are then the ones from before, never an older list read in its place; the next check that reads every list removes it. — the catalogue's entries joined with what the system knows of their manifests, from the cache; **nothing is fetched on a page load** (B-240): the catalogue files are the copy the last check left in the cache (`catalogs` in `<state>/catalog-cache.json`) and the bundled `/etc/occulite/catalog.json`, whatever *Check daily* says; the same holds for the start of the service (an installed addon without a manifest takes the catalogue's from that copy) and for an install's lookup. A system that never ran a check answers its bundled catalogue. An entry is `{git, manifest_path, verified, adapter?}` plus, once the user's check has fetched its manifest (or for an adapter the image carries), the manifest's fields flattened in — `id`, `name` and `description` (`{de, en}`), `homepage`, `changelog`, `licence`, `release`, `requires`, `ui`, `runtime` — and `tag` (the release tag the manifest was read at), `fetched`, `error` (the last fetch's failure, the previous manifest kept), `stars` (GitHub, from the last check), `latest: {version, asset, notes_url}` (the release the resolver would pick for this architecture; `notes_url` its GitHub page), **`release_notes`** (occulited task 26: the manifest's `changelog`, else `latest.notes_url`; absent when neither is an http(s) URL - the Addons page links it as *Release notes* beside an offered update) **`images`** (openccu-lite task 100: kind → `/api/system/v1/catalog/<id>/images/<kind>?v=<hash>`, the images the check fetched with the manifest - below) and `update_available: true` when the addon is installed and its `info` version differs from that release (leading `v` ignored, plain dotted numbers compared numerically, anything else by string — except that a prerelease on the release side of an otherwise equal or older version is never an update, and two prereleases of one version are ordered `dev` < `alpha` < `beta` < `rc` with their numbers numerically, as the system update does). An entry without `id` has not been checked yet. `checked` is when the last check that worked ran. **`?refresh=1` is the user's check**: the catalogue files again (`catalog.urls`, the published file first, the bundled copy `/etc/occulite/catalog.json` last, the first entry per repository winning), every entry's manifest at its latest GitHub release tag (an adapter from beside the catalogue file), the star counts and the latest releases, all conditional requests, all cached in `<state>/catalog-cache.json`; 502 `catalog-unreachable` only when no catalogue file loads (recorded in `check_error` as well); any other failure answers the view with `check_error`. Format: occulited's [catalog-format.md](catalog-format.md) and [manifest-format.md](manifest-format.md). |
| `POST /catalog/refresh` **`addons:write`** | The user's check without the view: what `?refresh=1` does. |
| `PUT /catalog/settings` **`addons:write`** | `{"daily"}` switches the daily check behind the Addons page's *Check daily* - the catalogue's daily release refresh and the installed addons' daily update check (`/addons/updates`) - persisted as `catalog.daily` in `occulited.json` (absent = off since B-241; config.md's *The outbound switches*) → `{ok, daily}`; `501` where the daily check cannot be switched. `GET /catalog` answers `daily` beside `catalog`. The button's check runs either way. |
| `GET /catalog/{id}/images/{kind}` **`system:read`** | A catalogue entry's icon or logo (openccu-lite task 100), `kind` as above: the copy **the user's check fetched with the manifest** from the addon's repository at the tag the manifest was read at (or its default branch, where the manifest came from there), beside the manifest - its directory in the repository is the package root the declared paths are relative to - with the same rules as an installed addon's - at most 256 KiB, the type by content, a file that is no image not kept - into `<state>/catalog-images/<sha256>`; a check whose manifest is unchanged keeps them and fetches only what is missing, a file no entry names any more is removed. No image is fetched for an adapter manifest (one the catalogue carries for a third-party addon: its paths name a package, not that repository). Nothing is fetched on a page load or by this route (B-240). The same headers as the installed addon's route; `404 not-found` for an addon the catalogue does not know, a kind its manifest does not declare, and one the check could not take. The Addons page shows it on a card that is not installed; an installed addon's card takes the installed one. |
| `POST /catalog/{id}/install` **`addons:write`** | Resolves the latest matching release on GitHub from the addon's manifest `release` (409 `install-running` while an upload or another catalogue install runs - the one install slot is taken before the `202` answers, so a start beside a running one is refused, never accepted and dropped (B-25); an id no fetched or bundled manifest names fails the run: *run a check first*), downloads, verifies a sha256 sidecar when the project publishes one, installs through the same installer an upload takes. **The package's own `openccu-lite.json` is what gets applied** (below); the catalogue's manifest stands in for a package without one. The same route updates an installed addon: `install_addon` runs the addon's own `update_script`. One at a time; progress at `GET /catalog/progress`. **The release list is read at the install, never taken from an earlier answer** (B-21): when it cannot be read the run fails before any download and installs nothing — the progress carries `error: "github-rate-limit"` with `retry_minutes` (GitHub's wait, 0 when it did not say) or `error: "releases-unreachable"`, and `message` in English (*GitHub rate limit, try again in N min: … nothing was installed*). |

### Device firmware

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /firmware` **`system:read`** | Fetcher status: enabled, last check, per paired type the deployed and the available version, last actions. `devices[]` carries `latest` (eQ-3's version for the device's type from the index) and `not_listed: true` when the index was read and has no entry for the type; `update_available` is true when `available_firmware` (the interface's deployed bundle) or `latest` beats `firmware` (a device reporting only major.minor — BidCos — is compared on those two). Types are matched as eQ-3's WebUI matches them: case-insensitive, an index type's last `_` read as a space, `HmIP-HAP-JS1` ≡ `HmIP-HAP JS1`, `HmIP-HAP-B1` taking `HmIP-HAP`'s entry; a type listed twice keeps its higher version; a download asks with the index's spelling. The index is asked with `?product=HM-CCU3&version=<VERSION>` and a fetched bundle whose `CCU3FirmwareVersionMin` is above the system's `VERSION` is refused (`failed`, detail names both; the manual upload is not checked). The last run (`last_run`, `last_error`, `last_result`, `devices`, `index_size`, `interface_errors`) is kept in `<state>/firmware-state.json` across restarts; with the switch on, a run that is due (none yet, or the last older than a day) comes 5 minutes after the start, a failed one is retried after an hour, and every run logs one `firmware: run` line (devices, index size, planned, deployed, failed). `deployed[].version` is `""` when the bundle's `info` has an empty, missing or all-zero `FirmwareVersion` (eQ-3 writes `0.0.0` into some); `deployed[].info` keeps the raw value. Such a bundle also carries `version_from_name` and `date_from_name`: the version and the build date of its update file's name (`HmIPW-DRS8_update_V1_2_6_220928.efw` → `1.2.6`, `2022-09-28`; the date written `yymmdd`, `yyyymmdd` or `yyyy_mm_dd`, `""` when the name has none), both absent when the info states a version or no file name follows `…_update_V<major>_<minor>[_…][_<date>].<ext>`. `last_result[]` carries the same two fields for a `deployed` or `downloaded` type. The page shows them muted, marked *from the file name*. `devices[]` carries `updatable` - the interface's `UPDATABLE` (rfd: an integer, hmipserver: a boolean; absent when it does not say); a device with `updatable: false` cannot be updated over the air at all: it is never planned or fetched for, has no `latest`/`not_listed`, `update_available` false, and the page shows *not updatable over the air*. `deployed[]` carries `dir`, the bundle's directory: the `TypeCode`, or `<TypeCode>-<Name>` when another type's bundle holds that TypeCode (HmIP-WRC2 and HM-LC-Dim1T-DR are both 261) - `type_code` is the bundle's own from its `info`. After a deploy only **BidCos-RF and HmIP-RF** are asked to `refreshDeployedDeviceFirmwareList` (as the WebUI does; hs485d and VirtualDevices have no user firmware), by the fetcher and the upload alike. |
| `GET /firmware/bundles/{type_code}/files/{name}` **`system:read`** | Any role: one text file of a deployed bundle (`{type_code}` is the bundle's `dir`) — `name` must be a plain name the bundle lists (no `..`, slash, backslash, directory or symlink), with the extension `.txt`, `.md` or `.log`, or the name `info`/`changelog.txt`; `200 text/plain; charset=utf-8` with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, a non-UTF-8 file converted from Windows-1252. `404 not-found`, `415 not-viewable`, `413 too-large` over 256 KB with `detail {size, limit}`, `501` without the firmware service. |
| `POST /firmware/check` **`system:write`** | Runs a check now. `PUT /firmware/settings {"enabled"}` is the outbound-call switch - the daily check and download, *Check daily* beside *Check now* on the Updates page; **off on a fresh system** (D-90, B-241), asked once on the welcome page as the eQ-3 checkbox. |
| `POST /firmware/upload` **`system:write`** | Manual upload of an eQ-3 `.tgz`, validated and deployed into `/etc/config/firmware/<TypeCode>/` - or `<TypeCode>-<Name>/` when another type's bundle holds that TypeCode, a bundle of the same type replacing its own wherever it lies - and BidCos-RF and HmIP-RF told to refresh. A bundle carries an `.efw`, an `.eq3` or — for the access points (HmIP-HAP, -HAP-JS1, -HAP2, -HAP2-A, HmIPW-DRAP) — a `.zip` holding `.eq3` images, deployed as it is (never unpacked; a `.zip` that is not a zip or holds no `.eq3` is refused). The fetcher deploys the same way. |

### System firmware update

| | |
| --- | --- |
| *(`/VERSION`)* | `VERSION=<OpenCCU base tag>` stays what the recovery compares; `LITE=<semver>` is openccu-lite's own version (`1.0.0-alpha.0`); `running.lite` in the answers below carries it and the feed compares it, as a semantic version with prerelease order, against the asset names `openccu-lite-<product>-<version>.zip`. The prerelease tags rank **`dev` < `alpha` < `beta` < `rc`** (< the release), not in SemVer's ASCII order, so `1.0.0-dev.31` < `1.0.0-beta.0`; numbers within a tag compare numerically (`beta.2` < `beta.10`), and a tag that is none of these ranks below `dev`. `GET /health` (open) answers `release` and `base` for the shell's top bar. |
| `GET /system-update` **`system:read`** | `{running: <the /VERSION record>, staged: null | {file, size, modified, kind, version, board, warning, recovery_armed, way_back, foreign}, feed: null | {enabled, feed_url, checked, error, error_host, error_timeout, installed_newer, downloading, available: null | {version, tag, name, url, size, sha256_url, published, notes_url, newer}}, container: ""\|"lxc"}`. A failed check's `error` names the feed's answer as `feed: HTTP <status>` with GitHub's `message`, not its JSON body (the raw rate-limit answer was one run of text wider than a phone); another body is cut at 300 characters. `installed_newer` (B-57): the system runs a newer version than the newest the feed offers (a round not yet published, a local build) - `available` is then the newest published one, not the installed one. `error_host` and `error_timeout` (seconds) are set when the feed's host did not answer in time (B-56), so the page names both in the user's language. **On a container** there is no recovery system and no partition to write: the rootfs is the template, swapped on the host (a new CT from the new template, the `/usr/local` volume moved over — [install-lxc.md](https://github.com/hobbyquaker/openccu-lite/blob/main/docs/install-lxc.md)), and `/usr/local` survives. `upload`, `install` and `download` answer `501 host-managed` there; `check` still runs and names the newer template. `staged.foreign` (occulited task 22): the file names another board than this system's, which the recovery refuses (`warning` says so). |
| `POST /system-update/check` **`power`** | Asks the release feed now (`system_update.feed`, ETag-cached; the system does it daily when enabled) and picks the asset for this product: `openccu-lite-<product>-<version>.zip` (on OpenCCU `OpenCCU-<version>-<PRODUCT>.<zip\|tar.xz>`), where the lite product is looked up from the upstream `PRODUCT` that `/VERSION` keeps — `ova`→`x86_64-ova`, `rpi3`→`aarch64-rpi3`, `rpi4`→`aarch64-rpi4`, `rpi5`→`aarch64-rpi5`, and for the CT templates `lxc_amd64`→`lxc-amd64`, `lxc_arm64`→`lxc-arm64` with the extension `.tar.xz`. **The feed is GitHub's release list** (`…/releases?per_page=20`, the default; a stored `…/releases/latest`, the default of earlier versions, follows it), because `releases/latest` never returns a prerelease: drafts are skipped; **a system that runs a prerelease** (`1.0.0-dev.26`, a beta) **follows prereleases**, a system on a release sees releases only — GitHub's `prerelease` flag or a prerelease version both count as one; of what is left, the release whose asset has the newest version by semver wins, whatever the list's order (on OpenCCU, whose versions are not semver, the first one listed). A feed that serves one release object (`releases/latest`, a hand-made feed) is read the same way. 502 `feed-unreachable` with `feed` when unreachable. **Timeouts (B-56):** a request for the feed's metadata (the release list, a `.sha256`) is bounded as a whole by 45 s, a download waits at most 30 s for its response header and then takes as long as it needs (the client's 30 minutes) - so a GitHub that accepts and never answers ends the check after 45 s, with `detail: {host, timeout}` and the message `<host> did not answer within 45s`; the catalogue (each try 30 s for the header) and eQ-3's firmware index (45 s) are bounded the same way; a feed with **no published release yet** (an empty list, or GitHub's 404 on `releases/latest`) says so in as many words rather than "HTTP 404 Not Found". |
| `GET /hmipserver/settings` **`system:read`**, `PUT /hmipserver/settings` **`system:write`** | occulited task 33: `{diagrams}` - whether hmipserver's diagram data is carried between its tmpfs and the stick at its start and stop (`hmipserver.diagrams` in occulited.json, off by default; [config.md](config.md)). `PUT {"diagrams": true\|false}` persists it → `{ok, diagrams, applies: "next-start"}` (the radio steps read it at hmipserver's next start or stop); `400` without the key, `501` where occulited does not manage hmipserver. With it off, a migrated system's diagram data is removed once. No page shows it yet. |
| `PUT /system-update/settings` **`power`** | `{"enabled"}` switches the release feed's daily check (*Check daily* beside *Check now*; **off on a fresh system**, D-90, B-241 - the welcome page's GitHub checkbox sets it together with `PUT /catalog/settings`), at once and persisted as `system_update.enabled` → `{ok, enabled}`; `GET /system-update`'s `feed.enabled` says it. `501` without a feed. The button's check runs either way. |
| `POST /system-update/download` **`power`** | Downloads the available release into `/usr/local/tmp`, verifies it against the published `.sha256` when there is one, and stages it exactly as an upload. Long-running; answers with the staged record, or `422 no-space` as an upload. **`{"version"}`** (occulited task 22) downloads that published version instead, from either channel - an older one too; `{"version": "latest"}` the newest of the channel the Updates page follows; `404 not_found` for a version the feed does not list. A release whose `.sha256` is published but cannot be read fails the download (`502 download-failed`) instead of staging it unverified; a verified one answers its checksum as `sha256`. |
| `GET /system-update/releases` **`power`** | occulited task 22: asks the feed now and lists this product's releases, newest first: `{running, channel, default_channel, releases: [{version, tag, name, url, size, sha256_url, published, notes_url, newer, prerelease, direction}]}`. `?channel=pre` (releases and prereleases), `stable` (releases only), `all`; none is `default_channel`, the one the Updates page follows (a system on a prerelease follows prereleases). `direction` is what installing it means: `upgrade`, `same`, `downgrade`, or `other` where a version is no semantic version. Asked for the default channel, the newest is also what `GET /system-update` reports as `feed.available` afterwards. `502 feed-unreachable` (with `detail: {host, timeout}` when the feed did not answer within 45 s, B-56; for the default channel that failure is also `GET /system-update`'s `feed.error`), `400 invalid` for another channel, `501` without a feed. |
| `POST /system-update/upload` **`power`** | Multipart `file` (or a raw body with `?name=`): stored as `/usr/local/tmp/<name>`, checked as the WebUI's Firmware update dialog checked it — a zip with `EULA.en`, `EULA.de` and an `*.img`, a tar(.gz) with `EULA.*`, a disk image with three partitions (the third ext4), an ext4 `rootfs`, a vfat `bootfs` — and linked as `/usr/local/.firmwareUpdate`. **`422 no-space`** (B-247) with `detail: {free, required}` when the recovery could not unpack it: the recovery's own rule (`fwinstall.sh`, step 5/7: a zip's unpacked total, a tar's file sizes, must be below the free space of `/usr/local/tmp` with the file still there) plus 64 MiB; the file is removed, nothing staged, and the Updates page says free and needed space and to remove old backups. A disk image, a `rootfs` or a `bootfs` is not unpacked and not checked. An `OpenCCU-<version>-<board>.*` name yields `version`/`board`, with a `warning` when the board is not this system's product (the recovery compares `PLATFORM`; `-lite` boards are the same platform). 422 with the reason otherwise; a previous staged file is replaced. **`way_back`**: the name is not an openccu-lite release (neither `openccu-lite-<product>-<version>.*` nor `OpenCCU-<version>-lite.<n>-<board>.*`), so it may be the way back to OpenCCU — a renamed file counts too. Staging one while HSTS is on switches HSTS into the clearing state (`max-age=0`, see *HTTPS redirect and HSTS*) with one reload, before OpenCCU's missing HSTS and later self-signed certificate can lock a browser out; the answer adds `hsts_cleared: true` and `hsts_clearing_until`, or `hsts_error` when the switch failed (the file stays staged). |
| `POST /system-update/install` **`power`** | Touches `/usr/local/.recoveryMode` (the bootloader then starts the recovery system, whose `fwinstall` writes bootfs and rootfs and keeps the user partition) and reboots. 409 when nothing is staged; `422 no-space` as for the upload when the room has gone since (a nightly backup), nothing armed. This is also **the way back to OpenCCU**: upload upstream's release zip and install. |
| `DELETE /system-update` **`power`** | Removes the staged file, the link and the marker. |

### Network, firewall, time, LEDs, SSH

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /network` **`system:read`** | `{network, settings, current, pending, writable, host_managed}` — the file, the editable settings, the live state (CURRENT_*), an open transaction. **`host_managed: true` on a container**: the veth and its address, gateway and DNS are the container manager's (Proxmox), so `writable` is `false` and the page is a display; what `current` shows is what the container was given. `network.domain` (from `/etc/resolv.conf`: `domain`, else the first `search`; always present, empty without one); `network.ipv6_state {available, enabled, gateway?, gateway_interface?, dns[]}`; per interface `operstate?`, `carrier?`, `speed?` (Mbit/s, absent when sysfs has none), `duplex?`, `mtu?`, `kind`, `driver?`, `default_route?`, `ipv4 [{address, prefix}]` (was a list of strings, always empty), `ipv6 [{address, prefix, scope, flags?}]` (scope and flags from `/proc/net/if_inet6`), `ipv6_enabled?`, `ipv6_autoconf?`, `statistics? {rx_bytes, tx_bytes, rx_errors, tx_errors, rx_dropped, tx_dropped}`; `lo` is skipped. The Network page shows the current receive and send rate from two of its 5 s polls of these counters (nothing in the API). **A domain change**: this route is where the page's poll sees a domain another DHCP lease brought — ACME names that equal the default of the domain seen last (`<host>.<old domain>, <host>`) become `<host>.<new domain>, <host>`; names set by hand and an empty list stay; the domain seen last is `acme/domain.json` ([config.md](config.md)), also looked at on start, `GET /certificate`, before a rename and at every renewal check. |
| `POST /network` **`system:write`** | Settings `{hostname, mode: dhcp\|static, address?, netmask?, gateway?, dns: []}`. Validated; applied **live** the way `eQ3StartNetwork` does at boot; a 90 s window opens (`{applied: false, pending: {token, settings, previous, deadline}}`). Hostname-only changes are saved at once (`applied: true`). **`dns` under DHCP is the override**: empty = the lease's servers; otherwise an exclusive openresolv record `lo.occulite` (the servers plus the lease's `domain`/`search`) replaces them, and a renewal does not undo it. A DHCP change of the DNS alone applies and reverts only that record (no DHCP restart). It is stored in `<state>/dns-override`, not in `netconfig` (whose `NAMESERVER1/2` stay the static setup's); occulited puts the record back after a boot and follows the lease's domain (checked every minute); a static setup removes it. `GET /network` reads it back as `settings.dns`, and `current.dns` shows it while it is set. `409` while a transaction is open. **`501 host-managed` on a container**, as are `confirm` and `revert`: nothing writes `/etc/config/netconfig` there. **A rename**: the hostname-only answer — and `POST /network/confirm` for a confirmed change that renames — carries `acme_names {state, names, previous, error?}`, `state` one of `adapted` (the ACME names equalled the old host's default `<host>.<domain>, <host>` or were empty, and were rewritten), `fitting`, `set-by-hand` (left alone), `not-in-use` (not in ACME mode or no certificate service; following names are still rewritten), `failed` (see `error`). With the HTTPS settings on the system the same answers carry `fqdn_redirect {on, state, target, previous?, reason?}`: the redirect from the bare host name follows the rename to `<new host>.<domain>` (`previous`: the name it led to, when it was rewritten), `state` and `reason` as in `GET /https` — typically `suspended` with `not-covered` until a certificate for the new name is installed. the hostname-only answer also carries `rename {hostname, previous, lease: {renewed, static?, error?, address?, at}}` — on DHCP the client was restarted under the new name (`systemctl reload occu-network` inside the unit with systemd; kill and restart of udhcpc without), so the DHCP server and the router's DNS learn it (`renewed`, the interface's address read back); on a static setup `static: true`, nothing to tell; a failed reload is `error`, the name set all the same — and `certificate {names, fits, mode: self-signed\|acme\|manual, known}`: the live certificate's names and whether one is the new host bare or as `<host>.<domain>`, for the page's reminder with the button to the Certificate page. `/etc/hosts` is edited (the `127.0.1.1` line), not replaced: an addon's or the user's entries stay. A rename on a DHCP setup is hostname-only now (the lease's address is not a setting); it opened a confirmation window before. Every DHCP client occulited starts itself (a DHCP change here, the rename's restart without systemd) sends the vendor class (option 60) of `/etc/dhcp-vendor-class`'s `DHCP_VENDOR_ID` - `openccu-lite` on the images - as `eQ3StartNetwork` and `lite-network-reload` do, and a CCU3's `eQ3-CCU3` without the file (openccu-lite task 327). **The welcome page** offers the rename as its first step (openccu-lite task 327: a fresh install is called `openccu-lite-<the last four hex digits of eth0's MAC>`): the current `settings` with the new name, which is this hostname-only change - not on a container or while a transaction is open. |
| `GET /wifi` **`system:read`** | Wi-Fi, occulited's beside upstream's network start (`occu-wifi.service` applies it as root: `occulited wifi up\|reload\|down`). `{chips, settings, state, status?, signal?, addresses, networks, confirm?, setup_error?}`. **`chips`** `[{iface, kind: onboard\|usb, present}]`: every interface with a `wireless` directory in sysfs, a USB one by its device path, and the onboard SDIO chip (`OF_NAME=wifi`) also while its driver is unloaded (`present: false`). **`settings`** is `/etc/config/wifi`: `{enabled, iface, country, mode: dhcp\|static, address?, netmask?, gateway?, dns?, preferred: eth\|wlan}`. **`state`**: `no-chip`, `off`, `starting` (switched on, no supplicant socket yet), `not-configured` (no network saved; scanning works), `connecting`, `connected` (the supplicant `COMPLETED` and an IPv4 address), `disconnected`. **`status`** is the supplicant's `STATUS` (`state`, `ssid`, `bssid`, `freq`, `key_mgmt`, `ip`), **`signal`** its `SIGNAL_POLL` (`rssi` dBm, `link_speed` Mbit/s, `freq`) while connected. **`networks`** `[{ssid, security: open\|wpa2\|wpa3\|wpa2-wpa3, hidden, priority}]` - never a key: `/etc/config/wpa_supplicant.conf` (root 0600, on the helper's read list) holds WPA2 keys hashed as `wpa_passphrase` does, and a WPA3 network's `sae_password`, which SAE needs in clear. **`confirm`**: the deadline of a change made from an address in a Wi-Fi subnet, reverted without `POST /wifi/confirm`. **`setup_error`**: why the last setup file on the boot partition (`openccu-lite-wifi.txt`, `.txt.txt`, or upstream\'s `SetupWIFI`) was refused. 501 `unsupported` off the systemd products. |
| `PUT /wifi` **`system:write`** | The settings above, validated (`iface` a `wl…` name, `country` two capitals, the static fields as for `POST /network`, at most two nameservers, `preferred` `eth` or `wlan`); written to `/etc/config/wifi`, then `occu-wifi` is **restarted** for the switch, the interface or the country (the country also goes into `wpa_supplicant.conf`) and **reloaded** for the addressing and the preference. The default route's metric follows `preferred`: Wi-Fi 5 when preferred, 600 when not, beside DHCP Ethernet's 10 (`dhcp.script`); a static Ethernet route (metric 0) moves to 700 while Wi-Fi is preferred. Answers the view. 422 `invalid` with the rule. |
| `POST /wifi/scan` **`system:write`** | Scans through the supplicant's control socket and answers `{networks: [{ssid, bssid, freq, signal, security, hidden?}], saved: [ssid]}` after 4 s at the earliest (a full two-band scan of the Pi's onboard chip takes that long) and 8 s at the latest: the strongest BSS per SSID, strongest first, one `hidden` entry for BSSs without a name; `security` also `enterprise` and `wep`, which the page cannot join. 422 while switched off. |
| `POST /wifi/networks` **`system:write`** | `{ssid, security, password, hidden}`: saves the network (one of the same SSID is replaced) with the highest priority, switches Wi-Fi on when it was off, and has the supplicant re-read its file (`RECONFIGURE`, or a restart of `occu-wifi` while its socket is not there). The password: 8 to 63 printable characters or 64 hex digits; never logged, never answered. Answers the view. |
| `DELETE /wifi/networks/{ssid}` **`system:write`** | Forgets a saved network (the supplicant re-reads its file). 422 for an SSID that is not saved. |
| `POST /wifi/confirm` **`system:write`** | Keeps a change made over the Wi-Fi: `{confirmed}`, false when there was none. |
| `GET /network/pending` **`system:read`** | The open transaction, or `null`. |
| `POST /network/confirm`, `POST /network/revert` **`system:write`** | `{token}`. Confirm persists `netconfig`; revert (or the timer) restores the previous live state. Confirm from the new address with `?sid=` — the cookie does not exist there. |
| `GET /network/ipv6` **`system:read`**, `POST /network/ipv6`, `POST /network/ipv6/confirm`, `POST /network/ipv6/revert` **`system:write`** | IPv6 per interface. `GET` → `{interfaces: {<name>: {mode, address?, prefix?, gateway?, dns?}}, pending, window_seconds: 60, writable}` — `mode` is `off`, `slaac`, `dhcpv6` or `static`; an interface the stored configuration does not name shows what the kernel does (`slaac` by default). `POST {interface, mode, address, prefix, gateway, dns}` applies it live — off: `disable_ipv6=1`; SLAAC: `accept_ra=1`, `autoconf=1`; DHCPv6: `autoconf=0` and `udhcpc6` in the transient unit `occu-dhcp6-<if>.service` with the image's `/usr/libexec/occu/lite-dhcp6` (IPv6 only; the leased /128 noted in `/run/occulite/dhcp6-<if>`, the nameservers as the resolvconf record `<if>.dhcp6`); static: `accept_ra=0`, `autoconf=0`, the address, a default route via `gateway`, the servers as `<if>.ipv6` — and answers the view with `pending: {token, interface, settings, previous, started, deadline}` and `changed` (false: nothing to change, nothing pending). IPv4 is never touched. `confirm {token}` stores it (`<state>/ipv6.json`, applied at every start); `revert {token}`, or no confirmation within 60 s, applies `previous` again; a change still pending when occulited stops is rolled back at its next start (`<state>/ipv6.pending.json`). 422 `invalid` (a bad setting, an interface that is not there, `all`/`default`/`lo`), 409 `pending`, 404 `not_found` for a token no longer pending; refused on a container (the host's network). |
| `GET /firewall` **`system:read`** | The firewall as one ordered list of INPUT rules in `/etc/config/firewall-rules.json` (in a backup). `{config, draft, pending, frame, local_networks, owners, error?}`. `config` is the confirmed file: `{version, policy: {ipv4: ACCEPT\|DROP, ipv6, log}, rules: [{id, port, port_to?, proto: tcp\|udp\|igmp, sport?, source, dest?, family: ipv4\|ipv6\|both, target: ACCEPT\|DROP\|REJECT, log?, disabled?, comment?, owner?}], active: ["<owner>/<port>/<proto>[\|<sport>\|<dest>]"], migration?: {at, notes: [{text, args}], dismissed?}}` — `port` 0 is every port, `port_to` makes a range `port..port_to`; `disabled` keeps a rule in the list without loading it; `source` is an address, a CIDR, `0/0` (the page shows *anywhere*) or `local networks` (the private, link-local and CGNAT ranges plus the interfaces' global networks, expanded per family into `local_networks`); a source or destination of one family limits the rule to it. `owner` marks an automatic rule: `web` (80, 443), `ssh` (22 while enabled), `rpc` (classic RPC's open ports), `hmip-ap` (9293, 9294, udp 43438, 43439 while HmIP-RF runs), `discovery` (always: udp and igmp to 224.0.0.0/24, udp to ff02::/16, udp 1900, udp from 43439 and 23272, udp 23272), `acme` (tcp 80 from anywhere while the certificate is ACME with HTTP-01; a certificate settings save syncs it), `addon:<id>` (an addon's opened ports). Owned rules are added when the owner starts wanting a port and removed when it stops; the user may edit, duplicate or delete them. An owned rule's `id` is derived from the owner and the port (the same at every start and after a remove and re-add), and an owner whose state is not known yet — `hmip-ap` before the radio detection has written its plan — keeps the rules the file is active for, so a boot where nothing changed rewrites neither the file nor a draft. `web`, `ssh` and `rpc` start from `local networks`, the rest from `0/0`; every automatic rule carries a comment saying why it is there. `draft` is the page's unapplied edit, `pending` `{started, deadline}` an applied draft waiting for its confirmation, `frame` per family the read-only rules INPUT always has before the list (loopback, conntrack, the ICMP a network needs, the DHCP client), `owners` the owners' labels. The migration notes are English templates with `{name}` values, which the page translates. |
| `PUT /firewall`, `DELETE /firewall/draft` **`system:write`** | `PUT {policy, rules}` sets the draft (validated; an id is given where missing; an owner is kept only on a port it owns) and answers the view; 409 while a confirm window is open. `DELETE` drops the draft. |
| `POST /firewall/apply`, `/confirm`, `/revert`, `/migration/dismiss` **`system:write`** | `apply` loads the draft through the helper with a **60 s confirm window**: the helper keeps the tables it replaced and puts them back by itself when no `confirm` comes (the page confirms over the new rules, so a rule that cuts it off reverts); `confirm` writes the draft to the file; `revert` puts the old tables back at once and keeps the draft. After a window that ran out the view's `error` says so. Automatic changes (an owner switching, the local networks changing) load without a window. `migration/dismiss` hides the conversion notice. Loading renders `iptables-restore` texts for INPUT and the `lite-input` chain only (`--noflush`; other chains stay), tested with `-t` first. At boot `occu-firewall.service` runs `occulited firewall load` before the network. |
| `GET /firewall/counters`, `POST /firewall/counters/reset` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | `{at, since, known, rules: {<id>: {packets, bytes}}, policy: {ipv4: {packets, bytes}, ipv6}}` — each rule's packets and bytes since the last load, both families and all of a rule's lines (a `local networks` rule is several) summed, LOG lines left out; the policy's count is what reached the end of INPUT. Read through the helper's `firewall-counters` (`iptables-save -c`) and laid onto the loaded rules in order (the draft while a confirm window is open); `known: false` when the tables do not hold those lines. `reset` loads the rules again, which starts every counter at zero; 409 while a confirm window is open. |
| `GET /firewall/listeners` **`system:read`** | `{listeners: [{proto, port, address, loopback, pid?, process?, unit?, cover: {ipv4?: {rule?, target, source?, owner?}, ipv6?}}], policy}` — every listening socket with its process and systemd unit (the helper maps socket inodes to pids through `/proc/<pid>/fd`), and per family the first rule covering it or, without one, the policy. A rule narrowed by a source port or a destination does not count as covering. |
| `GET /firewall/addons`, `PUT /firewall/addons/{id}/ports` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | The Addons page's port switches: `[{id, name, mode, ports: [{port, proto?, tls?, label?, listening, held_by?, owner_unknown?, open}]}]` for every installed addon that declares ports (the stored policy's runtime block merged with the addon's stored manifest). **`listening`** (occulited B-31) is true when a socket on the port and protocol (`tcp`/`tcp6` are `tcp`; a port declared without `proto` takes either) is held by a process in the addon's own unit `addon-<id>.service`, as the helper's socket owners say; **`held_by: {process, unit}`** names another holder (RedMatic's `node-red` on a port openccu-loom declares) with `listening: false`; when the owner cannot be read (the helper does not answer), a socket on the port counts as before and **`owner_unknown: true`** says so. Known limit: an addon whose daemon runs outside its unit (a root addon started by the boot's `init` pass, openccu-lite B-157) shows as held by that unit - true, and it points at the real problem. `PUT {"open": [8883]}` validates against the declaration (422 `invalid` otherwise), stores `open_ports` in the addon's policy and the firewall follows at once: an owned rule `addon:<id>` per open port, from `0/0`, its comment the port's label. An uninstall removes them. Answers the list. |
| `GET /remote-access`, `PUT /remote-access` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | Classic CCU RPC. `{classic: {plain, tls, auth: none\|password, user?, password_set}, ports: [{port, tls, interface, process, backend, open, running}], hs485d, rules}` — plain is 2001 (BidCos-RF), 2010 (HmIP-RF), 9292 (VirtualDevices), TLS 42001, 42010, 49292, and 2000/42000 (BidCos-Wired) where hs485d runs; `running` says the interface process listens on its loopback port; `rules` are classic RPC's owned firewall rules (`owner: rpc`, from `local networks` by default). `firewall: {ports: [{port, proto, target, rule?, source?, owner?}], hint}` is what the confirmed rules do with each port whose switch is on (IPv4, the first enabled rule for it or the policy — the same verdict as the access points' `firewall`), and `hint` is `closed` (no port on), `open` (every open port accepted) or `blocked` (an open port is not accepted); `null` without the rule file. The page shows it instead of the rule table. `PUT {classic: {plain, tls, auth}}` writes the userfs markers `/etc/config/classicRpcPlain` and `classicRpcTLS` through the helper (`S50lighttpd` turns them into the sockets); `auth: password` needs a pair first (422), `none` removes it. A change of the switches **restarts lighttpd 1.5 s after the answer** (a reload would keep a socket that goes); the firewall follows at once. the view carries `lite: {available, streams: {per_token, total, open}, buffer: {seconds, events}}` — read-only: lite-rpc is always on. `public: {enabled, account, available}` is the Control app's public mode; `PUT {public: {enabled, account}}` (alone or beside `classic`) switches it, in force at once, written to `auth.public` in `occulited.json`; the account is a name of the ladder's shape, an existing account above operate is refused (422). Without a session the App's paths then answer as that principal (`GET /api/auth/v1/state` says `public: true`); the warning `app-public` is listed while it is on. |
| `PUT /remote-access/classic-password` **`system:write`** | `{user, password}` or `{user, generate: true}`: the pair lighttpd checks with basic auth, realm `theRealm`, for every client but the loopback (as a CCU with authentication on). User: 1–32 letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`; a typed password at least 12 characters on one line; a generated one 32 characters, answered **once** as `password` beside the view and never again. Stored as `<user>:<SHA-512-crypt>` in `/etc/config/classic-rpc.htpasswd` (root 0600, through the helper); lighttpd is reloaded, which clears its auth cache. The plaintext is not kept or logged. |
| `GET /time`, `PUT /time` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | `{tz, zone, ntp_servers, has_ntp, now}`; `PUT {zone?, ntp_servers?}` — zone name into `/etc/config/TZ` + `updateTZ.sh` + `hwclock` + `SetInterfaceClock`; NTP into `/etc/config/ntpclient` + chrony restart. `GET /time/zones` lists IANA zones with country names. `POST /time/clock {time}` sets the clock by hand. **On a container** the clock is the host's (no chrony, `settimeofday` is EPERM unprivileged): `ntp_servers` and `/time/clock` answer `501 host-managed`, the zone is still set. |
| `GET /leds`, `PUT /leds {disabled}` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | The onboard (board) LEDs from `/var/hm_mode`; the `disableOnboardLED` marker, applied at once. The page's switch is on System → Status LED; the RPI-RF-MOD's RGB LED is not one of these (below). |
| `GET /ssh`, `PUT /ssh {enabled}` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | `{enabled, running}` — the `sshEnabled` marker and whether sshd runs, the latter from the service manager (`systemctl` on the systemd product), not from `/run/sshd.pid`, which is root-only and unreadable for the unprivileged daemon. `PUT` writes or removes the marker, re-applies the firewall (port 22 follows it) and restarts or stops sshd **through the service manager**, so systemd's idea of the unit and the marker stay in step. `POST /ssh/password {password}` sets root's password: the daemon itself runs `mkpasswd -m sha512 -s` as its own unprivileged user, the password on stdin, never in an argument - hashing needs no privilege and does not go through the privilege helper, whose program list has no `mkpasswd` (until openccu-lite task 302 it was asked of the helper, which refused it, and the route failed on every system). The hash is written by an operation of the privilege helper's own, which
replaces the password field of the `root:` line of `/etc/config/shadow` as root and leaves every
other field and line as it was: the file is 0640 root:root and the unprivileged daemon neither
reads nor rewrites it, which is why the route did not work at all before. At least 8
characters; a hash that is not a crypt hash is refused at the boundary, and when `mkpasswd` fails or answers nothing usable the answer is 400 `invalid` and the old password stays. **It asks for the user's own password every time** (see the keys below): without a confirmed ticket for `/api/system/v1/ssh/password` in `X-Occulite-Confirm` the answer is 403 `confirm-required`; a token is not asked. |
| `PUT /ssh/key-only {on}` **`system:write`** | Only key login for root. `{"on": true}` puts occulited's block (`PasswordAuthentication no`, `KbdInteractiveAuthentication no`) first into `/usr/local/etc/ssh/sshd_config` - the file the image's `sshd_config` includes at its end; sshd takes a keyword's first value and the image's own file sets neither - checked with `sshd -t` (a refused file is put back, nothing changes, `500`), then `systemctl try-reload-or-restart sshd`: new logins take a key only, open sessions stay. `{"on": false}` takes the block out (the file goes when nothing else is in it). Through the helper's `ssh-keyonly` operation (root's directory, 0700). **`409 no-key`** while root has no key at all. Answers the `GET /ssh` view, which carries **`key_only`** (absent when the helper could not say). Off by default; a fresh install stays off. |
| `GET /ssh/keys` **`system:read`**, `POST /ssh/keys {key}`, `DELETE /ssh/keys?fingerprint=` **`system:write`** | Root's public keys. `{managed: [key], other: [key]}`, where `key` is `{type, bits, comment?, fingerprint, options?}` and the fingerprint is `SHA256:…` as `ssh-keygen -l` prints it. `managed` are the keys of the section of `/usr/local/etc/ssh/authorized_keys` (what `/root/.ssh` links to) that the page keeps between `# BEGIN openccu-lite: …` and `# END openccu-lite`. `other` are the lines outside it, which the page lists and never changes. `POST` adds one key. It must be a modern one without options: `ssh-ed25519`, `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256/384/521`, `sk-ssh-ed25519@openssh.com`, `sk-ecdsa-sha2-nistp256@openssh.com`, or `ssh-rsa` of at least 3072 bits; one line, whose comment becomes the key's name. 422 `invalid` for anything else and 409 `exists` for a key the file holds already, both **before** the confirmation. Then it needs a confirmed ticket for `/api/system/v1/ssh/keys` in `X-Occulite-Confirm` (403 `confirm-required` without; a token is not asked). 201 with the key. `DELETE` removes a key of the section (404 for one outside it). Both are logged with who and from where. The helper writes the file: the section replaced, every other line as it was, a new file renamed over the old one, 0600 root. removing root's last key (the section's and the others counted) while only key login is on is `409 last-key`. |
| `GET /ssh/sessions` **`system:read`**, `DELETE /ssh/sessions/{id}` **`system:write`** | The SSH connections open now. `{sessions: [{id, user, tty?, from?, port?, since?, method?, key_type?, key_fingerprint?, own?}]}`. A session is an `sshd-session` process titled `user@pts/N` or `user@notty` (then without `tty`: a command or a file copy). `id` is its parent, the privileged `sshd-session`, which logged sshd's `Accepted … for … from … port … ssh2[: TYPE SHA256:…]` in this boot's journal; that line gives `from`, `port`, `method` (`publickey`, `password`), the key and `since`. `own` marks a session from the address the request came from. `DELETE` ends one of these sessions (SIGTERM to that process, through the helper, which checks that it is an `sshd-session`); 404 for any other id. |

### Status LED

occulited's controller (`internal/led`) owns the RPI-RF-MOD's RGB LED — the Charly, a CCU3 — once the
system is up; `hss_led` is not in the image. The boot's yellow (`S02InitRTC`) and the recovery
system's magenta stay the scripts'. The controller starts in **booting** (solid yellow, the same as
`S02InitRTC`'s) and takes over when `/var/status/startupFinished` exists and none of `multimacd`,
`rfd`, `hmipserver` is still activating — or after 15 minutes of uptime. **Shutdown** (yellow, fast)
shows when `startupFinished` disappears (occu-leds.service's stop, the first step of a shutdown) and
when occulited stops. What the LED shows is a frame of kernel triggers, written through the privilege
helper's `led` operation only when it changes, at most 5 frames a second: a blink keeps running in
the kernel through a pause of the daemon. Every 10 s the triggers are read back; a changed LED is set
again once and logged once per start, and after the third difference within a minute it is left
until the controller's own next change (`conflict: true`).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /led` **`system:read`** | `{available, reason?, hardware: {kind?, leds, colors, patterns, brightness, max_brightness?}, pwr, config, defaults, fixed, error_states, warning_ids, internet_hosts}`. `hardware.brightness` (openccu-lite task 315) says the LED has levels - the RPI-RF-MOD driven over the kernel's software PWM, `max_brightness` 255 - so a colour may be any `#rrggbb` beside the seven names, `breathe` is offered, and `brightness`, `night.dim` and `fade` take effect; on an on/off LED (`max_brightness` 1: the radio adapter's own LED, an image before dev.39) a free colour shows as the nearest of the seven (a channel at 128 or more lights), `breathe` as `slow`, and the levels are ignored. **`hardware.kind`** (openccu-lite task 326) is where the RPI-RF-MOD sits, from `/var/hm_mode`'s `HM_HMIP_DEVTYPE`/`HM_HMRF_DEVTYPE`: `rpi-rf-mod` (the Pi's header), `hb-rf-usb`, `hb-rf-usb-2` or `hb-rf-eth` (a radio adapter). Levels, patterns and fades only where the LED is the header's PWM LED (`/sys/class/leds/rpi_rf_mod:red/device/driver` is `leds_pwm`, task 315); the adapter's LED (`rpi_rf_mod_led`, no parent device, though it reports `max_brightness` 255) is on/off with `max_brightness` 1 and without the pattern trigger - no `breathe`, no `double`, the `timer` blink - so each step is one USB control transfer or UDP packet as before task 315; an older image's `leds-gpio` LED is on/off too. The HB-RF-ETH's LED brightness is the board's own setting. `internet_hosts`: the hosts the `no-internet` check connects to (below), `[]` when there is none. `reason` when not available: `no-module` (the radio module has no LED: HmIP-RFUSB, HM-MOD-RPI-PCB — the `rpi_rf_mod:*` devices the overlay gives every Pi are never written then), `no-leds`, `virtual`, `container`, `hm-lgw` (LAN gateway mode: nothing is written), `detecting` (`/var/hm_mode` not written yet, probed again every 10 s). `patterns` lacks `double` when the kernel has no pattern trigger: the probe looks for it in the LED's trigger list and under `/sys/module`, then asks the privilege helper to load `ledtrig-pattern` (its `led-module` operation, one of a closed list of names - the daemon's unit has `ProtectKernelModules=` and sees neither `/lib/modules` nor modprobe, openccu-lite B-299); a stored `double` is then shown as `flash`. `pwr`: the Pi's red power LED can be the error light (a system without an RGB LED, `HM_LED_RED` naming `PWR`). |
| `PUT /led` **`system:write`** | Admin. The `config` ([config.md](config.md)); answers the `GET /led` shape. `422 invalid` for an unknown or repeated state, a colour or pattern that does not exist (a colour is one of the seven names or `#rrggbb`), `alternate` without a second colour on other channels, night times that are not `HH:MM`, `show` other than `errors`/`off`/`dimmed`, `dim` outside 1–100, `brightness` outside 10–100, a locate that does not light or lasts outside 10–3600 s, `external.max_duration_s` outside 60–86 400. A state the list lacks is put in at its default place; `off` is always solid; a hex colour that is exactly a name's value is answered as the name (`#0000ff` → `blue`), black as `off`; `brightness` 0, `dim` 0 and an absent `fade` (a file from before task 315) read as 100, 30 and on. Stored in `<state_dir>/led.json`. Also on a system without the LED (the power LED's switch). |
| `GET /led/state` **`led`** or **`system:read`** | `{available, reason?, shown: {color, pattern, color2?, over_normal?, background?, dim?, level?, source, id, detail?, since}, active: [{id, since, detail?, level?}], overrides: [{id, color, pattern, color2?, over_normal?, priority, night, until?, by, since}], locate?: {color, pattern, until}, preview?: {…}, night, booting, conflict, write_error?, pwr?: {enabled, error}, capabilities: {colors, patterns, brightness}}`. `background`: the colour the blink's dark phase shows because the look has `over_normal` and it is visible (below, *Blinking over the normal colour*); absent for dark. `dim`: the night's level in percent while what is shown is dimmed (task 315); absent by day and for the fixed states, locate and the test. `capabilities` carries `brightness` and `max_brightness` as `GET /led`'s `hardware` does. `source` is `fixed` (booting, shutdown), `preview`, `locate`, `state`, `override`, `normal`, `night` (dark in the night window) or `off` (switched off). `detail` names the units down or failed, the warning ids, the release on offer, the number of addon updates. `active` lists every active state, also the ones below what is shown. |
| `POST /led/override` **`led`** | `{id?, color, pattern?, color2?, over_normal?, duration_s?, priority?: low\|normal\|high, night?: respect\|ignore}` → `200` the state plus `override`. `color` is a name or `#rrggbb`, `pattern` any of the patterns - `breathe` included (task 315); on an on/off LED both fall back as `GET /led` says. `over_normal: true` (optional) blinks over the normal colour instead of over dark, as a state can; dropped for `solid` and `alternate`. An older occulited refuses the field (`400`, unknown field). `id` defaults to the caller (`token:<name>`, the user); an id another caller set is `403` unless the caller has `system:write`. `duration_s` defaults to 300 and is capped at `external.max_duration_s`; `0` is *until cleared* (RedMatic-LED's semantics; `422` when `external.allow_until_cleared` is off). Unknown fields are refused (`400`). At most 16 overrides (`409 limit`); 2 requests per second per caller, refused ones counted (`429 rate-limited`). Overrides live in memory: a restart of occulited ends them. |
| `DELETE /led/override`, `DELETE /led/override/{id}` **`led`** | The caller's own (no id) or the id — another caller's only with `system:write` (`403`); `404 not-found` when there is none. `DELETE /led/overrides` (`system:write`) ends them all. Answers the state. |
| `POST /led/locate`, `DELETE /led/locate` **`led`** | `{duration_s?}` (the body may be empty; default the configured duration, at most 3600): the locate look (white, fast) above every state, the night and the switch included. |
| `POST /led/preview`, `DELETE /led/preview` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{color, pattern?, color2?, over_normal?}` for 10 s: the page's *Test*. |

Every write answers `501 unsupported` on a system without the LED (the configuration aside), and every
route `501` on a daemon without the controller.

**Blinking over the normal colour.** A state's look, an override and the preview may carry
`over_normal: true` (opt-in, off by default; not written while off): for `slow`, `fast`, `flash` and
`double` the pattern's dark phase shows the colour of the `normal` state instead of dark — yellow `slow`
over normal blue toggles yellow and blue every 500 ms, `flash` shows blue with a short yellow flash
every 2 s. It is kept only for those four patterns (dropped, not refused, for `solid`, `alternate` and
`off`; never on `normal` itself, `locate` or the `external` row). It follows the normal state's colour
(its first colour, steady, whatever normal's own pattern), and takes effect only while the LED is
switched on, outside the night window, with normal lit and in another colour than the blink —
otherwise the look blinks over dark exactly as without it, so a state in normal's own colour never
disappears (the page does not offer the option then). The frame: the blink colour's own channels
blink in phase, the normal colour's own channels in the opposite phase (the timer trigger with on and
off swapped, started when the first on phase ends; for `double` the complementary pattern string, or
the swapped `flash` timing without the pattern trigger), and channels both colours light stay on —
cyan over blue keeps blue lit and flashes green.

**The states**, the first active and enabled one wins. Above the list and fixed: `shutdown`,
`booting`, `preview`, `locate`. *booting* (solid yellow, as `S02InitRTC` leaves it) shows only until the
radio is known: until the radio run has written `/run/occulite/radio/render.json`, or - on an
image without it - until one of `multimacd`, `rfd`, `hmipserver`, `hs485d` has left `inactive`, or the
boot has finished (`startupFinished`, e.g. occulited restarted later), and at most for 15 minutes of uptime. From then on the list shows, `interfaces-starting` first during a normal
start - not after the addons any more. **Before the controller's first step** (it starts with the daemon, about 15 s after the daemon on a Raspberry Pi 3) the boot scripts own an RPI-RF-MOD's LED as on a CCU3: `S02InitRTC`'s solid yellow, then, from the network's start, `eQ3StartNetwork`'s slow blue blink ("the network is being set up"). On the Charly a boot therefore shows solid yellow for a second or two, the slow blue blink for some 20 s, then `interfaces-starting` (openccu-lite task 158, filmed 2026-10-03); a shutdown shows the fast yellow blink last. A normal start is still never red: a unit that is `activating`
is starting, not down, and until the boot has finished (`/var/status/startupFinished`, the gate of
`occu-leds.service`) a planned unit still `inactive` is waiting for its turn and counts as starting;
without the radio run's plan such a unit is neither starting nor down until then. `startupFinished`
disappearing is still the shutdown. The list, in its default order (the order is the configuration's):

| id | active when | default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `radio-down` | `multimacd`, `rfd` or `hmipserver` loaded and not active — failed, stopped, waiting for its automatic restart — for 15 s; a unit switched off on the Services page (masked) is not down | red, solid |
| `no-network` | `/var/status/hasLink` or `hasIP` missing for 15 s | yellow, fast |
| `service-failed` | another service unit `failed` for 15 s (`systemctl list-units`, every 10 s); addon units only with `addon_units` | red, slow |
| `storage-replace` | the Status page warning `storage`, variant `replace`, unsilenced | red, double |
| `radio-duty-cycle`, `radio-carrier-sense` | the radio's load is above one of the row's three levels (openccu-lite task 316): the highest duty cycle, or carrier sense, among the interfaces of the radio sampler's last poll (`GET /radio/health`'s sampling, once a minute and on the module's reports; no poll of the LED's own) is above a level's `threshold` (percent, strictly), and stays at a level until the value falls to the threshold less 5 points (the hysteresis); the highest active level wins. The look is the active level's (`config.radio`), not the row's: colour, animation and whether it blinks over the normal colour (`over_normal`, the page's *overlay*) or instead of it. The detail names the interface and the value (`HmIP-RF 63 %`); `shown.level` and `active[].level` say which level (1-3). Not held, not an error state (dark at night unless dimmed) | **off**; levels 25/50/75 % (duty cycle) and 5/10/20 % (carrier sense): yellow breathing over normal, `#ff4000` slow over normal, red fast |
| `interfaces-starting` | one of `multimacd`, `rfd`, `hmipserver`, `hs485d` is `activating` and not waiting for its automatic restart (and, during the boot, a planned one not started yet) - at the boot and whenever one is restarted; loaded, not masked, and needed by the radio plan (the `<unit>.enabled` markers, as for `radio-down`); the detail names the units, e.g. `rfd, hmipserver`; not held, not an error state (dark at night) | magenta, double, **over the normal colour** (solid blue with a double magenta blink) |
| `external` | the best override: the highest priority, the newest within it | the override's |
| `status-warning` | any other Status page warning no administrator silenced, except the ids in `warnings_off` — the warnings tracker's last evaluation (every five minutes and at every read of the page) | yellow, slow |
| `system-update` | the release feed offers a newer release | cyan, slow |
| `addon-update` | an installed addon has an update | cyan, double, **off** |
| `no-internet` | `hasIP`, and no TCP connection to a host the system talks to anyway: the host of each http(s) URL of the addon catalogue's index (`catalog.urls` while the catalogue is on; the bundled `file://` copy has none), then the ACME directory's host when the certificate mode is `acme`. The first that answers counts (3 s each, every 5 min, only while this state is on); no such host, or `/etc/config/internetCheckDisabled`, counts as reachable | blue, fast, **off** |
| `normal` | nothing above is active | blue, solid |

**Night** (`night`, in the system's zone from `/etc/config/timezone`, `to` before `from` across
midnight): `show: errors` keeps `radio-down`, `service-failed` and `storage-replace`, `off` keeps only
the fixed states; `normal` is dark and an override shows only with `night: ignore`. `show: dimmed`
(task 315) hides nothing. Whatever the night still shows - a state, an override, normal - is dimmed to
`night.dim` percent of the brightness (default 30; 100 = not dimmed) over PWM; the fixed states, locate and the
test are never dimmed, and an on/off LED ignores it. **Brightness and fades** (task 315, PWM): `brightness`
(10–100 %) is the LED's overall level; a colour's own shades sit below it, through a gamma curve of 2.2 (a
channel value of 128 is about a fifth of the duty cycle and looks half as bright; a lit channel is never driven
below 3 % of the maximum, where the timer's jitter would show as shimmer). With `fade` (default on) a change of
look cross-fades over 300 ms: each channel glides from the level it shows to the new look's first level, then the
new look starts. **Switched off**
(`enabled: false`): dark, the fixed states still show. **The power LED** (`pwr_error_light`, a system
without an RGB LED): `PWR` blinks (100/100 ms) while an enabled error state is active and gets its
`HM_LED_RED_MODE2` trigger back — `none` with `disableOnboardLED` — when it clears.

**Frames.** The helper's `led` operation takes `[{led, trigger, delay_on?, delay_off?, pattern?,
start_after_ms?, brightness?, fade?}]`, at most four, for `rpi_rf_mod:red|green|blue` and `PWR` under `/sys/class/leds`
(an entry that resolves outside `/sys` is refused), the triggers `none`, `default-on`, `timer`
(10–10 000 ms), `pattern` (bounded pairs) and the board LEDs' `heartbeat`, `mmc0`, `mmc1`, `input`,
`actpwr`; `brightness` (0–255) is the level written after `none` (the steady level) or `timer` (the level the
blink lights to); `fade` a one-shot pattern the helper runs first (`repeat 1`, waited for, at most 2 s). Every LED of
a frame goes to `none` first - except one that already runs the pattern trigger and gets a new pattern, whose
string replaces the running one at once, without a dark instant - then the triggers are set back to back, so the
channels of a mixed colour start together. A pattern string goes to `hr_pattern` (the hrtimer: exact milliseconds,
no drift between channels) where the kernel has it, else to `pattern`.

**Over PWM with the pattern trigger (task 315)** every look is a cycle of phases rendered per channel: a channel
whose level is the same in every phase is `none` plus its brightness, every other one a hard-step `hr_pattern`
string with the same durations on every channel - so the channels stay in step and a yellow blink has no red or
green fringe (the timer trigger rounded each channel to the kernel's 32 ms timer-wheel buckets). `slow` is
`L 500 L 0 0 500 0 0`, `fast` 100/100, `flash` 100/1900, `double` `L 150 L 0 0 150 0 0 L 150 L 0 0 1550 0 0`,
`alternate` the two colours' levels in two 500 ms phases, `breathe` eight 250 ms glides along a cosine from the
dark (or background) level to the colour and back; a background (`over_normal`) is the dark phases' levels. **On an
on/off LED, or without the pattern trigger**, the frames are the kernel's timer trigger as before: `solid` is
`default-on` (or `none` plus a brightness below the maximum), `slow` a 500/500 ms timer, `fast`
100/100, `flash` 100/1900, `double` the pattern `1 150 1 0 0 150 0 0 1 150 1 0 0 1550 0 0` at the level
(`ledtrig-pattern` is loaded at the probe through the helper's `led-module` operation, and once more on first use should it be missing), `alternate` two timers half a period apart, as `hss_led`
did; `breathe` is `slow`. The fork's `lite-status-led` shows the shutdown pattern from occu-leds.service's stop and yellow,
slow (*not supervised*) from occulited's `ExecStopPost`, for when the controller is not there.

### Certificate

The system's TLS certificate: S50lighttpd's self-signed one, or one from an ACME CA (Let's Encrypt,
ZeroSSL, a LAN CA such as step-ca) that occulited issues with lego as a library and renews twice a
day when fewer than 30 days — or less than half the lifetime — remain. The state is `<state-dir>/acme/` (config.md); the live file
stays `/etc/config/server.pem`, written through the privilege helper's own operation as
