`root:certs 0640`, lighttpd reloaded, the confined addons of the `certs` group restarted
(security.md). The user page is [tls-acme.md](https://github.com/hobbyquaker/openccu-lite/blob/main/docs/tls-acme.md).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /certificate` **`system:read`** | `{settings, current, current_error?, issued, live_is_issued, last, running, renew_below_days, next_check?, warning?, providers}`. `settings` is the stored configuration **without its secrets**: `{mode: self-signed\|acme\|manual, directory: letsencrypt\|letsencrypt-staging\|zerossl\|custom, directory_url, ca_root, email, eab_kid, eab_hmac_set, names: [], challenge: http-01\|dns-01, dns_provider, dns_credentials: {field: value} (the non-secret fields only), dns_secrets_set: {field: bool}}`. `current` describes the leaf of the live file — `{subject, issuer, issuer_cn?, issuer_org?, names, ips?, not_before, not_after, self_signed, days_left, chain, serial, fingerprint}` — read through the helper (the certificate blocks only, never the key); `issuer_cn` and `issuer_org` (the issuer's CN and its first O, omitted when empty) are what the pages show, with the full DN `issuer` as tooltip, and `issued` and the `POST /certificate/inspect` preview carry them too; `null` with `current_error` when there is none; `managed` (with `managed_mode`, `acme` or `manual`, and `managed_by`, the issuer) when the marker `/etc/config/server.pem.managed` beside the file says occulited installed it (the `server.pem.acme` beside it is still written and, alone, reads as mode `acme`). `pending` is the key + CSR made on the system and not yet answered by a certificate, `{algorithm, fingerprint, cn, sans, org?, created, csr}` (the request's PEM), or `null`. `issued` is the certificate this service last obtained, same shape; `live_is_issued` compares the two fingerprints. `last` and `running` are attempts: `{kind: test\|issue\|renew, started, finished?, ok, error?, warning?, directory, names, run_id?, installed, restarted_addons?, certificate?}` (`warning`: the run succeeded but the lifetime is under 48 hours) — `running` while one is in flight (the page polls this route), `last` afterwards (kept in `acme/last.json`, without lines). **The attempt's lines are in the journal**: one entry per line with `OCCULITE_RUN=acme`, `OCCULITE_RUN_ID=<run_id>`, `OCCULITE_RUN_KIND`, `OCCULITE_RUN_NAMES`, identifier `acme`, `warning` for a warning line and `err` for the failure; lego's own lines carry `OCCULITE_RUN_SOURCE=lego` (a `[WARN]` of lego's is a warning). The page reads them with `GET /log?run=<run_id>`. **No secret reaches the journal**, which can be forwarded to a syslog server: every line - and `error` in the attempt and in occulited's log - passes a redaction first, which replaces the EAB HMAC and every value stored under a field any DNS provider marks secret with `[redacted]`, as well as a PEM private key, a bearer credential and `token=`/`password=`/`api_key:`-shaped values. A `last.json` of an older binary with lines has them written into the journal at the next start, under a run id of the attempt's start, and loses them. `warning` is `expiring` (fewer than 14 days left; under `manual` too, since nobody renews that), `last-attempt-failed` (ACME only) or `self-signed` (mode ACME or manual but the live file is S50's), for the Status card. `providers` lists the DNS-01 providers the binary carries: `[{id, name, fields: [{key, label, secret?, optional?, placeholder?}], note?}]`. `suggested_names`: the default ACME names `<host>.<domain>, <host>` (or `<host>` without a domain). The renewal also orders when the issued certificate's names differ from the settings, once per set of names. Answering this route first applies a domain change not yet seen to names that follow the old domain's default (see `GET /network`), so the page never shows stale ones. |
| `PUT /certificate/settings` **`system:write`** | Admin. The `settings` shape above with the secrets as plain fields: `eab_hmac` and, inside `dns_credentials`, the provider's secret fields. **An empty secret keeps the stored value** (for the same EAB key id, the same provider), so a page that never saw the value cannot wipe it by saving; a changed provider drops the old provider's credentials. `names` are trimmed and lower-cased; an IP address, a name that is not a DNS name, a wildcard with HTTP-01, a custom directory that is not `https`, a CA root that is not PEM, ZeroSSL without EAB, a provider field the provider does not have: `422 invalid` with the reason. Answers like `GET`. Saving does not issue anything. An ACME save with blank `names` stores `suggested_names` instead of answering 422. |
| `POST /certificate/test` **`system:write`** | Admin. Runs the whole flow — account, challenge, order — against **Let's Encrypt's staging** directory for the two Let's Encrypt presets, and against the configured directory itself for ZeroSSL and a custom CA, and installs nothing; the settings are validated as if the mode were ACME, so a system still on self-signed can test first. `202` with the `GET` answer, `running` set; `409 busy` while another attempt runs; `422 invalid`. |
| `POST /certificate/issue` **`system:write`** | Admin. The flow for real: the chain and key stored under `acme/`, assembled into the live file, installed, lighttpd reloaded, the certs-group addons restarted (`restarted_addons` in the attempt). Needs mode `acme` (`422` otherwise). `202`/`409` as above. |
| `POST /certificate/renew` **`system:write`** | Admin. The same as issue, by hand — what the timer does on its own below 30 days. |
| `POST /certificate/self-signed` **`system:write`** | Admin, synchronous. Sets the mode to `self-signed` (from ACME or manual), removes `/etc/config/server.pem` and its markers through the helper and reloads lighttpd — `S50lighttpd`'s `check_certificate` generates a new self-signed certificate on that reload — then restarts the certs-group addons. `{status, restarted_addons, hsts_disabled, hsts_clearing_until?}` — `hsts_disabled` is `true` when HSTS was on and the installer switched it off in the same reload: a browser that has seen the header would refuse the self-signed certificate. That is the clearing state (`max-age=0` until `hsts_clearing_until`, see *HTTPS redirect and HSTS*), not no header; over the new self-signed certificate it reaches no browser, so the Certificate page switches HSTS off first, as a step of its own, while HSTS is on. The ACME settings, account and last issued chain stay under `acme/`, the manual key and chain under `tls/`, for the next switch. |
| `PUT /certificate/manual` **`system:write`** | Admin, synchronous. The user's own certificate. **Multipart** with the parts `certificate`, `chain` and `key` — each a file or a field, each **PEM or DER** (DER is detected by the first byte, `0x30`; several DER certificates back to back are a chain) — or **JSON** `{certificate, chain, key}` with the PEM strings. The chain is optional and may be appended to the certificate instead (any order: the leaf is put first, its issuers after it); the key is optional when the system generated one (`pending`) and the certificate was signed for that request. Keys: PKCS#8, PKCS#1 or SEC 1; an **encrypted key is refused** ("decrypt it first" — there is no password field, the system would have to store it). Refused with `422 invalid` and the reason: no certificate; no key at all; a key that is not the certificate's (both public-key fingerprints named, and whether the key given or the system's pending key was compared); an expired or not-yet-valid certificate (with its dates and the system's clock); material that does not parse. **A missing intermediate is a warning, not a refusal** (`warning`: the leaf's issuer is not in the chain). On success the live file is written through the helper with the marker naming mode `manual`, lighttpd reloaded, the certs-group addons restarted, the mode set to `manual`, the key and chain kept under `tls/`, and a pending request whose key this was is cleared. `{status, result: {certificate, restarted_addons, warning?, key_from: upload\|pending}, restarted_addons, warning}`. `409 busy` while an ACME attempt runs. |
| `POST /certificate/key` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{algorithm: p256\|p384\|rsa2048 (default p256), cn, sans: [], org}` → generates a private key and a PKCS#10 request (the common name as the subject and the first SAN, every other name a SAN, an address an IP entry, the organisation when given) and keeps both under `tls/`; an earlier pending key is replaced (the page asks first). Answers `{pending, csr, status}` — `pending` as in `GET`, `csr` the request's PEM; **the key itself never leaves the system**. `422 invalid` for an empty common name or an unknown algorithm. |
| `GET /certificate/csr` **`system:read`** | Admin. The pending request as a download: `Content-Type: application/pkcs10`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<cn>.csr"`. `404 not-found` when none has been generated. |
| `POST /certificate/inspect` **`system:write`** | Admin. The parsed preview the page shows under a PEM field as soon as its text parses, nothing saved: the same body as `PUT /certificate/manual` (multipart or JSON, any part may be empty) → `{certificate?, certificate_error?, chain?, chain_warning?, key?: {algorithm, fingerprint}, key_error?, matches?, pending_matches?, validity?}` — `certificate` in the `current` shape, `chain` the count, `matches` whether the key given belongs to the certificate given, `pending_matches` whether the system's pending key does, `validity` the expiry refusal's text. The ACME page uses it for the CA root field as well. |
| `GET /.well-known/acme-challenge/{token}` *(open, outside `/api`)* | The HTTP-01 challenge: lighttpd hands the path to occulited on port 80 (and the fork's HTTPS redirect leaves it alone); the answer is the key authorisation of a token of an order in flight, `404` for any other token, `405` for anything but `GET`/`HEAD`. |
| `GET /upnp/basic_dev.cgi` *(open, outside `/api`)* | The UPnP `Basic:1` device description, in place of ssdpd and OpenCCU's Tcl CGI of the same path (kept so a cached `LOCATION` and NetFinder still find it). No session — it is what the SSDP announcement carries over the air anyway: `deviceType` and `URLBase` built from the scheme and host the request came in on (lighttpd's `X-Forwarded-Proto`), `presentationURL` the web UI at a name the live certificate covers, so a double click ends on it without a certificate warning: `http://` + the first covered of the host the request came in on, the system's `<host>.<domain>`, its host name; with none covered the request's host — **always plain `http://`, the CCU's shape** (B-245: given `https://<name>/`, Windows' network view downloaded the page into its IE cache and opened the file instead of the browser; lighttpd's redirect takes the browser from `http://<name>/` to `https://<name>/`) — a port is dropped, the certificate's names are read at most once a minute (found 2026-09-23), `friendlyName` *openccu-lite - `<hostname>`*, `manufacturer` and `modelName` *openccu-lite*, the serial and `UDN` `uuid:upnp-BasicDevice-1_0-<serial>` (`/var/board_sgtin`, else `/var/board_serial`, else the hostname). No `serviceList`: an empty one made control points ask for a SCPD that does not exist. `text/xml`, every text node escaped; `GET` and `HEAD`, `405` for anything else. The fork's HTTPS and FQDN redirects leave `/upnp/` alone, as they do the challenge path. |

### Trust stores

Four named trust stores behind one API, so a fifth is a new entry and not new code paths.
**system**: the image's `ca-certificates` bundle as the system builds it into `/etc/ssl/certs` at boot (the fork's
`lite-ca-certificates`), used by addons and every other program; an addition is a file `occulite-<id>.crt` under
`/usr/local/share/ca-certificates`, a removal a `!<name>` line in `/usr/local/etc/ca-certificates.conf` (the image's
certificate stays listed, *distrusted*, and can be restored) — both on the userfs, written through the privilege helper,
followed by the rebuild script, so they survive reboots and image updates. **occulited**: occulited's own base set —
`trust.BaseNames`, six roots read from the image's bundle (USERTrust ECC/RSA and Sectigo's E46/R46 for GitHub and eQ-3,
ISRG X1/X2 for githubusercontent and Let's Encrypt), so the image alone updates them — plus anchors with the purpose
`occulited`, minus removed base entries (remembered in `trust-anchors.json`'s `removed`). **oidc**: the anchors,
unchanged. **acme**: the anchors trusted for a private ACME directory; the CA root the Certificate page kept in its
settings moved here once at start (`acme.Service.TakeCARoot`), and `PUT /certificate/settings` with a `ca_root` adds to
this store instead of storing it. The pools: system = the bundle file (read by occulited itself, not Go's cached system
pool); occulited = base + anchors − removed; oidc = system + oidc anchors; acme = occulited + acme anchors. **occulited's
own outbound clients** (the catalogue, GitHub's releases, eQ-3's firmware index, an addon's own update URL, lego's
directory connection) trust the occulited store alone: `trust.Store.HTTPClient` is a round tripper that rebuilds its
pool on a change and records a handshake lost to an unknown authority as a *failure* — host, the CA the presented chain
ends in, the chain, and the system store's certificate that would verify it (`candidate`). Strict, by decision: the
call fails until an administrator adds the CA. The Status page's warning `trust-ca` (id; variant the hosts, comma-
separated, `Lists`; params `{store, hosts, issuer, candidate}`; href `/system/trust#<store>`) names it, and the page
offers the one-click copy. A host that answers again, or whose candidate was added, clears its record.

**Pins (task 232; the maintainer, 2026-10-03: only OIDC and ACME, nothing else).** The **oidc** and **acme** stores hold
*pins* beside their certificates: the SHA-256 of a certificate's public key (its SubjectPublicKeyInfo, base64 as browsers
and `curl --pinnedpubkey` show it; a renewal with the same key keeps the pin), several per purpose (a backup pin for a
key rollover, entered as a bare hash before the key is in use). A connection of a purpose with pins must present a chain
one of the pins matches — any certificate of it, the leaf or a CA — or it fails with the fingerprint and key hash the
server presented, recorded as a *pin failure* (one per purpose and host) with the Status page's warning **`trust-pin`**
(variant `<purpose>:<host>`, severity error, params `{purpose, host, subject, fingerprint, spki}`, href
`/system/trust#<purpose>`) and the page's *Re-pin*. **Per pin, the mode**: `with-ca` (the default) runs the CA
validation of the purpose's pool as well; `only` lets the pin alone vouch — no CA validation, no name check (a self-signed
identity provider, a step-ca without a trusted root; the page asks in red). When several pins match, an `only` one wins.
The pins apply to every connection of the purpose: the OIDC client's discovery, token and userinfo calls; lego's
directory connection — the ACME *Test* of a Let's Encrypt preset goes to the staging directory, whose keys differ, so
pins fit a private directory. The verification is `trust.Verifier` (the pool plus the pins), used by the store's HTTP
client for both purposes; the OIDC settings' test is quiet (no record), the login and the ACME runs record. Kept in
`trust-anchors.json` as `pins` and `pin_failures`. Nothing else is pinned, by decision; the code takes another purpose
as one more entry in `trust.PinPurposes`.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /trust` **`system:read`** | `{stores: [{id, editable, certificates: [{id, subject, issuer, not_before, not_after, fingerprint, spki, ca, self_signed, names, expired, expires_soon, source: image\|added, origin?: oidc-settings\|acme-settings\|page\|copy, added?, added_by?, distrusted?, removable?}], pins?: [{id, purpose, spki, mode: with-ca\|only, subject?, not_after?, fingerprint?, added, added_by?}]}], pending: [{host, store, at, error, issuer, chain: [...], candidate?: {...}}], pin_failures: [{purpose, host, at, error, chain: [...]}]}` — the stores in the page's order system, occulited, oidc, acme; `editable` false for the system store without a helper or bundle (a development root). `id` = the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 (of the key hash for a pin); `spki` the public key's SHA-256, base64. `pins` only on the oidc and acme stores; `pin_failures` the connections whose chain matched none of their purpose's pins, the presented chain with each certificate's `spki`. |
| `POST /trust/{store}` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{pem}` (pasted, or an uploaded `.pem`/`.crt`) or `{der}` (an uploaded `.der`/`.cer`, base64; several DER certificates back to back are all taken; the bytes are parsed as uploaded — whitespace before the first certificate or after the last complete one is ignored, a certificate's own last byte never is, B-237). Every certificate of the text is added: a system addition writes the file and rebuilds the bundle (a distrusted image certificate is restored instead); an occulited one restores a removed base entry or becomes an anchor; oidc and acme anchors gain the purpose. Refused with 422 like `/oidc/trust` (`private_key`, `no_certificate`, `bad_certificate`, `expired`, `not_yet_valid`), `404 not_found` for an unknown store, `409 present` when the system store held everything already, `501 read_only` without a helper. `{added: [...], store: {...the store as it is now}}`. |
| `DELETE /trust/{store}/{id}` **`system:write`** | Admin. System: an added file goes, an image certificate is distrusted (`!<name>` line, rebuild); occulited: a base entry is remembered as removed, an anchor loses the purpose; oidc/acme: the anchor loses the purpose (and goes when it has none left). `404 not_found`; `422 invalid` for one that is distrusted already. `{store}`. |
| `POST /trust/{store}/{id}/restore` **`system:write`** | Admin. A distrusted system certificate or a removed occulited base entry is trusted again. `404` when it is not removed. `{store}`. |
| `POST /trust/{store}/{id}/copy` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{to}`: the certificate copied into another store — a copy, the stores independent afterwards (not a link); origin `copy`. `422 invalid` for the same store, `404` for an unknown store or id. `{added, store: {…the target}}`. A copy of a failure's candidate into its store clears the failure. |
| `GET /trust/{store}/{id}/pem` **`system:read`** | The certificate as PEM (`application/x-pem-file`, a download). |
| `POST /trust/{store}/pins/peer` **`system:write`** | Admin; `store` oidc or acme. `{url?}`: the chain the purpose's server presents, **read without verifying it** — the configured OIDC issuer or the ACME directory the settings point at, or the given `https://` URL (the form's, not saved yet) — for the administrator to compare fingerprints before pinning: `{url, host, chain: [{…the certificate's fields incl. spki, pem, pinned}], verified, error?, pin_only?}` — `pinned` whether its key is one of the purpose's pins already, `verified` whether the chain passes the purpose's verification as it stands (pool and pins), `pin_only` that a pin alone vouched. `422 invalid` without a configured server or for a plain-http one, `502 unreachable`, `404 not_found` for a store without pins. Nothing is trusted or pinned from it. |
| `POST /trust/{store}/pins` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{pem, mode?, replace?}` pins the public key of exactly one certificate (from `pins/peer`, or pasted: the provider's next certificate as a backup) and keeps its subject, validity and fingerprint for the display; `{spki, mode?, replace?}` pins a bare key hash (base64, or hex with or without colons as openssl prints it) — a backup pin for a key not in use yet. `mode` `with-ca` (default) or `only`; `replace` drops the purpose's other pins first (the page's *Re-pin*; a pin whose key the pending failure's chain carries clears that failure). `{pin, pins}`. `422 invalid` for a bad mode or hash, two certificates, `private_key`/`no_certificate`/`bad_certificate` as `/oidc/trust`, `409 present` when the key is pinned already, `404 not_found` for a store without pins. Applied to the running clients at once. |
| `DELETE /trust/{store}/pins/{id}` **`system:write`** | Admin. Forgets the pin; the purpose's last pin removed drops its pin failures too (nothing left to mismatch). `{pins}`; `404 not_found`. |

### HTTPS redirect and HSTS

The HTTP → HTTPS redirect and HSTS, in System → Certificate's HTTPS section. Both are markers on the userfs that
`S50lighttpd` reads at start and at every reload — the redirect one is upstream's, the HSTS one
openccu-lite's — and the markers *are* the settings: nothing is kept a second time.

**HSTS and the recovery system**. The recovery system and a system update's install
phase serve plain HTTP, and a way back to OpenCCU or a reset serves a self-signed certificate: a
browser that remembers HSTS for the system's name refuses them under that name for up to the max-age,
and removing the marker sends no header, so it clears nothing a browser already holds. The page asks
before `hsts` goes on and says so, with the system's address. Wherever the UI sends the browser into the
recovery — the power menu's *Reboot into the recovery system*, a reboot while an update is set to
install, the system update's install notice — it links to `http://<IP>/`, resolved when the question
opens: the host the browser uses when that is an IP literal, else the IPv4 address of the interface
with the default route from `GET /network` (another interface's when that one has none), an IPv6
address in brackets only without any IPv4, and the name with a warning when no address can be read.
The name follows as a second link only while `GET /https` answers `hsts: false` and not
`hsts_clearing`. The API itself is unchanged.

**Switching HSTS off clears.** A browser keeps its entry when the header
just stops; it forgets it when it sees `max-age=0` over the certificate it trusts, once per name. So
`hsts` off after on sends `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=0` for the previous max-age, at most
30 days: the marker holds `0`, and `/etc/config/hstsClearUntil` the Unix time that ends. The deadline
is a file of its own, not a second number in the marker: `S50lighttpd` has always read the marker as
digits only, so an older script would read `0 <epoch>` as a max-age of years, while a plain `0` is
harmless to every version. `S50lighttpd` sends no header once the time has passed; occulited removes
both files at its start, on `GET /https` and at every renewal check (one reload). The switch back to
self-signed and staging a system update that may be the way back to OpenCCU enter the same state.
The pages ask the user to open the system once by its name in every browser: the Certificate page's HTTPS section while
it clears, the Certificate page by switching HSTS off as a step of its own before the switch to
self-signed (`max-age=0` over the new self-signed certificate reaches no browser), and the Status
page beside a staged way back. **The default max-age is 7 days** (365 before); a stored value stays.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /https` **`system:read`** | `{redirect_https, hsts, hsts_max_age_days, hsts_clearing, hsts_clearing_until?, certificate, redirect_fqdn, redirect_fqdn_host, redirect_fqdn_target, redirect_fqdn_state, redirect_fqdn_reason?}`. `redirect_https`: `/etc/config/httpsRedirectEnabled` exists (every request on `:80` that is not `/.well-known/acme-challenge/` and not from the loopback is answered with a redirect to `https://`). `hsts`: `/etc/config/hstsEnabled` exists and does not hold `0`; `hsts_max_age_days` is its content (seconds) in whole days, `7` while off or when the file holds no number (`365` before). `hsts_clearing`: the marker holds `0` — HSTS is off, and lighttpd sends `max-age=0` until `hsts_clearing_until` (RFC 3339, from `/etc/config/hstsClearUntil`; absent when that file cannot be read, and then until the marker goes). A clearing whose deadline has passed is ended before the answer. `certificate` describes the live certificate for the page's rules — `{self_signed, managed, mode}` — or is `null` when the certificate service is not on the system or the file cannot be read. `501` when the system has no HTTPS configuration (development). **The bare host name redirected to `<host>.<domain>`**: `redirect_fqdn`: `/etc/config/fqdnRedirect` exists — lighttpd answers `GET`/`HEAD` for the bare host name (any case, with or without a port) outside `/api` and `/.well-known/acme-challenge/`, not from the loopback, with `302` to `https://<the name the file holds><path><query>`, on `:443` and, with `redirect_https`, on `:80` in one hop. `redirect_fqdn_target` is that name while on, the system's current `<host>.<domain>` while off, `null` without a domain; `redirect_fqdn_host` its first label (the host name without a target). `redirect_fqdn_state`: `active` (on, and the live certificate covers the target), `suspended` (on, but not redirecting — `S50lighttpd` uses the name only while the certificate covers it, `openssl x509 -checkhost` at every reload, so the install of a certificate for it brings the redirect back), `unavailable` (off, and it cannot be switched on) or `off`; `redirect_fqdn_reason` with `suspended` and `unavailable`: `no-domain`, `invalid-name`, `no-certificate`, `not-covered` (the certificate's DNS names, a wildcard for one label; the common name does not count). This route, `GET /network`, `GET /certificate`, a rename, occulited's start and every renewal check first rewrite a marker whose name is no longer the system's `<host>.<domain>` (one reload); without a domain it stays. |
| `PUT /https` **`system:write`** | Admin. `{redirect_https, hsts, hsts_max_age_days?, redirect_fqdn?}`. Writes or removes the markers through the helper (all under its `/etc/config/` prefix; the HSTS file holds `days × 86400`; `hsts_max_age_days` absent or `null` is 7 days). **`hsts: false` after `hsts: true` clears**: the marker becomes `0` and `/etc/config/hstsClearUntil` now plus the previous max-age, at most 30 days; while a clearing runs, `hsts: false` keeps its deadline and `hsts: true` ends it. Then one `systemctl reload lighttpd` (or the init script's reload) — none when nothing changed — and answers like `GET`. **Refused with `422 invalid`**: `hsts: true` while the live certificate is self-signed, or none can be read (a browser that has seen the header refuses an untrusted certificate for the whole max-age — the one way to lock yourself out; the page does not offer it either), and `hsts_max_age_days` outside 1…730 while `hsts` is on. A failed reload is a `500` with lighttpd's words; the markers stay as written. **`redirect_fqdn`** is optional: absent leaves `/etc/config/fqdnRedirect` as it is, so a client written for the three older fields does not switch it off. Switched on, the file gets the system's `<host>.<domain>` in lower case; **refused with `422 invalid`** and `detail.reason` (`no-domain`, `invalid-name`, `no-certificate`, `not-covered`) when there is no domain or the live certificate does not cover that name — a self-signed certificate that does is accepted. The refusal is for switching on only: a `PUT` that keeps it on while it is suspended goes through, with the target set to the system's current name. The file is written only when the switch or the name changes; one reload for the whole `PUT`. |

### HmIP local key mode

The HmIP network key kept on the system instead of at eQ-3's key server, so a radio swap needs no
internet. The switch writes `Network.Key` (and `Backbone.Key`) with `KeyServer.Mode=LOCAL` into
`/etc/config/crRFD/hmip_user.conf` (`0640` while it carries a key, `0644` without) - the only
hmipserver keys lite writes there - after copying the module's identity files
(`/etc/config/crRFD/data/<SGTIN>.ap/.apkx/.bbkx`) and that file aside into a snapshot, one per module
SGTIN under `<state-dir>/hmip-local-key/snapshots/` (`0700`, the files `0600`; kept until
discarded; a later switch on the same module keeps the older one). hmipserver writes a configured key
into the module at its start, so every switch restarts it. Every switch runs detached: the answer is
`202` with the status, the page polls `GET`, and a failure in the background lands in `error`. One
switch at a time, shared with the adapter exchange's retry and fresh start (`switching` then says
`retry` or `fresh-start`); none while a radio connection change or a flash runs. **One restart lock** covers everything that rewrites hmipserver's configuration and restarts it - the switches,
the exchange's retry and fresh start, and the device keys' apply (`POST /radio/hmip/device-keys/apply`):
it is held from the first write until the restart returned (not during the check that follows a
switch), and the second caller is refused naming the holder - a switch while the apply runs is
`409 local-key` `the device keys' apply is running`, the apply while a switch runs is `409` with
`a local key switch is running` (or `the adapter exchange is running`). **Every route is the
administrator's (`system:write`)**: the answer never carries a key, but switching the network's key
is not a read-only token's business. The page is `/system/keys#local-key`. Errors are `409 local-key`
with the reason in `message` for a switch that cannot be done now (a malformed key too, not a `422`),
`500 local-key` for a failure, `422 invalid-body` for a body that is no JSON object of the named
fields, `501 unsupported` without the service (development).

**The HmIP identity files are never changed without the user's word** (openccu-lite task 317, D-120):
every route that writes, moves, replaces or deletes `crRFD/data/<SGTIN>.ap`, `.apkx` or `.bbkx` - local
key mode on and off, the fresh start, the restore of a fresh-start snapshot, the way back after a module
move, a connection change that moves HmIP-RF, the device import and the backup restore - refuses a request
without `confirm: true` (`400 confirm`), and the destructive ones without the host name typed (`400
hostname`). **Identity copies:** before local key mode goes off, a fresh start, a snapshot restore and the
way back write or move those files, every module's identity files are copied into
`<state-dir>/hmip-local-key/identity-backups/<UTC time>-<reason>/` (0700, the files 0600; reasons
`local-key-off`, `fresh-start`, `restore`, `move-back`); a copy that fails changes nothing; the newest ten
stay. They are not listed by the API: the way back by hand should an action go wrong.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /radio/hmip/local-key[?devices=1]` **`system:write`** | `{available, sgtin?, enabled, source?, keyserver_mode, exchange_id, snapshots: [{sgtin, at, files, kind?}], revert_blocked?, override_active, override_since?, check?, switching?, error?, devices?}`, never a key. `available`: there is an HmIP module to switch (`sgtin`, from the last radio run's plan). `enabled`: `hmip_user.conf` carries `Network.Key` or `Network.Key.Base`; `source` then `entered`, `generated`, or `manual` for a key lite did not write. `keyserver_mode` as the file says it, `KEYSERVER_LOCAL` (the shipped template's) when unset. `exchange_id`: `/etc/config/hmip_address.conf` carries `accesspoint.exchange.id`, and hmipserver ignores a configured key. `snapshots` newest first; `kind: "fresh-start"` for a previous module's identity the adapter exchange's fresh start moved aside, `kind: "module-move"` with `choices` for the snapshot a connection change kept before moving HmIP-RF to another module (openccu-lite B-285; its files include the `<SGTIN>.dev` device files and `hmip_address.conf`). `revert_blocked` says why going back to eQ-3's key server is not possible now (off, no module in use, the only snapshot belongs to another module, no snapshot because the key was set by hand). `switching`: `on`, `off`, `override`, `retry`, `fresh-start` while one runs; `error`: the last switch's failure. `check` is the watch after a switch on: `{started, finished?, state, before, total, heard, unreachable: [address], quiet: [address], error?}` - hmipserver gets 3 min to answer after the restart (`waiting`, else `error`), then the devices that answered before the switch (access points left out; every radio device listed after the restart when hmipserver was silent before) are read every 30 s for 10 min (`running`); `failed` when most of them report unreachable or none is heard from again, else `ok`; `skipped` with no device to watch, `interrupted` when occulited restarted during it, `superseded` when a switch or the override cut it off (final at once, written before that switch's `202`; the cut-off check writes nothing more). A `failed` check while local key mode is on is the Status page's `hmip-local-key` warning (error; variant the check's start; `params` `{unreachable, total}`). `?devices=1` adds `devices`, the HmIP devices hmipserver lists (`-1` while it does not answer; the welcome step is for an empty network only). |
| `PUT /radio/hmip/local-key` **`system:write`** | `{mode, network_key?, backbone_key?, confirm, hostname?}` - local key mode on. **`confirm: true` is required** (openccu-lite task 317, D-120: hmipserver writes the module's identity files under the new key; `400 confirm` without), and for `mode: "generate"` - every paired device has to be taught in again - the host name typed as well (`400 hostname`), unless hmipserver lists no device (the welcome page's step on an empty network). `mode: "known"` takes the network key (32 hex digits; spaces, dashes and colons dropped, case ignored; never the example key `0102…0F10` or all zeros) and optionally the backbone key - without one `Backbone.Key` stays out, so the module keeps its own and the paired access points stay reachable; `mode: "generate"` generates both, 16 random bytes each (every HmIP device must be paired again). `409 local-key` when there is no HmIP module, local key mode is on already, `accesspoint.exchange.id` is set, a key is malformed (`network key: …` / `backbone key: …`), the mode is neither, or a switch, the device keys' apply, a connection change or a flash runs. In the background: the devices' reachability is read, the snapshot taken (a failure changes nothing), the keys written with `KeyServer.Mode=LOCAL` (every `.Base` line left from a hand edit goes), `source` recorded, hmipserver restarted, the check started. Logged with the source and the SGTIN. |
| `DELETE /radio/hmip/local-key` **`system:write`** | `{confirm: true, hostname}` (openccu-lite task 317, D-120: `400 confirm`, `400 hostname`; the devices taught in under the local key since have to be taught in again) - back to eQ-3's key server: the identity files in use are copied first (*identity copies* below), then the snapshot of the module in use goes back into `/etc/config/crRFD/data/`, the key lines of `hmip_user.conf` become the snapshot's (as a rule none, so `KeyServer.Mode` falls back to the template's) while every other line stays, the state (source, check, override) is cleared, hmipserver restarts. The snapshot stays until discarded. `409 local-key` with `revert_blocked` as the reason, or when a switch, the device keys' apply, a connection change or a flash runs. A check still running is `superseded` first; the clearing then takes it away with the rest (it stays, `superseded`, when the revert fails before the clearing). |
| `POST /radio/hmip/local-key/override` **`system:write`** | `{on}` - the key server for the next pairing: `on: true` writes `KeyServer.Mode=KEYSERVER_LOCAL`, `false` puts `LOCAL` back; hmipserver restarts either way. The override ends by itself once an install mode has come and gone (hmipserver's install mode read every 10 s), at the latest after 30 min; occulited's start resumes that watch. `409 local-key` when local key mode is off, or a switch, the device keys' apply, a connection change or a flash runs; asking for the state it is in already is a `202` that changes nothing. Like every switch it cuts off a check still running, which is `superseded` at once. The automatic end takes the same restart lock and tries again after 10 s while a switch, the apply, a connection change or a flash holds it off. |
| `DELETE /radio/hmip/local-key/snapshots/{sgtin}` **`system:write`** | Removes one module's snapshot (the SGTIN in any case; either kind) and answers the status with `200`. `409 local-key` when `{sgtin}` is not 24 hex digits or there is no snapshot of it (not `404`). The snapshot of the module in use may be discarded while local key mode is on; going back is then blocked. Logged. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/local-key/snapshots/{sgtin}/restore` **`system:write`** | `{confirm: true, hostname}` - puts a **fresh-start snapshot** back when local key mode is off (openccu-lite task 212; with it on, `DELETE /radio/hmip/local-key` is the way back, key lines and all): the previous module is in use again (the plan's SGTIN is `{sgtin}`), its `.ap/.apkx/.bbkx` return to `/etc/config/crRFD/data/` (root's for a moment, hmipserver's prep gives them back to it at its start), every other module's identity files in that directory - the identity the fresh start made for the refused module - move into a fresh-start snapshot of their own first (so hmipserver attempts no exchange onto the restored network), a marker of a rejected exchange goes, hmipserver restarts. `hmip_user.conf` is not touched. **The snapshot is consumed**: its files are the identity in use now, and restoring them again later would roll the network back behind the devices paired since. `400 confirm` / `400 hostname` (the name typed, case and surrounding space ignored, is not the system's - the status carries it as `hostname`) and nothing happens; `409 local-key` for a snapshot the switch made, no snapshot of `{sgtin}`, a module that is not in use ("put it back first"), local key mode on, no module, or beside a switch, a connection change or a flash. `202` with the status; `switching` says `restore` while the work runs. Logged at warning. |

### HmIP device keys

The devices' own keys from their stickers (the QR code `EQ01SG<SGTIN>DLK<key>`, or the printed
26-character KEY) in hmipserver's `/etc/config/crRFD/sgtin.map` (`SGTIN=KEY`, `0640`, hmipserver's
after its start), named by `SGTIN.LocalKey.MappingFile` in `hmip_user.conf`. With a device's key
there a plain install mode pairs it without eQ-3's key server. HmIP-RF reads the map when it starts,
so a change waits for its next start. **Every route needs `radio:keys`**; only the export answers a
key, and a session confirms it every time (`POST /api/auth/v1/ticket` with `confirm`). What a pairing
client without any of these scopes may know — the key-server mode and the **count** of stored keys —
is the `hmip` object of the metadata API's `GET /api/meta/v1/version` (
[meta-api.md → Feature detection](meta-api.md#feature-detection)).

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /radio/hmip/device-keys` **`radio:keys`** | `{rows, paired, with_key, stored, pending, devices_known, unread?, bad_lines?, applying?, error?}`: every paired HmIP device (the module and the receivers left out) with `has_key`, then the stored keys that match none, never a key. `pending` counts the changes (removals too) since HmIP-RF's start; `devices_known` is false while hmipserver does not answer; `unread` says why HmIP-RF would not read the map (`KeyServer.Mode=KEYSERVER`); `bad_lines` counts hand-edited lines that are not an SGTIN and a 16-byte key. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/device-keys` **`radio:keys`** | `{code}` (the QR text, anywhere in it) or `{sgtin, key}` (dashes and case as typed; the key as 32 hex digits or the printed 26 characters, converted as the WebUI does) → `{sgtin, address, paired, replaced?, same?}`. `422 invalid-code` with the reason. A serial that is no paired device is stored too; the page asks first. |
| `DELETE /radio/hmip/device-keys/{sgtin}` **`radio:keys`** | Removes a key; `404` when there is none. Answers the list. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/device-keys/apply` **`radio:keys`** | Restarts HmIP-RF in the background (`202`, the list with `applying`), holding local key mode's restart lock until the restart returned; `409` with `HmIP-RF is restarting already` for a second apply, `a radio connection change or a firmware flash is running`, or the lock's holder (`a local key switch is running`, `the adapter exchange is running`). |
| `GET /radio/hmip/device-keys/export` **`radio:keys`** | `{keys: [{sgtin, key, payload, address?, type?, name?}]}` for the key sheet, `Cache-Control: no-store`. A session needs a confirmed ticket in `X-Occulite-Confirm`, every time (`403 confirm-required` without); a token with the scope is not asked. Each export is logged: who, from where, how many keys. |

### The adapter exchange

hmipserver's ready step fails a start fast when eQ-3's key server rejected the move of the system's HmIP network to
the module in use; the marker's `cause` tells the two causes behind that one log line apart, read from
hmipserver's own output (nothing is probed): `unreachable` (a network or DNS matter - a WARN about an HTTP status, a
Java network exception before the rejection) or `refused` (the server answered and does not know the module). The
`hmip_fatal` object of `GET /radio/connections` and the `hmip-adapter` warning's `params.cause` carry it. All three
routes are the administrator's (`system:write`), like local key mode's.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /radio/hmip/exchange` **`system:write`** | `{fatal?, module, previous: [SGTIN], replaces_snapshots?: [SGTIN], local_key, exchange_id, switching?, error?, exchanges: [Exchange], hostname}`: the marker (`{code, line, adapter, cause?, at}`), the module HmIP-RF runs on, the previous modules whose identity files (`<SGTIN>.ap/.apkx/.bbkx`) are in hmipserver's data directory, which of them a snapshot is kept for already (a fresh start replaces it), whether local key mode is on, **the local record of adapter exchanges** (below) and the host name the fresh start's confirmation has to repeat. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/exchange/retry` **`system:write`** | The key server was not reached: clears the marker and restarts HmIP-RF, which tries the move again at its start. `202` with the view; `409 local-key` when HmIP-RF is not stopped on a rejected exchange, or a switch, the device keys' apply, a connection change or a flash runs. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/exchange/fresh-start` **`system:write`** | `{confirm: true, hostname, local_key}` - the guided fresh start for a refused module: every previous module's identity files move into a snapshot of their own (`kind: "fresh-start"`, listed with local key mode's snapshots, kept until discarded, replacing one kept for that module), with `local_key` the two keys are generated and written as the switch does unless local key mode is on already, the marker goes and HmIP-RF restarts with an empty network (every HmIP device is reset and paired again). `400 confirm` / `400 hostname` (the name typed, case and surrounding space ignored, is not the system's) and nothing happens; `409 local-key` as the retry, and when no previous identity is on the system or `accesspoint.exchange.id` rules a configured key out. `202` with the view; the work runs detached, `switching` says `fresh-start` while it does. |
| `POST /radio/hmip/module-move/back` **`system:write`** | `{confirm: true, hostname}` - **back to the previous module** after a move of HmIP-RF to another module (openccu-lite B-285), from the module-move snapshot the change kept: a connection change to the choices from before the move, and while the daemons are stopped every other module's identity in `/etc/config/crRFD/data/` (the one the exchange made for the module in use since) moves into a fresh-start snapshot of its own, the snapshot's `.ap/.apkx/.bbkx`, `<SGTIN>.dev` files and `/etc/config/hmip_address.conf` return (the data files root's for a moment, hmipserver's prep gives them back; the address file hmipserver's), a marker of a rejected exchange goes; then the detection and the plan run and hmipserver starts on the previous module with its own identity and the devices as they were - no key-server exchange. **The snapshot is consumed.** `400 confirm` / `400 hostname` and nothing happens; `409 local-key` without a module-move snapshot, with local key mode on, or beside a switch, a connection change or a flash; a choice the detection cannot carry (the previous module unplugged) fails the same way. `202` with the connections' status (`running` set; the page polls it); `switching` on the local key status says `move-back` while it runs. Logged at warning. |

**The local record of adapter exchanges** (openccu-lite task 301): hmipserver's unit steps keep `/etc/config/occulite/hmip-exchanges.jsonl`, one JSON object per line, appended, in every backup, never pruned, no outgoing call. The prep step notes an exchange the start will attempt (another module's identity file in hmipserver's data directory, none of the module in use); the ready step writes the entry with its outcome, or the stop after it (the next prep at the latest) when the move did not show in the files yet. An `Exchange` is `{at, from, to, address?, to_version?, mode?, outcome, cause?, line?}`: `from` the module whose identity was on the system (comma-separated when several), `to` the module HmIP-RF was started on, `address` the HmIP network's address at the start; `to_version` the application firmware of the module HmIP-RF was started on, from the plan; `mode` `key-server` (eQ-3's key server, local key mode off), `local-key` (offline, the system's own key) or `local-swap` (hmipserver swapped the access point locally without asking the server because the module's application firmware cannot take the network key over - *"Could not exchange network key, adapter version not supported"*, openccu-lite B-289; that module holds no network key), empty when the entry was closed without hmipserver's output; `outcome` `accepted` (the module in use has its identity file, the previous module's is gone), `rejected` (the marker, with `cause` unreachable/refused; or a local swap with local key mode off, `cause: adapter-version` - openccu-lite B-289: a failed move, whatever hmipserver logs after it; records written before B-289 say `accepted` for it) or `unknown`; `line` hmipserver's own word on the exchange (the *"Could not exchange network key …"* line for `adapter-version`). The prep step logs a start that will move the network onto a module below 2.8.0. The view answers it newest first. The Interfaces page's refused diagnosis reads from it whether the previous module took the network over without the server (a local swap), the one cause of a refusal it can name; eQ-3's key server itself does not say why it refuses.

### Heating groups

The heating groups — the `VirtualDevices` group devices `INT000000N` (BidCos `HM-CC-VG-1`, and hmipserver's HmIP
heating groups) — are hmipserver's: its group pages, `POST /pages/jpages/group/<command>?sid=…` on its HTTP port
(39292, loopback), create, change and delete them, and before every command hmipserver checks the sid with the CCU
JSON-RPC `Event.poll` on `http://127.0.0.1/api/homematic.cgi`. occulited is the pages' only client on openccu-lite
(`internal/hmgroups`): it uses a session of its own (a random sid in the CCU's shape, in memory, never stored),
answers hmipserver's check for that sid alone, and reads the pages as JSON — the fork's FreeMarker templates render
the WebUI's models as JSON (an image without them answers `502` naming that). The ReGa side effects the WebUI made
from the browser afterwards are the API's: the members' `hmip.inHeatingGroup` in the metadata store and the group
device's object, named `"<name> INT000000N"`, removed with the group. Reads need `system:read`, changes
`system:write`. Without hmipserver's port (a development root) every route is `501 unsupported`; a command
hmipserver does not answer is `502 hmipserver` with its word (`errorCode 42` = the session check failed).

Member ids are hmipserver's: on openccu-lite the device address (`KEQ9000003`, `00010000000A10:1`), as
`GET /groups/types` and a group's `assignable` list them. `devices_to_configure` after a change are the members
whose configuration is still pending (hmipserver's `configureDevices`); a client shows them, nothing else to do.

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /groups` **`system:read`** | `{groups: [{id, name, type, type_label, device, ref}], devices_to_configure: [Member]}` — `device` is the group device's address (`INT0000001`), `ref` its metadata ref (`VirtualDevices.INT0000001`). A `Member` is `{id, serial, type}`. |
| `GET /groups/types` **`system:read`** | `{types: [{id, label, assignable: [Member], leftover: [Member]}]}` — the types the editor offers (`HomeMatic.heating` "Heating_Control", `hmip.heating.group` "HmIP-Heizungssteuerung"), each with the devices a new group of it could take now and the ones that fit no group any more. |
| `GET /groups/{id}` **`system:read`** | `{id, name, type, device, ref, device_name, forbid_single_operation, members: [Member], assignable: [Member], leftover: [Member], types: [{id, label}]}`. `404 unknown-group` for an id the list does not have (hmipserver answers nothing at all for one; the API asks the list first). |
| `POST /groups` **`system:write`** | `{name, type, members: [id], forbid_single_operation?}` → the group as `GET /groups/{id}` answers it plus `devices_to_configure`. hmipserver's `create` then `save`; the name is 1–64 characters on one line (trimmed), `type` one of `GET /groups/types`, `members` hmipserver's ids. `422 invalid` names the field. A create that fails leaves no group behind (below). |
| `PUT /groups/{id}` **`system:write`** | `{name?, members?, forbid_single_operation?}` — the name and the members **as a whole** (adding and removing is one save with the new list, as the WebUI did it); a field left out keeps its value. Answers as `POST`. A member that left loses `inHeatingGroup`; the group device's object is renamed. `404 unknown-group`. A change that fails half-way is undone (below). |
| `DELETE /groups/{id}` **`system:write`** | `{deleted, former_members: [Member]}` — `deleted` is the group's id (a number, `{"deleted": 6, …}`); the former members' `inHeatingGroup` goes false, the group device's object is deleted. `404 unknown-group`. |
| `POST /api/homematic.cgi` **open, loopback only** | hmipserver's session check, `{"method":"Event.poll","params":{"_session_id_":"<sid>"}}`: occulited's own sid → `{"version":"1.1","result":[],"error":null}`, anything else → `{"version":"1.1","result":null,"error":{"name":"JSONRPCError","code":400,"message":"access denied: …"}}`, the CCU's two answers. From anywhere but `127.0.0.1` (the connection's address, or on a connection from the loopback the **last** element of `X-Forwarded-For`, the one lighttpd appends — a client-sent element before it never counts, B-230) it is `404`. Not a CCU JSON-API. |

**When a save fails** (B-269): hmipserver's `save` stores the group — a new one with its id, a changed one with its
name — before it takes the members, and a save that dies in between is never answered. So after a save that failed
or went unanswered, the API reads the group back:
- **create:** a new group of that name that holds all the members asked for is the success the answer missed (`200`
  as usual); one that does not is deleted again, and the answer is `502 hmipserver` saying so. When that delete
  fails too, the message names the group that is left.
- **change:** a group that holds the whole change is the success; one that holds a part of it is saved back to its
  old name, members and "operate as group only" (`502`, "its old state was restored", or "restoring it failed");
  an unchanged one is `502` "the group is unchanged".

The member ids travel to hmipserver as a string holding a JSON list, the way the WebUI's library sent them: hmipserver
reads the field as text and parses it again, and a plain array reached it as `[a, b]` without quotes, which breaks
at an HmIP channel address's colon — the cause of B-269's `502` after 30 s with an empty group left behind. Each
command has 30 s (hmipserver answers a save in well under a second; an XML-RPC `init` on `VirtualDevices` can hold
its event loop for 10 s) and its own connection (hmipserver drops idle keep-alive connections, and a command sent on
one it has just dropped failed with `EOF`). Reading a group passes hmipserver the group device's name
`"<name> INT000000N"`, since its `edit` stores the name it gets; a new group gets that name with its real id when
it is read back after the save.

The store: hmipserver writes `/etc/config/groups.gson` (HMServer.conf's `groupStorageFilePath`) in place, and its
unit is `ProtectSystem=strict` with the file — not the directory — in `ReadWritePaths`; `occulited radio prep
hmipserver` makes the file exist as hmipserver's before the unit's namespace is set up, as the no-groups object
`{"groups":[]}` when it is missing or empty (gson reads an empty file as null and the server's start thread dies).
Measured on the OVA 2026-09-22: a BidCos heating group with the HM-Sec-SC created, renamed, listed after an
hmipserver restart and deleted; the group device `INT0000001` appears on `VirtualDevices` while the group exists.

### Backup and restore

| | |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /backup` **`backup`** | Admin. Runs `createBackup.sh`, streams the CCU-compatible `.sbk` (`Content-Disposition` with the CCU's file name). The shell fetches it with a one-time ticket (`?ticket=`, `POST /ticket`), never with the session in the URL. **With encryption on** the stream is `<name>.sbk.age`: the `.sbk` unchanged inside one binary age v1 stream for two X25519 recipients, the system's own identity and the recovery key, plus a metadata stanza `-> github.com/hobbyquaker/occulited/backup v1 box=<fp> rec=<fp>` naming both keys' fingerprints in the clear (age identities ignore it; the name is fixed before the first release that writes encrypted backups). No `Content-Length` then. `?encrypted=false` gives the plain `.sbk` although encryption is on, **with a confirmed ticket** (the password every time, `POST /api/auth/v1/ticket {path: "/api/system/v1/backup/unencrypted", confirm: true, password}`) in `?confirm=` or `X-Occulite-Confirm`, else `403 confirm-required`; a token session passes without one; the download is journaled. Every download's SHA-256, name and size is noted in `<state>/backup-key/created.json` (below), so a restore can say the file was made here. |
| `POST /restore/check` **`power`** | Multipart `file` or raw body → stored under `/usr/local/tmp/restore-*.sbk`, `restoreBackup.sh -c -f` run with one empty line on its standard input (openccu-lite task 296: the check goes on past the key prompt to the end instead of stopping at it, so a backup with a key is `ok` when it is sound, and the script's "does NOT match …, forced restore." about the empty key reads "the passphrase is asked for below."); answer `{file, check: {ok, output, backup_version, running_version, needs_key, has_rega, backup_key, system_key, key_index}, encryption}` — `backup_key`/`system_key`: which side has a BidCos security key other than the factory key (the script's own verdicts; `key_index > 0` counts for the backup too), `key_index` the backup's `key_index` file when a key is involved. A backup with a ReGa database is accepted; the names come through the metadata import. the upload's first bytes are sniffed. A plain `.sbk` goes on as before (`encryption: {encrypted: false, needs_recovery_key: false, created_here}`). An age file (binary, or ASCII-armored `-----BEGIN AGE ENCRYPTED FILE-----`; the file name is never trusted) is **decrypted while it uploads** when the system's own identity opens its header, stored as `restore-<name>.sbk` and checked: `encryption: {encrypted: true, format: "age-v1", armored?, box_fingerprint?, recovery_fingerprint?, known: "current"\|"previous"\|"unknown", key_created?, opened_with: "box", needs_recovery_key: false, created_here}`. One the system's key does not open is stored as it is as `restore-<name>.sbk.age` with `check: null` and `needs_recovery_key: true` (`passphrase: true` for an age passphrase file, which the system cannot open at all); `known` and `key_created` come from the metadata stanza's recovery fingerprint against the current and earlier recovery keys. A damaged file is `422 corrupt` and nothing is kept. **One upload at a time:** before the new upload is stored, every `restore-*.sbk` and `restore-*.sbk.age` under `/usr/local/tmp` is removed — what an earlier attempt left behind (an encrypted upload whose key never came, a checked file never applied) does not wait for the next boot's `S06InitSystem`; the carried identity dotfile is not touched. `created_here` says the upload's hash (of the file as received) is in this system's list of handed-out backups. |
| `POST /restore/decrypt` **`power`** | `{file, recovery_key}` — the `restore-*.sbk.age` name `/restore/check` returned, and the recovery key as the code (any grouping, case or spacing; `i`, `l`, `o` read as `1`, `1`, `0`) or as the age identity from the kit. Decrypts it into `restore-*.sbk` (into occulited's staging directory, moved into place by the helper once the stream ended), removes the `.sbk.age`, runs the check and answers like `/restore/check` with `opened_with: "recovery"` and `known` for the key typed. `422 invalid-key` for a checksum failure (a typo, never "wrong key"), `is-recipient` for an `age1…` public key, `unsupported` for a post-quantum or plugin identity, `wrong-key` right after the header when the key does not open the file (no payload is read; **the upload stays** for the next try), `corrupt` on a failed chunk or a truncated file — the partial output *and the damaged upload itself* are removed, `detail: {upload_removed: true}` says so, and the page goes back to the file picker (no key will open it, and it held 200 MB until the next boot); `507 no-space` when the staging filesystem has less room than the encrypted file (the upload stays); 400 for another name, 404 when the upload is gone. |
| `POST /restore/apply` **`power`** | `{file, key?, force?, confirm}` - **`confirm: true` is required** (openccu-lite task 317, D-120: the restore replaces `/usr/local`, the HmIP identity files with it; `400 confirm` without) → `restoreBackup.sh` without `-r` (`-f` with `force`): the script checks the archive, sets the security key when the backup brings one, moves the tree to `/usr/local/tmp` and marks `/usr/local/.doBackupRestore`; it runs on a context of its own (a client that gives up mid-way does not cut the staging short; 10 min bound). Then the route answers **200 `{ok: true, output, rebooting: true}`** and the ServiceManager reboots a moment later, as `/system-update/install` does — the answer reaches the client first (with `-r` the script rebooted under the open request, the route answered `422 restore-failed "context canceled"` or the connection dropped, and the boot marker was removed although the restore was under way). A script failure is `422 restore-failed` with its output (`detail.key_check`), nothing staged for the boot marker. **The passphrase check (openccu-lite task 296):** the answer carries `key_check: {backup, system, key_index}` as `/restore/verify-key` says it, and the journal line the verdicts; a restore without the backup's passphrase (`mismatch` or `skipped`) runs with `force` after the page's warning and confirmation - and then the script is given a random placeholder instead of the typed word: its step 3 sets this system's key from what it reads when the system has none and the backup has one, and a wrong word there would later pass as the right one; the restore at boot brings the backup's own `keys` and `crypttool.cfg` over it. Nothing re-keys a BidCos device at the restore: rfd's key store comes back as it is. When the reboot does not start (or there is no ServiceManager), the answer is `200 {ok: true, output, rebooting: false, message}`: the restore is staged and applies at the next reboot; the page says so. The key is handed to the script's prompt as one line on its standard input, an empty line without a key (without stdin the script stopped at the prompt whenever the system or the backup had a security key). Only a `restore-*.sbk` name: an `.sbk.age` is refused (400) until `/restore/decrypt` turned it into the `.sbk`. **`422 hmip-firmware`** (openccu-lite B-289) before the script runs, with `detail: {module, version, minimum}`, when the backup's HmIP identity belongs to another module than this system's HmIP-RF module, the backup's local key mode is off and this module's application firmware is below 2.8.0 (`/restore/devices`' `hmip_firmware`). before the script runs, the system's backup identity is copied to `/usr/local/tmp/.occulite-box-identity` (`0600`, occulited's, through the helper): the restore at boot wipes `/usr/local` except `tmp`, `S06InitSystem` empties `/usr/local/tmp` but keeps dotfiles, and no backup contains that directory — so the identity survives the restore without ever being in an archive. At the next start occulited adopts it when the restored state directory brought none (`backup-key/` is `.nobackup`) and removes the file either way; a factory reset makes the filesystem anew and carries nothing. |
| `POST /restore/verify-key` **`power`** | `{file, key}` (openccu-lite task 296) — the passphrase of the backup's BidCos security key (the *System-Sicherheitsschlüssel*) checked, **never a gate**: `200 {backup, system, key_index}` whatever the verdict. `backup`: `match`, `mismatch`, `skipped` (no passphrase given, the backup has its own key) or `none` (the backup is on the factory key, `key_index 0`) — checked in Go against the backup's `signature` file, which is crypttool's signature of the inner archive: the MD5 of `usr_local.tar.gz` encrypted (AES-128, one block) with the MD5 of the passphrase, so no key material is compared and no tool runs; the archive's MD5 is computed once per upload and kept while the file is unchanged. `system`: the same verdicts for this system's own key (`crypttool -v -t 3 -k`, only for a passphrase of letters, digits and `_` as the firmware takes them - anything else is a mismatch without a helper call), `unknown` when crypttool could not say. The passphrase is used for the comparison only: not stored, not logged (the journal line has the verdicts), not in an answer; the privilege helper's refusal line writes `-k <redacted>`. `400 invalid`, `404 not_found` as `/restore/apply`, `422 not_a_backup`. |
| `GET /restore/devices?file=` **`power`** | What a checked upload (`restore-*.sbk`) holds of the three radios and what this system has. `{backup: {bidcos_rf: {devices, address, serial, has_key, gateways}, hmip: {devices, identity_sgtin, local_key, device_key_map}, bidcos_wired: {devices, gateways}, key_index, files: [...], version}, target: {paired: {<interface>: {devices, known, error}}, devices, unknown: [...], user_key, importable, hmip_module?: {hardware, serial, sgtin, version?}, bidcos_module?: {hardware, serial, version?}}, module_changed, non_default_key, hmip_firmware?}` — the backup read in Go from the inner `usr_local.tar.gz` (rfd's `*.dev` under `etc/config/rfd`, hmipserver's under `crRFD/data`, the identity by its `.ap` file, `ids`, a non-empty `keys`, the gateway sections of `rfd.conf`/`hs485d.conf`, `hmip_user.conf`'s network key, `sgtin.map`); the target is `CountPaired` over the interfaces (the factory reset's count, own entries left out), `crypttool -v -t 0` (without crypttool a non-empty `etc/config/keys`), and the radio plan's modules. `importable` = no device paired and every interface answered. **`module_changed`** (openccu-lite task 275): the backup's HmIP identity is bound to another module than this system's HmIP-RF module, so hmipserver has to take it over after the import (the adapter exchange); **`non_default_key`** (task 278): the backup's BidCos key store is not the factory key (`key_index > 0` or a non-empty `keys`). **`hmip_firmware`** `{module, version, minimum}` (openccu-lite B-289) is there when `module_changed`, the backup's local key mode is off and this system's HmIP module (`hmip_module.version`, its application firmware) is below 2.8.0: a module that cannot take the HmIP network's key - the import and the restore are refused (`422 hmip-firmware`), and the page names the firmware update first; a backup in local key mode moves offline and is not refused. `422 not_a_backup`, `400 invalid`, `404 not_found` as `/restore/apply`. |
| `POST /restore/import-devices` **`power`** | `{file, replace_key?, key?, confirm}` — **`confirm: true` is required** (openccu-lite task 317, D-120: the backup's HmIP identity files are written; `400 confirm` without, before every other check); the backup's paired devices with their radio identity onto this system, **all three radios, all or nothing, only onto a system without paired devices**. Refused: `422 hmip-firmware` (openccu-lite B-289; `detail: {module, version, minimum}`, as the view's `hmip_firmware`), `409 paired` (devices here; `detail.target`), `409 unknown` (an interface did not answer), `422 nothing_to_import`, `422 key_replace` (this system has a BidCos key store of its own - `target.user_key` - and `replace_key: true` was not given; the backup's store replaces it, and nothing is paired here that it could orphan). **No passphrase needed** (task 278, option B): the backup's `keys` and `crypttool.cfg` come as they are - rfd reads the derived key - and the panel says that a non-default key came along. **The passphrase as a check** (task 296): `key` is the backup's passphrase when the user gave it; it is checked as `/restore/verify-key` checks it and never refuses the import; the verdict (`match`, `mismatch`, `skipped`, `none`) is the answer's `key_check`, the record's `bidcos_rf.key_check` and the journal line's; the passphrase itself is nowhere. **The names first** (task 281): the ReGa names, rooms and functions of the same checked file are imported into the metadata store (merge, as `POST /api/meta/v1/import/regadom` with `source: restore:<file>`) before the reboot empties `/usr/local/tmp`; the result's `names: {ok, objects, rooms, functions, changed, error?}` reports it, and a names failure (no ReGa database in the backup, say) does not stop the device import. Then `Root.ImportRadio`: this system's `rfd/`, `crRFD/`, `hs485d/`, `ids`, `keys`, `crypttool.cfg`, `hmip_address.conf`, `rfd.conf`, `hs485d.conf` are moved aside to `/etc/config/.import-devices-aside/<UTC time>/` (never deleted), the backup's written through the helper (a member that climbs or is a link is dropped; 4 MB per file), owned by rfd/hmipserver/hs485d. Not taken: `InterfacesList.xml` (the radio plan renders it), the ReGa database, anything else. The import is recorded in the state directory (`devices-import.json`: the identity's module and this system's, the key store's provenance - `GET /radio/import` below). Then the reboot as `/restore/apply` (`200 {ok, imported: {backup, written, aside, non_default_key, target_key_replaced}, record, names, rebooting}`; `rebooting: false` + `message` when it did not start). At boot the plan cuts `rfd.conf`/`hs485d.conf` for this hardware and hmipserver takes the imported identity over onto this system's module (the adapter exchange). |
| `GET /radio/import` **`system:read`** | The record of the last device import and how it stands (openccu-lite task 275): `{imported: false}`, or `{imported: true, record: {at, file, version, hmip: {from_sgtin, to_sgtin, to_module, module_changed, local_key, devices}, bidcos_rf: {address, serial, devices, non_default_key, key_index, target_key_replaced, module}}, outcome: {hmip: {state, cause?, module_now, line?}, bidcos_rf: {took?, interface?, connected, error?}}, switching?, error?}`. `hmip.state`: `none` (the identity belongs to this module, or there is none), `pending` (the previous module's `<SGTIN>.ap` is still in hmipserver's data directory and no marker: hmipserver has not taken it over yet), `done` (this module's `.ap` is there and the previous one's gone - hmipserver rewrites them when the exchange succeeds), `rejected` (the marker of D-102, written after the import; `cause` unreachable/refused; or, openccu-lite B-289, `cause: adapter-version` when the files moved but hmipserver could not hand the network key to the module - its *"Could not exchange network key, adapter version not supported"* line since the import, or the local record's entry onto this module - with local key mode off in the import), `no-module`, `unknown`; `line` is hmipserver's own "Adapter exchange …" line from the journal since the import (the "Could not exchange network key …" line for `adapter-version`). `bidcos_rf.took`: rfd's local radio entry of `listBidcosInterfaces` carries the imported serial (rfd names it by the serial it runs with), nil when rfd did not answer (`error`). |
| `POST /radio/import/retry` **`power`** | Restarts HmIP-RF so that hmipserver attempts the exchange again (a marker of a rejected exchange goes with it), through the local key service's restart lock. `202` with the view; `404 not_found` without a record; `409 local-key` while a switch, a connection change or a flash runs; `501` without the local key service. |
| `DELETE /radio/import` **`power`** | Removes the record (the notice on the Interfaces page and the `devices-import` warning go with it). `204`. |
| `GET/PUT /backup/schedule` **`system:read` / `system:write`** | The firmware's nightly `cronBackup.sh` (00:07): `{enabled, path, max_backups, path_exists, on_userfs?, real_path?, backups: [{name, size, time}]}`; `PUT {enabled, path, max_backups}` writes `NoCronBackup`, `CronBackupPath`, `CronBackupMaxBackups`. The script refuses a path on the root filesystem, on tmpfs or `/usr/local` — but it compares the resolved path against `/usr/local` *exactly*, and on a system with no USB stick upstream's `S62HMServer.script` points `/media/usb0` at the SD card, so its own default target passes that guard and the backup lands on the system. `on_userfs` says so (the target resolves onto the same filesystem as `/usr/local`), with `real_path` when the symlink hides it; the Backup and Status pages carry a notice. `POST /backup/schedule/run` starts the directory target's run through the backup target pipeline (`202`, as `POST /backup/targets/directory/run`); `cronBackup.sh` is not used on openccu-lite any more. `GET` answers `503 stale` when the directory does not answer within 5 s. |
| `GET /backup/encryption` **`backup`** | Administrator: `{enabled, recovery: {fingerprint, created} \| null, previous: [{fingerprint, created, retired}], box: {fingerprint, created} \| null}`. Fingerprints only (the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 over the `age1…` recipient string, `cd22-595a-8524-3c56`) — a recipient string never leaves the daemon, since whoever has one can encrypt a planted backup to this system; the recovery key itself the system never has. `recovery` is the current recovery key, `previous` the retired ones (a backup made with one of them still needs it), `box` the system's own identity (made at the first confirmation, kept in `<state>/backup-key/`, in no backup). 501 where the store is not wired. |
| `POST /backup/encryption/recovery` **`backup`** | Starts a setup or a rotation. `{recipient: "age1…"}` — the public half of the recovery key the browser derived from the code it made (WebCrypto; the secret never crosses the network) — or `{generate: true}` where the browser cannot (no WebCrypto on plain HTTP): then the system makes the code and returns it in this one answer. → `{pending_id, fingerprint, recovery_key?}`. The pending recipient waits in memory for 30 minutes; nothing is saved yet. 422 `invalid-key` for a recipient that does not parse, `unsupported` for a post-quantum (`age1pq…`) or plugin recipient. |
| `POST /backup/encryption/confirm` **`backup`** | `{pending_id}`, sent once the user ticked *I have stored the recovery key outside this system* (a tick only). Makes the recipient the current recovery key, moves the old one to `previous` with `retired` (the same recipient again rotates nothing; an old one confirmed again comes back to the front), switches encryption on and makes the system's identity when there is none. A generated code is dropped from memory here. Answers the view. 404 `pending-unknown` for an unknown or expired id. |
| `PUT /backup/encryption` **`backup`** | `{enabled}`: the switch for what `GET /backup` hands out. Off keeps the recovery key (backups made so far stay encrypted); on without a recovery key is `422 no-recovery-key` — a backup encrypted to the system alone would die with it. Journaled. |
| `POST /backup/encryption/test` **`backup`** | `{recovery_key}` (the code or an age identity) → `{matches: "current"\|"previous"\|"none", fingerprint, created?, retired?, identity, recovery_key}` without storing anything: *Test my recovery key*, and the emergency kit printed again for a key that matches (`identity` is the derived `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1…` for `age -d` on a PC, `recovery_key` the code grouped, empty when an identity was given). 422 `invalid-key` (a typo), `is-recipient`, `unsupported`. |
| `GET /backup/targets` **`system:read`** | The backup targets. `{nightly: {enabled, time: "00:07"}, container, kinds: {directory, share, nfs, cifs, sftp}, encryption, hostname, needed_bytes, targets: [Target]}`. NFS and SMB shares are defined once on System → Storage (`GET /storage/shares`); a backup target of kind **`share`** names one and a folder (`share: {id, folder}`; the folder is the per-system directory, `subdir` stays empty; the backups go to `/media/net/<id>/<folder>`, mounted by the share's automount; mount and unmount act on the share's mount). The `nfs`/`cifs` targets are **migrated at every start of occulited** (idempotent, a journal line per target): each becomes a share named after the target (`TrueNAS` → `truenas`; the target's own id when that name is taken; a share with that name and the same source left by an interrupted run is taken over, any other share never), its SMB password moves into the share's credentials file, and the target keeps its id, results and settings as kind `share` with its `subdir` as the folder; the old units go. `POST`/`PUT` of a target of kind `nfs` or `cifs` is `400 invalid` since. The **directory target** may be chosen as a location (`directory: {path, location}`: `usb:<label>/<folder>` or `userfs:backup…`); `path` then follows the location — the stick's mount now, `/media/.usb-not-plugged-in/<folder>` while it is not plugged in (which the delivery calls `no-medium`) — and the marker `CronBackupPath` is written with the path at the save; without a location the path is used as before. `kinds[k]` is `""` when the kind works here, `"container"` (NFS/SMB in a container: mount on the host, pass a directory in) or `"tool"` (no `mount.cifs`; NFS without `mount.nfs` works by address only). **`Target`:** `{id, name, kind: "directory"\|"share"\|"nfs"\|"cifs"\|"sftp", enabled, max_backups, subdir, encrypt, append_only, created?, directory?: {path, location?}, share?: {id, folder}, nfs?: {server, export, version}, cifs?: {server, share, version, seal, user, domain?, has_password}, sftp?: {host, port, user, path, public_key?, host_key?: {type, fingerprint}}, state: {state, detail?, checked_at?, failures, next_retry_at?, mounted, source?, options?, free_bytes?, total_bytes?, unsupported?}, last_backup?: {at, ok, state, step?, error?, name?, size?, duration_ms?, sha256?, encrypted, removed?, last_ok?}}`. `state.state` is one of `unsupported`, `idle`, `connecting`, `writable`, `read-only`, `full`, `unreachable`, `auth-failed`, `host-key-unknown`, `host-key-changed`, `no-sftp`, `stale`, `no-medium`, `update-room`, `running`, `error` — the newer of the last test/probe and the last delivery, `running` while a create or deliver unit for it (or `all`, `nightly`) is active. The **directory target** has the fixed id `directory` and is upstream's markers (`CronBackupPath`, `CronBackupMaxBackups`); it exists while a path is set. Ids are `^[a-z][a-z0-9]{0,15}$` (generated `t` + 7), no `-` (systemd's unit-name escaping). Secrets never appear: a CIFS password only as `has_password`, an SSH key only as its public line. **Room for a system update** (B-247): a directory target on the system's own user partition (the same filesystem as `/usr/local`, e.g. `/media/usb0/backup` without a stick) keeps **2.75 GB** free for a staged update — every release unpacks to 2.42 GB there (the zip's `unzip -v` total; the recovery refuses the update with less) plus a margin. Before the backup is made (estimated at the newest backup's size, the staging copy in `/usr/local/tmp` counted) and again before the copy, the oldest backups in the directory are removed until it fits — any backup file there, since the partition is this system's own, never the newest one; the journal names what went (`backup: old backups removed to keep room for a system update`, Info; the delivery's `removed`). When even that is not enough the target is skipped: `last.json` `update-room`, step `space`, the error with the free and needed bytes; `backup: skipped, it would leave too little room for a system update` (Warn) and the Status warning `backup-delivery` with the cause `update-room`. `max_backups` still applies after a delivery; a USB stick, a share or an SFTP server is not touched by this rule. |
| `POST /backup/targets` **`system:write`** | A `Target` without `id` (ignored when sent) → `201 {target, apply_error?}`. An SFTP target gets its ed25519 key at once; an NFS/SMB target's units are rendered through the helper (`apply_error` when that failed; the target is saved). A second directory target is `409 exists`. `cifs.password` is write-only (stored in `backup-targets/<id>/cifs.cred`, 0600). `400 invalid` for any field outside its shape. |
| `PUT /backup/targets/{id}` **`system:write`** | The same body; the kind cannot change (`400`). A CIFS edit without `password` keeps the stored one. `404 not_found`. |
| `DELETE /backup/targets/{id}` **`system:write`** | Removes the target's units and secrets (the directory target: its `CronBackupPath`) — never a file on the target. `204`. |
| `POST /backup/targets/{id}/test` **`system:write`** | The write test: 1 MiB created exclusively, written, synced, read back against its SHA-256, deleted — as root through the helper for the directory (only under `/media`) and the mounts, as occulited over SFTP. → `{ok, state, step: "mkdir"\|"statfs"\|"create"\|"write"\|"fsync"\|"read"\|"compare"\|"delete"\|"connect"\|"mount"\|"timeout"\|"done", error?, free_bytes, total_bytes, needed_bytes, write_mbps?, fs_type?, at}`; `state` is `full` when the free space is below `needed_bytes` (the last backup's size and a tenth). A mount that did not come up (written onto the tmpfs under the mount point) is `unreachable`; the mount unit's journal lines decide `auth-failed` vs `unreachable` — read only while the share is **not** mounted: on a mounted share the error is its own reason (EACCES/EPERM/EROFS on a root-squashed export → `read-only`), and an attempt that ended in systemd's "Mounted …" is never a reason. |
| `POST /backup/targets/{id}/mount`, `…/unmount` **`system:write`** | NFS/SMB only (`409 not-a-mount` otherwise): mount now (the first access through the automount, bounded by the prober's 5 s) or detach now (`systemctl stop` of the mount unit, which carries `LazyUnmount=`/`ForceUnmount=`); the automount stays armed. → the target. |
| `POST /backup/targets/{id}/run`, `POST /backup/run` **`backup`** | Back up now: `systemctl start --no-block occu-backup-create@<id>.service` (`/backup/run` with `{target?}`, default `all`). `202 {started, instance}`; `409 busy` while a run is active.
