The units (the fork's) run `occulited -backup create <instance>` as root — `createBackup.sh` once into `/usr/local/tmp/occulite-backup/`, encryption, the verified copies into the directory and the mounts (`.partial`, fsync, SHA-256 read back, rename, `0640 root:occulite` or `0644` where root cannot chown) — and then `occulited -backup deliver <instance>` as occulite: the SFTP uploads (`.partial`, `fsync@openssh.com`, size check, `posix-rename@openssh.com`), the record of the files handed out, the staging emptied. **Before `createBackup.sh`** every directory and share target is probed as root — the delivery's checks, its folder made, a small file written and removed: one that cannot take a file gets its `last.json` at once (`read-only`, `unreachable`, …, the step that failed), the others get their copy, the create unit fails; when no target is left, no backup is made. Retention applies after a verified delivery, to `<hostname>-*.sbk[.age]` only, never on an `append_only` target. The result per target is `backup-targets/<id>/last.json`. |
| `GET /backup/targets/{id}/backups` **`system:read`** | `{backups: [{name, size, time, encrypted, recovery_fingerprint?, known?: "current"\|"previous"\|"unknown"}]}`, newest first; the ten newest encrypted files' age headers are read for the key they need. on a share (and a task-86 NFS/SMB target) the folder is listed and the files opened **as root through the helper** (`sharelist`, `shareopen`: only under `/media/net/<id>`, no link followed, only `*.sbk[.age]` opened, the file passed as a descriptor) — on a root-squashed export the folders root made are nobody's and occulite could not read them; the restore check's `{target, name}` opens the file the same way. A share that does not answer within 40 s (the first access may mount it) is `503 stale`; a target's error `502 target` with `detail.state`. |
| `POST /backup/targets/{id}/keypair` **`system:write`** | SFTP: a new ed25519 key (the old one stops working) → `{public_key}` (`ssh-ed25519 … openccu-lite-backup@<hostname>`). The page shows it as `restrict <key>` for the server's `authorized_keys`. |
| `GET /backup/targets/{id}/hostkey`, `PUT …` **`system:write`** | SFTP: `GET` connects far enough to see the server's key → `{type, fingerprint: "SHA256:…", trusted, changed, trusted_fingerprint?}`; `PUT {fingerprint}` scans again and pins the key only when it is the one confirmed (`409 fingerprint` otherwise). A pinned key that changed is `host-key-changed` and never accepted on its own. |
| `PUT /backup/nightly` **`system:write`** | `{enabled}` → `{enabled, time}`: the nightly run for every enabled target (upstream's `NoCronBackup` marker; the fork's timer at 00:07 plus up to 30 minutes). |
| `POST /restore/check` with `{target, name}` **`power`** | `Content-Type: application/json` and a backup's name on a target instead of an upload: the file is read from the target (SFTP, or the directory/mount through the prober) and goes through exactly the upload's path (sniffed, decrypted with the system's key when it opens it, checked). `400` for a name that is not a `.sbk[.age]` file name, `404` for an unknown target. |

## `/api/rpc/v1` — lite-rpc

The interface processes' XML-RPC over the web port with the API's credentials, and their events as a stream instead of a
callback server at the client. Every route needs **`rpc:read`** in the table; each call the tier its method needs
(the table in `internal/literpc/tiers.go`: reads `rpc:read`, `setValue` and `putParamset(…, VALUES)` `rpc:operate`,
paramsets, links, install mode, metadata, teams `rpc:configure`, delete, re-key, firmware, restore `rpc:admin`; an
**unknown method needs `rpc:admin`**). A `system.multicall` is checked per inner call. **`init` is never forwarded**
(fault `-1`, *"init is not available remotely on openccu-lite: subscribe to /api/rpc/v1/events - see docs/rpc-remote.md"*).
lite-rpc is **always on**; without the service (a development system) the paths answer 501.

**Credentials:** a token as `Authorization: Bearer olt_…` or as the **basic-auth password** (the user name is ignored;
that works on the whole API). **Never in the query string** here: `?sid=` is 400. A **browser session** (the shell)
passes with the session in the Authorization header (the shell sends it); the cookie alone opens the **stream** and
reads only from the system's own origin and can **call no method** (403 with the reason) — the header credential and
the Origin check, for these paths; since task 259 the whole API asks a state-changing call on the cookie alone for
`X-Occulite-Request` (see `/api/auth/v1`). **The system's own origin** is what the browser says: `Sec-Fetch-Site:
same-origin`/`none`, or an `Origin` that is this host; another value of either is refused whatever else the request
carries. A browser that says neither — `Sec-Fetch-Site` goes out from a secure context only (HTTPS, localhost), and
a same-origin GET carries no `Origin`, so that is every page of the system opened over plain `http://<address>/` —
passes with the header **`X-Occulite-Request`** (any value): a page of another origin cannot set it without a CORS
preflight, which nothing on the system answers, while an `<img>` or a navigation from another port of the host, which
brings the cookie too, cannot set it at all (occulited B-47). An `EventSource` can set no header either, so over plain
HTTP it is refused; the Control app reads its stream with `fetch()` — the header, and the session as Bearer when it
has one (the public mode's principal has none: the origin rule is all it keeps, on requests too). The WebSocket
handshake always carries `Origin`.

| Route | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /interfaces` | `[{name, protocol: "xmlrpc", url_path, running}]`, sorted by name — **the place for the interfaces this system runs** (a client's setup pre-selects them from here once it holds a credential with `rpc:read`; the open `/api/meta/v1/version` does not name them, as they tell the radio hardware): every `InterfacesList.xml` entry the subscriber speaks to — `xmlrpc://`, `http(s)://`, and BidCos-RF/BidCos-Wired on their `xmlrpc_bin://` ports, which answer XML-RPC too; not CUxD. `running` is the subscriber's view (the process answered its last init or ping). Live: the file is watched. |
| `POST /xmlrpc/{interface}` | One XML-RPC call (any declared charset), checked and forwarded to the process — at its `InterfacesList.xml` URL, the path included (`/groups` for VirtualDevices) — in ISO-8859-1 (a character outside Latin-1 becomes a numeric character reference); the answer as the process gave it, re-encoded in **UTF-8**. A refusal (init, the tier) is a fault with code `-1` over 200, as XML-RPC clients expect. Not a call: 400; no such interface: 404 `unknown-interface`; the process does not answer: **503 `down`** naming it. Body limit 4 MB. |
| `POST /json/{interface}` | The same as **JSON-RPC 2.0**: `{jsonrpc: "2.0", method, params: [...], id}`, a batch as an array (the multicall equivalent), a request without `id` is forwarded without an answer (204 when nothing answers). Errors: a process fault keeps its code, init and a refused tier `-32601`, a process down `-32000`, `-32700`/`-32600`/`-32602` as the spec. Types: `i4`/`int`/`double` ↔ number (an integral number becomes `i4`), `boolean` ↔ bool, `dateTime.iso8601` → RFC 3339 string, `base64` ↔ `{"base64": "…"}`, **`{"double": n}` → `double` whatever n looks like** (S2: a `FLOAT` datapoint written as `1`), struct ↔ object, array ↔ array. |
| `GET /events` | **SSE** (`text/event-stream`; `X-Accel-Buffering: no`): `: connected` at once, `: ping` every 15 s (a client treats 45 s of silence as dead). Messages `id: <boot_id>-<seq>` + `event:` + one JSON `data:` line — except `resync` and the `?devices=1` snapshot's `newDevices`, which carry no `id:` (they are not in the ring; a client keeps the last id it saw): `hello {boot_id, seq, interfaces, buffer: {seconds: 300, events: 5000}}` first; `event {interface, address, key, value, ts, batch?, lc?, confirmed?, previous_for_s?}` (`batch` = the seq of the first event of the `system.multicall` it came in, on every event of that call; an event of a datapoint the state store keeps carries the entry's `lc`, `confirmed: true` and, after a change the store saw, `previous_for_s` — the same values `GET /state` answers); `state {interface, address, datapoint, value, ts, lc, confirmed, source, previous_for_s?}` (the state store's sweep created, changed or confirmed an entry, or a read of a maintenance channel by the service-message store found a value that differs from the entry — an acknowledged `STICKY_UNREACH`, which comes without an event; `ts` is the read's time, `source: "sweep"`); `interface {interface, state: up\|down\|restarted\|added\|removed, ts}`; `newDevices`/`deleteDevices`/`readdedDevice {interface, addresses, ts}`; `updateDevice {interface, addresses, hint, ts}` (`hint` as the interface sent it: 0 unspecified, 1 the links changed, 2 the description changed - read it again; 0 when the call had none); `replaceDevice {interface, addresses, old, new, ts}` (`addresses` is `[old, new]`, as before); `resync {reason: gap\|boot\|overflow}`. **Filters** (repeatable, comma-separable; AND across kinds, OR within one): `interface=`, `address=` (a device matches its channels), `key=` (`datapoint=` is its alias; both apply to `event` and `state`), `type=`. **Resume:** `Last-Event-ID` (or `?last_event_id=`) replays the ring (5 minutes or 5,000 events); older than the ring → `resync gap` first, another boot id → `resync boot`. **A stop** (a restart, an update) ends the response at once - the stop cancels every stream before the process exits - so a client reconnects right away and gets `resync boot`; its read timeout is only for a connection that went half-open. **`?devices=1`** sends one `newDevices {interface, devices: [descriptions]}` per interface (a `listDevices` each) before the live events. **Overflow:** each stream has a queue of 1024 messages; a client that does not read fast enough loses what does not fit, and at the next heartbeat (within 15 s) gets `resync overflow` and is disconnected. **Limits:** 3 streams per token or session by default (`rpc.streams_per_session` in occulited.json, 1-16, read at the start; it was 2 until occulited task 19), 16 in total (SSE and WebSocket together): **429 `too-many-streams`**. `GET /streams`' `limits` and `/version`'s `capabilities.limits.streams_per_token` say the limit in force. The Control app keeps one stream per window open while the App is shown (since occulited B-53 not while another page of the shell is shown or the window is hidden, after 10 s) and says *not live* in its title while it has none (too many windows, or reconnecting). A browser over https behind lighttpd has eight streams at once on its one HTTP/2 connection, and the shell's own stream takes one: a limit above 4 lets an account's App windows fill them. A revoked token ends the stream at the next heartbeat. |
| `GET /state` | **The state store:** the last value of every datapoint of the chosen set — the datapoints the App's cards draw and the service datapoints (`datapoints` below; `internal/devstate/keys.go` is the list) — `{entries: [{interface, address, datapoint, value, ts, lc, confirmed, confirmed_at?, source: event\|sweep\|restored, previous?, previous_for_s?}], total, unconfirmed, next?, event_id, sweeps: {<interface>: {at, channels, read, failed, error?, seconds}}, datapoints?}`. `ts` is when the last report arrived (the same value or not), `lc` when the value last became a different one — *since when* is `lc`; `previous`/`previous_for_s` the value before `lc` and how long it stood (absent until the store saw a change); all times RFC 3339. **`confirmed`**: `false` for an entry that came back from occulited's database file after a restart (`source: "restored"`) until an event or the sweep reports the datapoint — a client shows it and does not act on it; `true` otherwise, `confirmed_at` when that happened. **The sweep:** when an interface comes up (at occulited's start, right after the registration), its channels whose entries are not confirmed, and the channels never read, are read with `getParamset(VALUES)` one at a time, 200 ms apart (answered from the process's cache: no radio traffic); a datapoint the answer does not name (a key's `PRESS_SHORT`) stays unconfirmed until its next event. The service-message store's reads of the maintenance channels (`GET /api/system/v1/service-messages`: every 15 minutes, and every 60 s for a device with an active sticky message) are taken as well, where a kept datapoint's value differs from the entry or the entry is not confirmed — `source: "sweep"`, a `state` message on the stream — so a value whose change sent no event does not stay. **Filters** as the stream's: `interface=`, `address=` (a device matches its channels), `datapoint=` (alias `key=`), repeatable or comma-separated. **Pages:** `limit=` 1–5000 (default 1000), sorted by interface, address, datapoint; `next` is the cursor of the next page, passed as `after=`; `total` and `unconfirmed` count everything the filter matches; `datapoints` (the chosen set, `ERROR_*` for the prefix) comes with the first page only. **`event_id`** is the stream's position taken before the read: open `GET /events` with it as `Last-Event-ID` and nothing between the read and the stream is lost (an event in both is harmless). The store lives in occulited's database file (`GET /api/system/v1/datastore`, bucket `state`): in `ram` it starts empty at every restart and the sweep fills it; `ram-sync` writes it at the interval; `persistent` writes a value change at once and the timestamp-only refreshes at the interval — after a power cut `ts` may be up to one interval old, the value is right. Not in XML-RPC: a lite-only read. |
| `GET /history` | **The datapoint history:** one series — `interface=`, `address=` (the channel) and `datapoint=` (alias `key=`) are required — as compact rows **`[[ts, value], …]`**, oldest first, `ts` in **milliseconds since the epoch**. `from=`/`to=` (RFC 3339 or milliseconds) cut it; `max_points=` keeps every nth row counted from the newest (the newest is always in). No aggregation, no queries across series: a card asks for one and draws it. **404 `not-recorded`** when the datapoint is not in the list; `[]` when it is and nothing came yet. **What is kept:** a ring of the last **500 rows per series** (no maximum age: the answer's times say how old a row is), in occulited's database file (bucket `history`, the file's storage mode as `GET /api/system/v1/datastore` says; at most 1,000 series). **Sampled** datapoints (`ACTUAL_TEMPERATURE`, `TEMPERATURE`, `SET_POINT_TEMPERATURE`, `SET_TEMPERATURE`, `HUMIDITY`, `LEVEL`, `LEVEL_2`, `ILLUMINATION`, `CURRENT_ILLUMINATION`, `POWER`) write at most one row a minute and none while the value stays within its dead-band of the last row (0.1 for the temperatures, 1 for humidity, 0.01 — 1 % — for a level); **event-shaped** ones (`STATE`, `MOTION`, `PRESENCE_DETECTION_STATE`, `PRESS_SHORT`, `PRESS_LONG`, `SMOKE_DETECTOR_ALARM_STATUS`, `WINDOW_STATE`) write a row at every change — the time between two rows is how long a value stood. The list is what the App's cards draw; `PUT /api/system/v1/datastore/history` adds or removes names. Fed by every report the state store gets (events and its sweep). |
| `GET /events/ws` | The same stream over **WebSocket** (an HTTP upgrade through lighttpd; the credential at the handshake; the same query parameters). Text frames, one JSON object each: `{"id", "type", "data"}`. A ping every 15 s (the client's library pongs; no pong for 45 s closes it). Close codes: **1001** `stopping` (occulited stops or restarts - an update, a hot deploy: reconnect at once with `last_event_id=`), **4001** `overflow` (after the `resync`), **4003** `unauthorized` (the token went), **4004** `disabled` (lite-rpc switched off). Subprotocol `openccu-lite.rpc-events.v1` echoed when offered. Client frames are ignored (resume is by reconnecting with `last_event_id=`); a plain GET is 426. |
| `GET /streams` **`system:read`** | The open streams for the Remote access page: `{limits: {per_token, total}, streams: [{id, transport: sse\|websocket, subject: {kind: token\|session, name}, remote, filter, since, sent, dropped, last_resync?}]}`. |
| `DELETE /streams/{id}` **`system:write`** | Ends one (the page's ✕; 204, 404 when gone). The client reconnects on its own. |
| `GET /api/system/v1/rpc-trace` **`system:read`**, `PUT` **`system:write`** | the RPC trace's switch: `{mode: off\|until\|permanent, until?, on, sd_warning, available}`; `PUT {mode, minutes?}` (1–43200 for `until`). On, every call between a client or occulited and an interface process (`<enc> <who> → <interface> <method> <params>` and `← <interface> <who> <method> <result\|fault>`; a callback `xmlrpc <interface> → occulited <method> <params>`), every stream delivery (`→ <transport> <name>@<remote> event <interface> <address> <key> <value>`) and the streams' life (`stream … connect\|disconnect`) go to the journal as **`SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=rpc-trace`** at debug (`journalctl -t rpc-trace`; the Log page with `?tag=rpc-trace&severity=debug`). Lines are capped at 8 kB (`…[N bytes cut]`); a token is named, never shown. A timed trace ends on its own and its deadline survives a restart (`occulited.json` `rpc.trace`). `sd_warning` is true on an rpi3/rpi4 whose journal persists (the permanent choice wears the card). |

**Seeding values, and what it costs on air** (openccu-lite task 287, measured on a lab system with rfd and
hmipserver at debug level): **HmIP** answers `getParamset(…, VALUES)` and `getValue` from hmipserver's cache - no
frame, whatever the channel (a battery device without values answers `{}`); **BidCos** asks an always-listening
actuator for its status on **every** `getParamset(VALUES)` and `getValue` of a value datapoint (`CONFIG_STATUS_REQ`
→ `INFO_ACTUATOR_STATUS`, one frame pair, ~0.05 s of airtime), whatever the age of the last value; channel 0,
`MASTER` and the virtual channels are free, and a `-1 Failure` on a BidCos channel is that status request going
unanswered after rfd's retries (the device is away, or a battery device rfd cannot ask) - the most expensive case.
So a client seeds from `/state` and the event stream for what they carry, reads HmIP `VALUES` freely, reads BidCos
actuators' `VALUES` once at start and paced (rfd serialises them, about five a second) rather than as a poll, and
skips what `UNREACH` says is away. The integer `DUTY_CYCLE` (one percent is about 36 s of airtime an hour) does not
show a seed of a few hundred calls; the frames are there all the same.

The journal (area `rpc`): every stream's open and close with the transport, why it ended and how much it sent; refused
`init` and methods; over-limit refusals; the switch. The spec for integrators — `docs/rpc-remote.md`, which the `init` fault names — the reference clients and the fake
system are still to come.

## Off the HTTP API: the two discovery answers occulited gives on UDP

Not routes, but part of what occulited answers for and therefore part of this contract.

| Where | What |
| --- | --- |
| **UDP 1900, SSDP** (in place of `ssdpd`) | Joins `239.255.255.250:1900`, sends `NOTIFY … ssdp:alive` at start and every 30 min, `ssdp:byebye` on stop, and answers an `M-SEARCH` whose `MAN` is `"ssdp:discover"` and whose `ST` is `upnp:rootdevice`, `ssdp:all` or this system's uuid, after a random wait inside the request's `MX` (capped at 5 s). `LOCATION http://<address>/upnp/basic_dev.cgi`, `USN uuid:upnp-BasicDevice-1_0-<serial>::upnp:rootdevice`, `CACHE-CONTROL max-age=5000`. IPv4. The `LOCATION` and the group membership follow the address the system has now, and the answer to a searcher names the address of this system that shares the most bits with the searcher's. **The identity** (`/var/board_sgtin`, else `/var/board_serial`, else the host name) is written by the radio detection seconds after occulited starts (B-199): the first `ssdp:alive` **waits for it, up to 15 s**, is **repeated once a minute later** (the spec's "more than once"; a switch port that passes nothing for its first half minute after link-up loses the first one), and an identity that changes afterwards is taken back with an `ssdp:byebye` under the old `USN` and announced again under the new one, checked every 2 s (B-245: a system that had announced its host name and then described its serial never appeared in Windows' network view, which keeps devices by `USN`). |
| **UDP 43439, the eQ-3 discovery** (in place of `eq3configd`) | Answers `I` (identify) only: `<the request's version, sender id and counter> <type> 00 <serial> 00 '>' 'I' 01 <version> 00 00`, with the type **`eQ3-HmIP-CCU3-App-lite`**, the serial from `/var/board_sgtin` or `/var/board_serial`, and the version the lite image's (`LITE=` in `/VERSION`, else `VERSION=`). The request's type and serial are patterns compared character by character, a `*` or the end of either string ending the comparison with a match, so `eQ3-*`, `*` and an exact `eQ3-HmIP-CCU3-App` all find this system. **`n`, `c`, `C` and every other opcode get the NAK** (`… '>' <opcode> 00`): the network is configured on the Network page and nowhere else, and nothing here reads or writes `netconfig`. The answer goes back to the sender for a probe addressed to this system and to the broadcast address for one that was broadcast (the datagram's own destination, `IP_PKTINFO`); a probe with a `*` in the serial waits a random moment up to 1.3 s first. |

## Errors

`400 invalid` (validation), `401 unauthenticated`, `403 forbidden` (with `scope`: the scope the session or token lacks), `404 not_found`, `409`
(`pending`, `duplicate`), `422` (`install-failed`, `restore-failed`), `501 unsupported` (the
feature is not wired on this system), `502 ccu-unreachable`, `500 internal`.

**Body limits** (B-232): every JSON body has a limit — 64 KiB for a body of a few fields, 1 MiB for the larger ones
(an import, a certificate's PEM), a route's own where it says so (8 KiB, 4 MiB) — and a body over it is
**`413 too-large`** with `detail.limit` (a route that answers its errors with `400 bad-request` or its own code keeps
that for a malformed body). Uploads (backups, addons, firmware) stream to the disk and have their own limits. The
server closes a keep-alive connection that carries no request for 120 s; a request's header has to arrive within 10 s.
A running request — the streams, the log follow, lite-rpc's events, an upload — has no time limit.

## Addon CGIs

lighttpd proxies all of `/addons/` to occulited, behind its session gate, and serves nothing
there itself. occulited checks the session (any role, like the gate; browsers without one
are sent to `/login`) for every request under `/addons/`.

**Static files** (B-120): everything that is not a CGI is served by occulited as `occulite`, through
`os.Root` on the addon's own directory (the parent of its `www`, wherever the `www` link leads), so a
link out of that tree answers 404, as do dotfiles, anything that is neither a regular file nor a
directory, and a `.cgi`/`.ccc` path segment; only `GET` and `HEAD` are allowed. A directory answers
its `index.htm`, `index.html`, `index.xhtml` or `default.htm`.

**CGIs**: a `*.cgi` or `*.ccc` (or a directory whose index is `index.cgi`) is run: occulited builds
the CGI environment mod_cgi built (`REQUEST_METHOD`, `QUERY_STRING`,
`SCRIPT_NAME`, `PATH_INFO`, `REMOTE_ADDR` from the last element of `X-Forwarded-For` (lighttpd's; B-230), `HTTP_*`, `CONTENT_*`), runs
`/bin/tclsh <script>` (or the `.ccc` directly) through the privilege helper **as the addon's user
when the addon is confined**, root otherwise, feeds the body on stdin (64 MB cap, 5 min),
and answers with the script's headers (`Status:`, `Location:` → 302) and body. The `?sid=@…@`
convention and the tclrega shim's session check are untouched: the session mirror is 0711 with
0644 files named by the SHA-256 of the session id, so a CGI holding the sid can hash it
and open its file (the shim does) and nobody can list them.

## Embedding contract for addon pages

The shell shows an addon's settings page (its `Config-Url`, from the Addons page, at the shell path
`/addon-settings/<id>`), an addon's own web frontend (from the addon dropdown) and every `nav.d`
entry in an iframe of the same origin. `/addons/<id>` is kept as an in-app alias
that the router replaces with `/addon-settings/<id>`; typed or reloaded, `/addons/…` is the addons'
own files and CGIs (see *Addon CGIs*).

**Kept pages**: an addon's frontend and settings page stay loaded while other pages
show — hidden with `visibility: hidden` at their last size, never moved in the DOM (a moved iframe
reloads) — so a websocket and the page's state survive a switch. At most three are kept; opening
a fourth drops the least recently shown. The addon dropdown's ✕ drops both pages of an addon, a
logout drops all, and a page is dropped when its addon's version, running state, pid or on/off
switch changes. A `nav.d` entry is kept only when its drop-in sets `"keep_alive": true`. A kept page
keeps receiving the look messages below. What the page gets:

- **The session**: `?sid=@xxxxxxxxxx@` appended to the URL, the CCU convention. lighttpd's gate has
  already checked it; the page's own `tclrega` check sees the same id. **Not appended** for an addon
  whose manifest says it reads the gate's `X-Occulite-Session`
  (`session_header` on `GET /nav` and `GET /addons`): that page takes the session
  from the header. The frame's own requests under `/addons/` carry the **gate cookie** (task 259),
  never the API's session cookie, which is scoped to `/api`; a page that calls the API from the
  browser sends the id it was handed as `Authorization: Bearer`, or, riding on the browser's
  cookie, adds `X-Occulite-Request` to every state-changing call (see `/api/auth/v1`). No
  Content-Security-Policy applies under `/addons/`; the shell's is its own.
- **The look**: `theme=system|light|dark` and `lang=de|en` on the URL for the first paint, and a
  `postMessage` from the shell — on load and on every change — of
  `{type: "openccu-lite:theme", theme, lang}` with the shell's origin as target. A page that
  wants to follow the shell listens for it; one that does not is left alone.
  **Node-RED's own protocol is spoken too** (28.5): a framed editor (5.0.6 and later) posts
  `{type: "request-theme"}` and the shell answers `{type: "set-theme", payload: {theme: "auto"|"light"|"dark"}}`,
  again on every change; and the editor's language, a localStorage key of the shared origin
  (`editor-language`, `de` or `en-US`), is kept in step with the shell's. RedMatic needs nothing
  for either.
- **Escape hatch**: the shell detects a page that refuses embedding (`X-Frame-Options`,
  `frame-ancestors`) and offers "open in a new tab" instead. Since 28.5 the same two values are also cookies on the origin, `ol-theme` (`light`\|`dark`, with `system` resolved against `prefers-color-scheme`) and `ol-lang` (`de`\|`en`), set by the shell whenever they change, for addon pages rendered on the server that never see the frame URL or a message.

Nothing else crosses the frame: no shared JavaScript, no CSS, no globals. The shell's UI kit is
homematic-manager's, so a page built on it matches by construction.
